Charged scalar field equation

The (electrically charged) Reissner–Nordström–de Sitter solution to the Einstein–Maxwell equations are pairs
(\mathcal{M}_{M,Q,\Lambda},g_{M,Q,\Lambda},F_{Q})
, with
(\mathcal{M}_{M,Q,\Lambda},g_{M,Q,\Lambda})
defined in §
2.1
and

F=-\frac{Q}{r^{2}}dv\wedge dr.

The constant
Q\in\mathbb{R}
can be interpreted as the total electrical charge of the Reissner--Nordström spacetime.
11
11

11

More generally, one may consider electromagnetically charged Reissner–Nordström–de Sitter solutions
(\mathcal{M}_{M,e,\Lambda},g_{M,e,\Lambda},F_{Q,P})
, with
P\in\mathbb{R}
,
e=\sqrt{Q^{2}+P^{2}}
and
F_{Q,P}=-\frac{Q}{r^{2}}dt\wedge dr+P\sin\theta d\theta\wedge d\varphi
. The analysis of the present paper can be generalized to that setting, with the key difference being the replacement of spherical harmonics
Y_{\ell,m}
with so-called “monopole harmonics”
\widetilde{Y}_{j,m,P\mathfrak{q}}
, with eigenvalues
\lambda_{j}=j(j+1)-(\mathfrak{q}P)^{2}
,
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}+|\mathfrak{q}P|
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}
,
|m|\leq j
, provided that the scalar field charge
\mathfrak{q}
satisfies
\mathfrak{q}P\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}
(Dirac quantization).

We define the following 1-form on
\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{M,e}
:
\widetilde{A}=-\frac{Q}{r}dt
and observe that
F=d\widetilde{A}
. Note that the 1-form
\widetilde{A}
is not well-defined on
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. Define:

\widehat{A}:=-\frac{Q}{r}d\tau.

The 1-form
\widehat{A}
is well-defined everywhere on
\mathcal{M}_{M,Q,\Lambda}
and also on the extended manifold
\widehat{M}_{M,Q,\Lambda}
. We can relate
\widetilde{A}
and
\widehat{A}
as follows:

\widehat{A}=\widetilde{A}-\frac{Q}{r}(\Omega^{-2}-\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}})dr=\widetilde{A}-d\left(\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}\frac{Q}{r^{\prime}}(\Omega^{-2}-\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}})(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}\right),

so we also have that
d\widehat{A}=F
. In the remainder of the article, we will assume that
P=0
.

We will refer to a choice of 1-form
A
satisfying
F=dA
as an
electromagnetic gauge
. Given an electromagnetic gauge
A
, we define the following linear operator in terms of the Levi–Civita covariant derivative
\nabla
:

{}^{A}D:=\nabla-i\mathfrak{q}A\otimes(\cdot),

with
\mathfrak{q}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Given a vector field
Y
, we moreover write
{}^{A}D_{Y}:=\nabla_{Y}-i\mathfrak{q}A(Y)\mathbf{1}
.

Given a tensor field
T
, we moreover apply the notational convention:
(^{A}D_{\mu})(^{A}D_{\nu})T:=((^{A}D)^{2}T)_{\mu\nu}
.

The
inhomogeneous charged scalar field equation
with inhomogeneity
G_{A}
with respect to
(g_{M,Q,\Lambda},A)
is then defined as follows:

(g^{-1}_{Q,M,\Lambda})^{\mu\nu}(^{A}D_{\mu})(^{A}D_{\nu})\phi-\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\phi=G_{A}.

(2.16)

In the context of (
2.16
), we refer to
\mathfrak{q}
as the
scalar field charge parameter
. Note that by definition of
D
and
A
,
\mathfrak{q}
has units of inverse length and
Q
has units of length, so the product

q:=\mathfrak{q}\cdot Q

(2.17)

is dimensionless.

We introduce the parameter
\beta_{\ell}\in[0,\infty)\cup i(0,\infty)
, which will play a vital role in the bounded frequency analysis in Fourier space and appears also in the late-time asymptotics of
[
Gaj26
]
. Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then

\beta_{\ell}:=\sqrt{(2\ell+1)^{2}-4q^{2}}=\sqrt{1+4\ell(\ell+1)-4q^{2}}.

(2.18)

Suppose
A^{\prime}=A+df
for some
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{M,Q,\Lambda})
and
\phi^{\prime}=e^{i\mathfrak{q}f}\phi
. Then for any vector field
Y
, we have that:

{}^{A^{\prime}}D\phi^{\prime}=e^{i\mathfrak{q}f}(^{A}D\phi).

Hence
\phi
is a solution to (
2.16
) if and only if
\phi^{\prime}
satisfies:

(g^{-1}_{M,Q,\Lambda})^{\mu\nu}(^{A^{\prime}}D)_{\mu}(^{A^{\prime}}D)_{\nu}\phi^{\prime}-\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\phi^{\prime}=e^{i\mathfrak{q}f}G_{A}=:G_{A^{\prime}}.

(2.19)

We refer to
\phi^{\prime}
as a
gauge transformation
of
\phi
.

Let
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
. We introduce the shorthand notation:

D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}:=(^{A}\not{D}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{\gamma_{1}}(r\Omega(^{A}D_{X}))^{\gamma_{2}}(^{A}D_{T})^{\gamma_{3}},

(2.20)

with
{}^{A}\not{D}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-i\mathfrak{q}\not{A}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\otimes(\cdot)
and
\not{A}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=A^{\theta}d\theta+A^{\varphi}d\varphi
.

In the remainder of the paper, we will assume without loss of generality that
\not{A}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\equiv 0
, so that
\not{D}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
.

We will also denote:

\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}:=\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{\gamma_{1}}(r\Omega X)^{\gamma_{2}}T^{\gamma_{3}}.

(2.21)

In the remainder of the article, we will mainly work with the quantity

\psi:=r\cdot\phi

instead of
\phi
, as it appears more naturally in the relevant energies and equations.

The following proposition provides more explicit expressions for (
2.16
) with respect to
\widehat{A}
in terms
\psi
:

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
\phi
be a solution to (
2.16
) with
A=\widehat{A}
. Then
\psi
satisfies:

rG_{\widehat{A}}=X(\Omega^{2}X\psi)+r^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T^{2}\psi-2(1-\mathbbm{h})(T+iqr^{-1})X\psi\\
+\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)T\psi-\left[\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}r^{-1}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}-iqr^{-1}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-q^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}r^{-2}-iqr^{-2}(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\psi\\
=X(\Omega^{2}X\psi)+r^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}K_{c}^{2}\psi-2(1-\mathbbm{h})XK_{c}\psi+2iq(1-\mathbbm{h})\rho_{c}X\psi\\
+\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iq\rho_{c}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)K_{c}\psi-\left[\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}r^{-1}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}-iq\rho_{c}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-q^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\rho_{c}^{2}-iqr^{-2}(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\psi.

(2.22)

In particular, for
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r)=\Omega^{-2}
, we have that
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 1
,
\widehat{A}=\widetilde{A}
and:

r\Omega^{2}G_{\widetilde{A}}=Y_{*}^{2}\psi-K_{c}^{2}\psi+r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-2iq\rho_{c}K_{c}\psi+\left[q^{2}\rho_{c}^{2}-r^{-1}\Omega^{2}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}-\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]\psi.

(2.23)

With respect to the coordinate chart
(\tau,\rho_{c},\theta,\varphi)
we moreover obtain:

r^{2}(rG_{\widehat{A}})=\partial_{\rho_{c}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\partial_{\rho_{c}}\psi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-r^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}K_{c}^{2}\psi+2(1-\mathbbm{h})\partial_{\rho_{c}}K_{c}\psi+2iq(1-\mathbbm{h})\rho_{c}\partial_{\rho_{c}}\psi\\
+\left(r^{2}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iq\rho_{c}r^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)K_{c}\psi-\left[r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}r^{2}-iq\rho_{c}r^{2}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-q^{2}\rho_{c}^{2}r^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}-iq(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\psi

(2.24)

and with respect to the coordinate chart
(\tau,\rho_{+},\theta,\varphi)
:

r^{2}(rG_{\widehat{A}})=\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-r^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}K_{+}^{2}\psi-2(1-\mathbbm{h})\partial_{\rho_{+}}K_{+}\psi+2iq(1-\mathbbm{h})\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi\\
+\left(r^{2}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}+2iq\rho_{+}r^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)K_{*}\psi-\left[r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}r^{2}+iq\rho_{+}r^{2}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-q^{2}\rho_{+}^{2}r^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}-iq(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\psi.

(2.25)

Proof.

First, we expand:

(g^{-1})_{M,Q,\Lambda}^{\mu\nu}(^{A}D)_{\mu}(^{A}D)_{\nu}\phi=\square_{g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}\phi-2i\mathfrak{q}A^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\phi+\left[i\mathfrak{q}\nabla_{\mu}A^{\mu}-\mathfrak{q}^{2}A^{\mu}A_{\mu}\right]\phi\\
=\square_{g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}\phi-2i\mathfrak{q}A^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\phi+\left[-i\mathfrak{q}\frac{1}{\sqrt{-\det g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}}\partial_{\mu}(\sqrt{-\det g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}A^{\mu})-\mathfrak{q}^{2}A^{\mu}A_{\mu}\right]\phi.

Then we take
A=\widehat{A}
and we use that
\sqrt{-\det g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}=r^{2}\sin\theta
and that the dual vector field to
\widehat{A}
takes the following form with respect to
(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)
coordinates,

\widehat{A}^{\sharp}:=\frac{Q}{r}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\partial_{\tau}+\frac{Q}{r}(1-\mathbbm{h})\partial_{r}

to obtain:

(g^{-1}_{M,Q,\Lambda})^{\mu\nu}(^{A}D)_{\mu}(^{A})D_{\nu}\phi=\square_{g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}\phi-2i\mathfrak{q}Qr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\partial_{\tau}\phi-2i\mathfrak{q}Qr^{-1}(1-\mathbbm{h})\partial_{r}\phi\\
+\left[-i\mathfrak{q}Qr^{-2}\partial_{r}(r(1-\mathbbm{h}))+\mathfrak{q}^{2}Q^{2}r^{-2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right]\phi\\
=\square_{g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}\phi-2i\mathfrak{q}Qr^{-2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\partial_{\tau}\psi-2i\mathfrak{q}Qr^{-2}(1-\mathbbm{h})\partial_{r}\psi+\left[i\mathfrak{q}Qr^{-2}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}+\mathfrak{q}^{2}Q^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}r^{-3}+i\mathfrak{q}Qr^{-3}(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\psi.

We conclude that (
2.22
) holds by writing
q=\mathfrak{q}Q
and combining

r\square_{g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}\phi=\frac{r}{\sqrt{-\det g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\sqrt{-\det g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}(g^{-1}_{M,e})^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\beta}(r^{-1}\psi)\right)

with (
2.9
) to obtain:

r\square_{g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}\phi=X(\Omega^{2}X\psi)+r^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T^{2}\psi-2(1-\mathbbm{h})TX\psi+\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}T\psi-r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}\psi

and writing
T=K_{c}-iqr_{c}^{-1}
.

To obtain (
2.24
), we use that
\partial_{\rho_{c}}=-r^{2}X
. Equation (
2.25
) follows from (
2.22
) by using that
\partial_{\rho_{+}}=r^{2}X
and writing
T=K_{+}-iqr_{+}^{-1}
.
∎

2.9.
Local estimates for the charged scalar field equation

In this section, we state basic local energy estimates for solutions to (
2.16
).

Theorem 2.5

(Existence and uniqueness for the charged scalar field equation)
.

Let
A\in\Omega^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{M,Q,\Lambda})
. Consider the initial data pair:

(\upphi,T\upphi)\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})\times C^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})

and assume that
G_{A}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M}_{M,Q,\Lambda})
.

Then there exists a unique solution
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M}_{M,Q,\Lambda})
to (
2.16
) with respect to
A
and
G_{A}
, such that
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}})=(\upphi,T\upphi)
.

If we take
A=\widehat{A}
and assume that
G_{\widehat{A}}\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{M,Q,\Lambda})
, then
\psi=r\phi\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{M,Q,\Lambda})
.

Proof.

This follows from standard local existence and uniqueness for linear wave equations and relies on the existence of the energy estimates in Theorem
2.6
with
p=2
. In the case that
A=\widehat{A}
, we can additionally commute with
(r^{2}X)^{k}
to derive the desired regularity on the extended manifold
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{M,Q,\Lambda}
.
∎

Let
p\in\mathbb{R}
. We define the
energy densities

\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi]
as follows:

\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi]:=(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}\Omega^{2}|^{A}D_{X}\psi|^{2}+(\Omega^{-1}r)^{\min\{p,0\}}(\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}|^{A}D_{T}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|^{A}\not{D}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}).

Given
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
consider the operators
\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}
and
D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}
as defined in (
2.20
). Then we define the following higher-order energy densities:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]:=

\displaystyle\>(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}\Omega^{2}|^{A}D_{X}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+(\Omega^{-1}r)^{\min\{p,0\}}\left(\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}|^{A}D_{T}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|^{A}\not{D}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi]:=

\displaystyle\>(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}\Omega^{2}|^{A}D_{X}D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+(\Omega^{-1}r)^{\min\{p,0\}}\left(\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}|^{A}D_{T}D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|^{A}\not{D}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}\right).

We refer to integrals with respect to the volume form
d\sigma dr

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]\,d\sigma dr

as (higher-order, weighted)
energies
. Note that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\sim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]r^{-2}\,d\mu_{\Sigma_{\tau}},

with
d\mu_{\Sigma_{\tau}}
a natural choice of induced volume form on
\Sigma_{\tau}
.

Theorem 2.6

(Local energy estimates for the charged scalar field equation)
.

Let
\phi
be a solution to (
2.16
) with
A=\widehat{A}
. Let
0\leq p\leq 2
. For all
0\leq\tau_{A}\leq\tau_{B}<\infty
and
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists a constant
C_{\tau_{b}-\tau_{a}}=C_{\tau_{b}-\tau_{a}}(r_{+},q,\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}},p,N,\tau_{b}-\tau_{a})>0
such that:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{b}}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\leq C_{\tau_{b}-\tau_{A}}\left[\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{a}}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{a}}^{\tau_{b}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2}|D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}(r^{3}G_{A})|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\right].

(2.26)

Proof.

The proof is a standard local-in-time energy estimate. Without loss of generality, we derive (
2.26
) with
A=\widehat{A}
by multiplying both sides of (
2.22
) with
T\overline{\psi}
and
(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}X\overline{\psi}
, integrating by parts, applying a Grönwall inequality to estimate the spacetime integrals in terms on the initial energy flux. After multiplying
rG_{A}
with
r^{2}
, we can commute straightforwardly with
\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}
.
∎

Remark 2.1
.

By combining Theorem
2.5
and Theorem
2.6
with a standard density argument, we have global uniqueness and existence of solutions arising from more general initial data that satisfy only
\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]\,d\sigma dr<\infty
for some
p\in[0,2]
.

2.10.
Cut-off and bump functions

Let
R_{i}\in(r_{+},r_{c})
and
r_{i}\in(1,\infty)
, where
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
r_{3}<r_{2}<r_{1}
,
R_{1}>R_{2}>R_{3}
. Assume moreover that
2\rho_{c}(R_{i})<\rho_{c}(r_{+})
and
2\rho_{+}(r_{i})<\rho_{+}(r_{c})
. We define the corresponding cut-off functions
\chi_{R_{i}},\chi_{r_{i}}:[1,r_{c})\to[0,1]
as as smooth functions satisfying:

\chi_{R_{i}}(r)=\begin{cases}1\quad\textnormal{for $\rho_{c}(r)\leq\rho_{c}(R_{i})$},\\
0\quad\textnormal{for $\rho_{c}(r)\geq 2\rho_{c}(R_{i})$},\end{cases}

with
0\leq\frac{d\chi_{R_{i}}}{dr}=-r^{-2}\frac{d\chi_{R_{i}}}{d\rho_{c}}\leq CR_{i}^{-2}(\rho_{c}(R_{i}))^{-1}
and
\left|\frac{d^{2}\chi_{R_{i}}}{dr^{2}}\right|\leq CR_{i}^{-4}(\rho_{c}(R_{i}))^{-2}
for for some constant
C>0
, and

\chi_{r_{i}}(r)=\begin{cases}1\quad\textnormal{for $\rho_{+}(r)\leq\rho_{+}(r_{i})$},\\
0\quad\textnormal{for $\rho_{+}(r)\geq 2\rho_{+}(r_{i})$},\end{cases}

with
0\leq-\frac{d\chi_{r_{i}}}{dr}=-r^{-2}\frac{d\chi_{r_{i}}}{d\rho_{+}}\leq Cr_{i}^{-2}(\rho_{+}(r_{i}))^{-1}
and
\left|\frac{d^{2}\chi_{r_{i}}}{dr^{2}}\right|\leq Cr_{i}^{-4}(\rho_{+}(r_{i}))^{-2}
for for some constant
C>0
.

We will also use that
\supp\left(\frac{d\chi_{R_{i}}}{dr}\right)\subset\left(\frac{1}{2-r_{c}^{-1}R_{i}}R_{i},R_{i}\right)
and
\supp\left(\frac{d\chi_{r_{i}}}{dr}\right)\subset\left(r_{i},\frac{1}{2-r_{+}^{-1}r_{i}}r_{i}\right)
.

Let
\eta>0
be suitably small. We define
\chi_{K},\chi_{T}:[r_{+},r_{c})\to\mathbb{R}
as smooth cut-off functions satisfying the following properties:
\chi_{K}(r)=1
for
r\leq r_{\sharp}-\eta
,
\chi_{K}(r)=0
for
r\geq r_{\sharp}+\eta
and
\frac{d\chi_{K}}{dr}\leq 0
,
|\frac{d\chi_{K}}{dr}|\leq C\eta^{-1}
for some numerical constant
C>0
. Similarly,
\chi_{T}(r)=1
for
r\geq r_{\sharp}+\eta
,
\chi_{T}(r)=0
for
r\leq r_{\sharp}-\eta
,
\frac{d\chi_{T}}{dr}\geq 0
and
|\frac{d\chi_{T}}{dr}|\leq C\eta^{-1}
.

Finally, for
\eta>0
suitably small, we define
\upzeta:[r_{+},r_{c})\to[0,1]
to be a smooth function satisfying
\upzeta(r)=1
for
r\notin(r_{\sharp}-2\eta,r_{\sharp}+2\eta)
and
\upzeta(r)=0
for
r\in(r_{\sharp}-\eta,r_{\sharp}+\eta)
.

3.
Precise statement of the main theorem

In this section, we give a precise version of Theorem
1.1
.

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
\phi
be a solution to (
2.16
) and let
\psi=r\phi
. Assume that
\kappa_{+}\geq\kappa_{c}\geq 0
,
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{1}
and
q_{0}\leq|q|\leq q_{1}
with
q_{0}=0
when
\kappa_{c}=0
. Let
\epsilon,\delta,\eta,q_{0},\kappa_{1}>0
,
N,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
r_{+}<r_{H}<r_{I}<r_{c}
. Consider
1<p<\min\{1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},2\}+\epsilon
. Then, for
\kappa_{+}\leq\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{1}
suitably small, or
q_{1}
suitably small, there exist a constant
C=C(\epsilon,\delta,\eta,q_{1},\kappa_{1},p,r_{H},r_{I},\mathbbm{h},N)>0
, such that:

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2\epsilon}[D_{T}^{k}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\,d\sigma dr+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=k}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}D_{Y_{*}}^{k_{2}+1}D_{T}^{k_{3}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+|D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}D_{Y_{*}}^{k_{2}}D_{T}^{k_{3}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\\
+\upzeta(|D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}D_{Y_{*}}^{k_{2}}D_{T}^{k_{3}+1}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+|D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}+1}D_{Y_{*}}^{k_{2}}D_{T}^{k_{3}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{+}\rho_{c})^{2\epsilon}(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}\Omega^{2}|D_{X}D_{T}^{k}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\\
+\rho_{+}\rho_{c}\Omega^{-2}\left[(\Omega^{-1}r)^{-\delta}|D_{T}^{k+1}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+(\rho_{+}\rho_{c})^{2\epsilon}(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p-2}(|{D}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}D_{T}^{k}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+|D_{T}^{k}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2})\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{T}^{k}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1-2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|r^{3}D_{T}^{k}G_{A}|^{2}+\upzeta|D_{T}^{k+1}G_{A}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\,d\tau.

(3.1)

In particular, (
3.1
) holds for all
q
on extremal Reissner–Nordström. Furthermore, under the assumption of quantitative mode stability in the form of Condition
1
in §
7.4
, (
3.1
) holds also when
\kappa_{+}>\kappa_{1}
and
q_{1}
is not assumed to be small.

If
|q|<\left(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\ell\right)
, or equivalently,
\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}>\sqrt{3}\left(\ell+\frac{1}{2}\right)
, then (
3.1
) holds also with
\epsilon=0
and
1<p<1+\sqrt{1-\frac{16q^{2}}{(2\ell+1)^{2}}}
.

Remark 3.1
.

The assumption that
|q|\geq q_{0}>0
when
\kappa_{c}>0
is not necessary and can be easily removed. However, since we restrict our estimates in the case
|q|\leq q_{0}
(with
q_{0}>0
small) to the
\kappa_{c}=0
setting (see §
4
), for the sake of simplicity, we exclude the case
|q|<q_{0}
and
\kappa_{c}>0
from the statement of the theorem.

We will prove Theorem
3.1
in §
9.4
.

4.
Integrated estimates for small
|q|

In this section, we derive integrated energy estimates that are only valid for very small
|q|
, using only a decomposition into spherical harmonic modes. We easily obtain an integrated energy estimate for solutions to (
2.16
) that are supported on bounded angular frequencies as a corollary from (
r
-weighted) integrated energy estimates valid in the
q=0
case. This is the content of Theorem
4.1
. In Corollary
4.2
, we control the higher angular frequencies, using the purely physical-space based integrated energy estimates
modulo zeroth order terms
from Appendix
A
.

In this section, we will restrict to
\kappa_{c}=0
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\kappa_{c}=0
,
\kappa_{+}\geq 0
. Let
r^{-1}\psi
be a solution to (
2.16
) arising from the initial value problem in Theorem
2.5
with
q=0
. Let
0\leq p\leq 2
,
\delta>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Then there exists a constant
C=C(\tilde{\mathbbm{h}},r_{H},r_{I},\delta,N)>0
such that:

\sup_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}}\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}Y_{*}^{k_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}Y_{*}^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}\\
+(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}+1}Y_{*}^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}+1}T^{k_{2}}Y_{*}^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma dr\Bigg]d\tau\\
+\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}p(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]+(2-p)(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{k}\psi|^{2}+(r-r_{+})^{-1+\delta}|T^{k+1}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}p\mathcal{E}_{p-1}[T^{k}\psi]+(2-p)r^{-1}(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{k}\psi|^{2}+r^{-1+\delta}|T^{k+1}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\rho_{+}r^{-1}(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}r^{-2}|T^{k}(r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{T^{k+1}\psi}\cdot rG_{\widehat{A}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|.

(4.1)

Furthermore, for
L_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\psi=\psi_{\leq L_{0}}
, we can remove the factor
(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}
and the term
\overline{T^{k+1}\psi}\cdot rG_{\widehat{A}}
above at the expense of making the constant
C
depend on
L_{0}
.

Proof.

Consider first the case
N=0
. The control of the integral in
\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}
follows from a
Morawetz estimate
or integrated local energy decay estimate. In the
\kappa_{+}>0
case, this follows as a special case from
[
Civ12
]
[Theorem 3.2.1]. The
\kappa_{+}=0
case follows from
[
Are11
]
[Theorem 1]. Strictly speaking, the proof of
[
Are11
]
[Theorem 1] does not consider simultaneously the extremal and sub-extremal cases. A purely physical-space-based proof, which is moreover uniform in
\kappa_{+}
, follows from
[
Gaj23b
]
[§6] with
D(r)=1-\frac{2M}{r}+Q^{2}r^{-2}
. Indeed, the desired Morawetz estimates for
\ell=0
follow from
[
Gaj23b
]
[Proposition 6.1] and for
\psi_{\ell}
with
\ell\geq 1
, we simply combine
[
Gaj23b
]
[Proposition 6.1] with
[
Gaj23b
]
[Proposition 6.2], the latter which remains unchanged when
D(r)=1-\frac{2M}{r}+Q^{2}r^{-2}
. In order to obtain uniform-in-
\kappa_{+}
estimates for higher spherical harmonic modes
\psi_{\geq L_{0}}
, with
L_{0}
arbitrarily large, we can directly apply Corollary
A.2
(or alternatively, modify
[
Gaj23b
]
[Proposition 6.5]).

Control over the integrals in
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
and
\{r\geq r_{I}\}
follows from
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates and
(r-r_{+})^{-p}
-weighted energy estimates; see for example
[
AAG20
]
[Proposition 6.5] for the
|Q|=M
case or Proposition
9.8
below for estimates that are uniform in
\kappa_{+}
.

To conclude the
N\geq 1
cases, we commute with the Killing vector field
T
and apply standard elliptic estimates in
\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}
.
∎

Remark 4.1
.

An analogue of Theorem
4.1
can also be shown to hold when
\kappa_{c}>0
by applying the results in
[
Veg24
]
.

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
\kappa_{c}=0
,
\kappa_{+}\geq 0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
r^{-1}\psi
be a solution to (
2.16
) arising from the initial value problem in Theorem
2.5
with 1-form
A=\widehat{A}
and denote with
r^{-1}\widetilde{\psi}
the solution corresponding to
A=\widetilde{A}
.

Then for
1\leq p<2
and
|q|
suitably small (depending on
p
) there exists a constant
C=C(\tilde{\mathbbm{h}},r_{H},r_{I},p,q,N)>0
, such that:

\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]}\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma drd\sigma+\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|T^{k+1}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|K_{+}T^{k}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\notin(r_{H},r_{I})\}}\mathcal{E}_{p-1}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\in(r_{H},r_{I})\}}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}+1}T^{k_{2}}Y_{*}^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}+1}Y_{*}^{k_{3}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2})\\
+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}Y_{*}^{k_{3}+1}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}Y_{*}^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma drd\sigma+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\rho_{+}r^{-1}(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}r^{-2}|T^{k}(r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{T^{k+1}\psi}rT^{k}G_{\widehat{A}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|.

(4.2)

Proof.

We first consider the case of bounded angular frequencies:
\psi=\psi_{\leq L_{0}}
. We rearrange the terms in (
2.22
), using (
2.19
) to obtain:

\square_{g_{M,Q,\Lambda}}(e^{iq\chi_{r_{1}}\tau}\phi)=e^{iq\chi_{r_{1}}\tau}G_{\hat{A}}+F_{q},

with

F_{q}=-2iqr^{-1}\left[(1-\mathbbm{h})(\chi_{r_{1}}r_{+}^{-1}-r^{-1})\partial_{r}(e^{iq\chi_{r_{1}}\tau}\psi)+(\chi_{r_{1}}r_{+}^{-1}-r^{-1})\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T(e^{iq\chi_{r_{1}}\tau}\psi)\right]\\
+r^{-1}\left[-iqr^{-2}\left(1-\mathbbm{h}-(\chi_{r_{1}}r_{+}^{-1}-r^{-1})r^{2}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}\right)-q^{2}(\chi_{r_{1}}r_{+}^{-1}-r^{-1})^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right]e^{iq\chi_{r_{1}}\tau}\psi\\
+q\frac{d\chi_{r_{1}}}{dr}O(1)(|X\psi|+|T\psi|)+q\frac{d^{2}\chi_{r_{1}}}{dr^{2}}O(1)|\psi|.

Using that
\supp(\frac{d\chi_{r_{1}}}{dr})\subset(r_{1},\frac{1}{2-r_{+}^{-1}r_{1}}r_{1})
for
r_{1}>r_{+}
, we can estimate

\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1})^{1-p}r^{p+1}|rF_{q}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\leq Cq^{2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\rho_{+}\mathcal{E}_{p}[e^{iq\chi_{r_{1}}\tau}\psi]+\rho_{+}^{1-p}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+Cq^{2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}\mathcal{E}_{p-1}[e^{iq\chi_{r_{1}}\tau}\psi]+r^{p-3}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+Cq^{2}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|Y_{*}\psi|^{2}+|\psi|^{2}+|T\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\right]d\tau.

When
p<2
, we can apply a standard Hardy inequality to estimate:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\rho_{+}^{1-p}\chi_{r_{1}}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{p-3}\chi_{r_{1}}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\leq C(2-p)^{-2}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\rho_{+}^{3-p}\chi_{r_{1}}|X\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{p-1}\chi_{r_{1}}|X\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\right]\\
+C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{1}\leq r\leq R_{1}\}}\psi^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

For
\phi
supported on spherical harmonics with
\ell\leq L_{0}
, we can therefore apply (
4.1
) with
0<p<2
(without the degenerate factor
(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}
!) and
G_{\widehat{A}}
replaced by
G_{\hat{A}}+F_{q}
and absorb the terms with a factor
q^{2}
into the LHS of (
4.1
) for
|q|
suitably small, depending on
p
.

Now consider
\psi=\psi_{\geq L_{0}}
then we can instead apply Corollary
A.2
.
∎

5.
Integrated energy estimates in frequency space: set-up

In order to obtain integrated energy estimates
without
a smallness assumption on
|q|
, we will need to apply a Fourier transform in the time coordinate
t
and perform a frequency-space-based analysis.

We provide below an outline of the arguments in frequency space.

•

In the present section, we will introduce the a priori assumption of
future integrability
to make sense of the Fourier transform and derive a Schrödinger-type radial ODE (
5.13
). The analysis in §§
5
–
8
concerns only (
5.13
) and can therefore be read independently from the rest of the paper. Here, we will moreover derive the main relevant properties of the potentials appearing in the radial ODE.

•

In §
6
, we consider large time
or
angular frequencies and derive
L^{2}
-estimates for solutions to (
5.13
) by employing “microlocal” energy currents or vector field multiplier, i.e. by a systematic integration by parts adapted to the large frequency behaviour of the potential in (
5.13
).

•

In §
7
, we restrict to bounded time
and
angular frequencies. We derive
L^{2}
-estimates for solutions to (
5.13
) by utilizing Green’s formula (
7.50
) and deriving appropriate
L^{\infty}
-estimates for homogeneous solutions to (
5.13
), as well as uniform estimates for the corresponding Wronskian.

•

In §
8
, we combine the estimates in the different frequency ranges.

•

We then derive energy estimates in physical space in §
9
from the estimates in §
8
by applying Plancherel’s theorem.

5.1.
Sufficient integrability, future integrability and the Fourier transform

In this section, we will introduce the notion of future integrability of solutions
\psi_{\ell m}:[0,\infty)_{\tau}\times[r_{+},\infty)_{r}\to\mathbb{C}
to (
2.16
) in the gauge
A=\widehat{A}=-Qr^{-1}d\tau
with fixed angular frequency
\ell
and azimuthal number
m
.

Let
\xi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth cut-off function such that
\xi(\tau)=1
when
\tau\geq 1
and
\xi(\tau)=0
when
\tau\leq 0
. Let

\uppsi_{\ell m}:\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[r_{+},\infty)_{r}\to\mathbb{C}

be the trivial extension of
\xi\cdot\psi_{\ell m}(\tau(t,r),r)
from
\{\tau\geq 0\}\subset\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[r_{+},\infty)_{r}
to
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[r_{+},\infty)_{r}
.
Then we can apply (
2.22
) to obtain:

(g^{-1}_{M,Q,\Lambda})^{\mu\nu}(^{\widehat{A}}D)_{\mu}(^{\widehat{A}}D)_{\nu}(r^{-1}\uppsi_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m})=\xi(G_{\widehat{A}})_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m}+(F_{\widehat{A},\xi})_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m},

(5.1)

with

r\Omega^{2}(F_{\widehat{A},\xi})_{\ell m}=-2\dot{\xi}\Omega^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T\psi_{\ell m}-\ddot{\xi}\Omega^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\psi_{\ell m}+2\dot{\xi}(1-\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\Omega^{2})\Omega^{2}X\psi_{\ell m}+\Omega^{2}\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)\dot{\xi}\psi_{\ell m},

(5.2)

where
\dot{\xi}:=\frac{d\xi}{d\tau},\ddot{\xi}:=\frac{d^{2}\xi}{d\tau^{2}}
and where we also identify
\xi(G_{\widehat{A}})_{\ell m}
with its trivial extension to
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[r_{+},\infty)_{r}
.

In what follows, we will frequently omit the subscript
\ell m
in the notation.

With respect to the gauge
\widetilde{A}=-Qr^{-1}dt=\widehat{A}+Qr^{-1}dr_{*}-Qr^{-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}dr
, we then obtain:

(g^{-1}_{M,Q,\Lambda})^{\mu\nu}(^{\widetilde{A}}D)_{\mu}(^{\widetilde{A}}D)_{\nu}(r^{-1}\widetilde{\uppsi})=\xi G_{\widetilde{A}}+F_{\widetilde{A},\xi},

(5.3)

with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\uppsi}:=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{+}^{-1}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\uppsi,

(5.4)

\displaystyle G_{\widetilde{A}}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{+}^{-1}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}G_{\widehat{A}},

(5.5)

\displaystyle F_{\widetilde{A},\xi}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{+}^{-1}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}F_{\widehat{A},\xi}.

(5.6)

The following alternative expressions will also be useful

\displaystyle\widetilde{\uppsi}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{c}^{-1}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{2}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\uppsi,

(5.7)

\displaystyle G_{\widetilde{A}}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{c}^{-1}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{2}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}G_{\widehat{A}},

(5.8)

\displaystyle F_{\widetilde{A},\xi}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{c}^{-1}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{2}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}F_{\widehat{A},\xi}.

(5.9)

Definition 5.1
.

A function
f:\mathbb{R}_{t}\times[M,\infty)_{r}\to\mathbb{C}
is
sufficiently integrable
if: for any
r\in(r_{+},r_{c})
and any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|Y_{*}^{k}f|^{2}(t,r)\,dt<\infty,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{t}}|D_{\underline{L}}f|^{2}(t,r(r_{*}))\,dtdr_{*}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{t}}|D_{L}f|^{2}(t,r_{*}(r))\,dtdr_{*}<\infty.

(5.11)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|Y_{*}^{k}\left((g^{-1}_{M,Q,\Lambda})^{\mu\nu}(^{\widehat{A}}D)_{\mu}(^{\widehat{A}}D)_{\nu}(r^{-1}f)\right)|^{2}(t,r)\,dt<\infty.

(5.12)

Definition 5.2
.

A solution
\psi
to (
2.16
) with
A=\widehat{A}
is called
future integrable
if for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
with
|m|\leq\ell
,
\uppsi_{\ell m}
is sufficiently integrable.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{F}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{t})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\omega})
denote the Fourier transform. Let

\widehat{\omega}:=\omega-qr_{c}^{-1}.

Assume that
\uppsi_{\ell m}
is sufficiently integrable. Then for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
r_{*}\in\mathbb{R}
,

u_{\ell m}^{(k)}(\cdot,r_{*}):=(\mathfrak{F}(Y_{*}^{k}\widetilde{\uppsi}))_{\ell m}(\cdot,r(r_{*}))\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\omega})
is well-defined and
u_{\ell m}(\cdot,r_{*})
satisfies the following equation in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\omega})
:

u_{\ell m}^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,r_{*})+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell})u_{\ell m}(\cdot,r_{*})=H_{\ell m}(\cdot,r_{*}),

(5.13)

with

H_{\ell m}(r_{*})=r\Omega^{2}(\mathfrak{F}(\widehat{G}_{\widetilde{A}}(\cdot,r(r_{*}))))_{\ell m}+r\Omega^{2}(\mathfrak{F}(F_{\widetilde{A},\xi}(\cdot,r(r_{*}))))_{\ell m}

and
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}:[r_{+},r_{c})\to\mathbb{R}
is defined as follows:

V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r):=\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}-{q}^{2}\rho_{c}^{2}(r)+2{q}\widehat{\omega}\rho_{c}(r)+\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right].

Proof.

We apply (
2.23
) to obtain:

r(g^{-1}_{M,Q,\Lambda})^{\mu\nu}(^{\widetilde{A}}D)_{\mu}(^{\widetilde{A}}D)_{\nu}(r^{-1}\widetilde{\uppsi}_{\ell m})=-K_{c}^{2}\widetilde{\uppsi}_{\ell m}+Y_{*}^{2}\widetilde{\uppsi}_{\ell m}+r^{-2}(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\uppsi})_{\ell m}+q^{2}\rho_{c}^{2}\widetilde{\uppsi}\\
-\left[\frac{d}{dr}(\Omega^{2})r^{-1}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]\Omega^{2}\widetilde{\uppsi}_{\ell m}-2iqr^{-1}K_{c}\widetilde{\uppsi}_{\ell m}.

To obtain (
5.13
), we first replace
K_{c}
with
-i\widehat{\omega}\mathbf{1}
and using that (
5.10
) in the definition of sufficient integrability allows us to take a Fourier transform in
t
.
∎

Let:

\widetilde{\omega}:=\omega-qr_{+}^{-1}.

It will be convenient to define the following shifted potential
\widetilde{V}_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}:[r_{+},r_{c})_{r}\to\infty
:

\widetilde{V}_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}(r)-\widetilde{\omega}^{2}:=V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r)-\widehat{\omega}^{2}.

We can then express:

\widetilde{V}_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}(r)=\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}-{q}^{2}\rho_{+}^{2}(r)-2{q}\widetilde{\omega}\rho_{+}(r)+\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right].

Given a solution
u_{\ell m}
to (
5.13
), we define the auxiliary functions:

\displaystyle v_{\ell m}:=

\displaystyle\>e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u_{\ell m},

(5.14)

\displaystyle w_{\ell m}:=

\displaystyle\>e^{-i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u_{\ell m}.

(5.15)

The functions
v_{\ell m}
and
w_{\ell m}
may be thought of as Fourier transforms of gauge transformations of
\uppsi
with respect to the time coordinates
\tau=t+r_{*}
and
\tau=t-r_{*}
, respectively, where
v_{\ell m}
corresponds to the gauge choice
A=-\frac{Q}{r}dv
and
w_{\ell m}
corresponds to the gauge choice
A=-\frac{Q}{r}du
, cf. (
5.4
) with
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\equiv 0
and (
5.7
) with
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 0
.

Observe that by Lemma
2.3
, the following equality holds when
\kappa_{c}=\kappa_{+}=0
:

\widetilde{V}_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}(r(r_{*}))=V_{-\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r(-r_{*})).

(5.16)

In the lemma below, we establish an
L^{2}
-version of the appropriate boundary conditions satisfied by solutions to (
5.13
).

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume that
\uppsi_{\ell m}
is sufficiently integrable. Then there exist sequences
\{r_{i}\}
and
\{\tilde{r}_{i}\}
with
r_{i}\to r_{c}
and
\tilde{r}_{i}\to r_{+}
as
i\to\infty
, such that:

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}||w^{\prime}_{\ell m}(\cdot,r_{*}(r_{i}))||_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\omega})}\to\>

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}||v^{\prime}_{\ell m}(\cdot,r_{*}(\tilde{r}_{i}))||_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\omega})}\to\>

\displaystyle 0

as
i\to\infty
. In particular,
v^{\prime}_{\ell m}(r_{*})\to 0
as
r_{*}\to-\infty
and
w^{\prime}_{\ell m}(r_{*})\to 0
as
r_{*}\to\infty
for almost every
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

We will suppress the subscript
\ell m
in the notation below. By the assumption (
5.11
), together with the Plancherel identity, we have that for
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}=0

D_{\underline{L}}\uppsi=\underline{L}\uppsi=(T-Y_{*})\uppsi
, there exists a constant
C(\uppsi)>0
such that:

C(\uppsi)>\frac{1}{4}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{t}}|(T-Y_{*})\uppsi|^{2}\,dtdr_{*}=\frac{1}{4}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{t}}|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\uppsi}+iq(r_{+}^{-1}-\rho_{+}(r_{*}))\widetilde{\uppsi}-Y_{*}\uppsi|^{2}\,dtdr_{*}\\
=\frac{1}{4}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}|v^{\prime}|^{2}\,d\omega dr_{*}.

Consider the sequence
\{-2^{i}\}
. By the mean-value theorem, there exists a sequence
\{(r_{*})_{i}\}
with
-2^{i+1}\leq(r_{*})_{i}\leq-2^{i}
, such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}|v^{\prime}|^{2}(\omega,(r_{*})_{i})\,d\omega\leq C(\uppsi)2^{-i-1}\to 0

as
i\to\infty
. Since
v^{\prime}((r_{*})_{i},\cdot)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\omega})
converges to 0 , it must follow that
v^{\prime}((r_{*})_{i},\omega)\to 0
pointwise for almost all
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
.

The convergence properties of
w_{\ell m}^{\prime}
follow analogously by considering
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 0
so that
D_{L}\uppsi=L\uppsi
.
∎

Since we will derive
L^{2}_{\omega}
-estimates for (
5.13
), we can consider, without loss of generality solutions to (
5.13
) that are valid outside of a zero measure subset of
\mathbb{R}_{\omega}
. The proposition below demonstrates that by omitting a zero measure subset of frequencies, we can in fact restrict to smooth solutions to (
5.13
).

Proposition 5.3
.

Assume that
\uppsi_{\ell m}
is sufficiently integrable, such that
\uppsi_{\ell m}
is a solution to (
5.1
). Then, for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
with
|m|\leq\ell
,
H_{m\ell}(\omega,\cdot)
is smooth for almost all
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
and the corresponding solution
u_{\ell m}(\omega,\cdot)
to (
5.13
) is smooth and satisfies the boundary conditions:

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{*}\to\infty}w^{\prime}_{\ell m}(\omega,r_{*})\to\>

\displaystyle 0,

(5.17)

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{*}\to-\infty}v^{\prime}_{\ell m}(\omega,r_{*})\to\>

\displaystyle 0

(5.18)

Proof.

By (
5.12
), we have that
u_{\ell m}^{(k)}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\omega})
are well-defined for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and there exists constants
D_{k}(\uppsi)>0
such that
||u_{\ell m}||_{H^{k}((r_{*}-\epsilon,r_{*}+\epsilon))L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{\omega})}\leq D_{k}(\uppsi)
.

By Fubini’s theorem and a Sobolev embedding, there therefore exist zero-Lesbesgue-measure subsets
U_{k}\subset\mathbb{R}_{\omega}
, such that
u_{\ell m}(\omega,\cdot)\in C^{k}((r_{*}-\epsilon,r_{*}+\epsilon))
for
\omega\in U_{k}^{c}
. Note that
U:=\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}U_{k}
also has zero measure and
u_{\ell m}(\omega,\cdot)\in C^{\infty}((r_{*}-\epsilon,r_{*}+\epsilon))
for all
\omega\in U^{c}
. The same argument also applies to
H_{\ell m}
to conclude that
H_{\ell m}(\omega,\cdot)\in C^{\infty}((r_{*}-\epsilon,r_{*}+\epsilon))
. Since
r_{*}\in\mathbb{R}
was arbitrary and
\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}
can be covered by a countable number of sets of the form
(r_{*}-\epsilon,r_{*}+\epsilon)
, we conclude smoothness of
u_{\ell m}(\omega,\cdot)
and
H_{\ell m}(\omega,\cdot)
for almost every
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
.

Finally, (
5.17
) and (
5.18
) follow (after removing a further zero-measure subset) from Lemma
5.2
.
∎

5.2.
Properties of the potential
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}

The analysis of solutions to (
5.13
) depends crucially on the precise form of the potential
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}
.
For the sake of notational convenience, we will set
r_{+}=1
the remainder of §
5
!

In the case
\kappa_{c}=\kappa_{+}=0
, the potentials
{V}_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r)
and
\widetilde{V}_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}
are symmetric under the transformations
r_{*}\mapsto-r_{*}
and
\widehat{\omega}\mapsto-\widetilde{\omega}
by (
5.16
). More generally,
\widetilde{V}_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}
has the same qualitative behaviour when we consider the transformation
(r_{*},\widehat{\omega},\kappa_{c},\kappa_{+})\mapsto(-r_{*},-\widetilde{\omega},\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c})
.

We will denote:

V^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}(r):=V_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}(r)+{q}^{2}(r^{-1}-r_{c}^{-1})^{2}-\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]=\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}+2{q}\widehat{\omega}(r^{-1}-r_{c}^{-1}).

The
V^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}
part of the potential
V_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}
is relevant when studying the large
|\omega|
or large
\ell
behaviour. In the lemma below, we determine the shape of
V^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

In the interval
[1,r_{c})
function
V^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}
has at most two critical points.

(1)

Suppose that
q\widehat{\omega}\neq 0
. In the case of exactly two critical points, there is a local minimum at
r=r_{\rm min}
and a local maximum at
r=r_{\rm max}
, with
r_{\rm min}<r_{\rm max}
if
q\widehat{\omega}>0
and
r_{\rm min}>r_{\rm max}
if
q\widehat{\omega}<0
.

(2)

Suppose that
q\widehat{\omega}\neq 0
. In the case of exactly one critical point, the critical point must be a global maximum.

(3)

In the absence of critical points,
V^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}
is decreasing if
q\widehat{\omega}>0
and increasing if
q\widehat{\omega}<0
.

Proof.

Suppose that
q\widehat{\omega}=0
. Then
V^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}(r)=\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}
and
\Omega^{2}r^{-2}=r^{-2}-2Mr^{-3}+Q^{2}r^{-4}
satisfies
\frac{d}{dr}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2})=0
if and only if
r=r_{\sharp}=\frac{3M}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{9M^{2}-8Q^{2}}
, which corresponds to a maximum.

Suppose that
q\widehat{\omega}\neq 0
. We consider the expressions:

\displaystyle r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}}{dr}=

\displaystyle\>-2q\widehat{\omega}r^{3}-2\ell(\ell+1)(r^{2}-3Mr+2Q^{2}),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dr}\left(r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}}{dr}\right)(r)=

\displaystyle\>-6{q}\widehat{\omega}r^{2}-2\ell(\ell+1)(2r-3M).

The critical points of
r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}}{dr}
are then located at:

r_{1,2}=\frac{\ell(\ell+1)\pm\sqrt{\ell^{2}(\ell+1)^{2}+9M\ell(\ell+1){q}\widehat{\omega}}}{-3{q}\widehat{\omega}}

Suppose that
{q}\widehat{\omega}>0
. Then only one of the values
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
can be non-negative, so there can be at most one critical point of
r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}
in the interval
[1,r_{c})
. As a result,
\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}
can have at most two zeroes, so
V^{\sharp}_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}
can have at most two critical points.

By extending the domain of the polynomial
r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}
to
r\in\mathbb{R}
, we note that when
q\widehat{\omega}>0
,
(r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr})(r)\to\pm\infty
as
r\to\mp\infty
, so the critical point with the largest
r
-value must be a local maximum of
r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}
. If there is another critical point, it has to be a local minimum, since a maximum or saddle point would contradict
\frac{d}{dr}(r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr})
having at most one zero. We conclude that
V^{\sharp}_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}
either has no critical points and is strictly decreasing or a maximum at
r=r_{\rm max}>1
. In the latter case,
V^{\sharp}
can have an additional critical point at
r=r_{\rm min}<r_{\rm max}
, which must be a minimum.

Now suppose that
{q}\widehat{\omega}<0
. Then it is straightforward to show that:

r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}}{dr}(1)=\ell(\ell+1)\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}(1)-2q\widehat{\omega}\geq-2q\widehat{\omega}>0.

Since
r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}}{dr}
is a polynomial of third degree, it can have at most three zeroes. Note that
(r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}}{dr})(r)\to\pm\infty
as
r\to\pm\infty
when
q\widehat{\omega}>0
and
r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}}{dr}(1)>0
, there must be one zero located in
r<r_{+}
and
r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}}{dr}
can therefore have at most two zeroes in
[r_{+},r_{c})
. This implies that
V^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}
can have at most two critical points.

The critical point with the largest
r
-value, if it exists, must be a local minimum since
(r^{5}\frac{dV^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}}{dr})(r)\to\infty
as
r\to\infty
when
q\widehat{\omega}<0
. The other critical point must be a maximum, since the presence of a another minimum or saddle point contradicts the fact that
V^{\sharp}_{\omega\ell}
has at most two critical points.
∎

When
\ell
is sufficiently large and
\widehat{\omega}^{2}\lesssim\ell(\ell+1)
, we can give a more precise characterization of the nature and locations of the critical points of
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}
; see also Figure
1
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\eta>0
and
\beta>0
. Then there exists a
L_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
suitably large depending on
q
,
\beta
and
\eta
, such that for all
\ell\geq L_{0}+1
and
\widehat{\omega}^{2}\leq\beta^{-1}\ell(\ell+1)
,
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r)
has a unique maximum at
r=r_{\rm max}
, a global maximum, with

|r_{\rm max}-r_{\sharp}|\leq\frac{\eta}{2}.

Furthermore,

(1)

Suppose that
q\widehat{\omega}<0
. For suitably small
\kappa_{c}
(depending on
L_{0}
and
\beta
),
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r)
has exactly one additional critical point: a local minimum located at
r=r_{\rm min,\infty}>r_{\rm max}
, with
\rho_{c}(r_{\rm min,\infty})<\eta
for
L_{0}
suitably large.

(2)

Suppose that
q\widehat{\omega}\geq 0
and suppose additionally that
q\widetilde{\omega}<0
. Then
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r)
has no additional critical points in
[r_{+},r_{c})
.

(3)

If
q\widehat{\omega}\geq 0
and
q\widetilde{\omega}\geq 0
, then for
\kappa_{+}
suitably small,
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r)
has exactly one additional critical point: a local minimum located at
r=r_{\rm min,+}<r_{\rm max}
, with
\rho_{+}(r_{\rm min,+})<\eta
for
L_{0}
suitably large and
r_{\rm min}=r_{+}
if
q\widetilde{\omega}=0
and
\kappa_{+}=0
.

Proof.

Note first of all that
|\widehat{\omega}|\leq\beta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)}\ll\ell(\ell+1)
for
L_{0}
suitably large. Since

r^{5}\frac{dV_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}(r)=2\ell(\ell+1)(r^{2}-3r+2Q^{2})+q^{2}O(r^{2})-q\widehat{\omega}O(r^{2})+O(r),

we must have that for
\eta>0
suitably small,
L_{0}
sufficiently large,
\frac{dV_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}
must change sign in the interval
(r_{\sharp}-\frac{\eta}{2},r_{\sharp}+\frac{\eta}{2})
, where
r_{\sharp}
is the largest root of the polynomial
r^{2}-3r+2Q^{2}
, so
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}
must have a critical point in
(r_{\sharp}-\frac{\eta}{2},r_{\sharp}+\frac{\eta}{2})
. Furthermore,
\frac{d^{2}V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr^{2}}<0
in
(r_{\sharp}-\frac{\eta}{2},r_{\sharp}+\frac{\eta}{2})
, so that critical point must be a local maximum.

We have that

V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(1)=-\left[{q}^{2}(1-r_{c}^{-1})-2{q}\widehat{\omega}\right](1-r_{c}^{-1}).

By taking
L_{0}
sufficiently large, we moreover have that
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r_{\rm max})\geq\frac{1}{16}\ell(\ell+1)
, so for
L_{0}
sufficiently large,
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r_{\rm max})>V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(1)
, so
r_{\rm max}
is in fact always a global maximum.

When
q\widehat{\omega}<0
, we have that
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}
is strictly increasing for suitably large
L_{0}
and
r
, and it is decreasing for
r>r_{\rm max}
, with
r-r_{\rm max}
suitably small. For
\kappa_{c}\geq 0
suitably small, it must therefore have a minimum at some
r_{c}>r_{\rm min,\infty}>r_{\rm max}
and
\rho_{c}(r_{\rm min,\infty})>0
can be made arbitrarily small by taking
L_{0}
suitably large.

Furthermore, for suitably large
L_{0}
,
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}
can be approximated by
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}^{\sharp}
, so we can apply Lemma
5.4
to conclude that there are no further critical points. This concludes property 1..

Let
q\omega\geq 0
. When
\kappa_{+}\neq 0
, we use the relative largeness of
\ell(\ell+1)
to obtain
\frac{dV_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}>0
for
r<r_{\rm max}
. When
\kappa_{+}=0
, we have that for
q\widetilde{\omega}<0
:

\frac{dV_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}(1)=\frac{d\widetilde{V}_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}}{dr}(1)=-2q\widetilde{\omega}>0,

so we can apply the relative largeness of
\ell(\ell+1)
to obtain also in this case
\frac{dV_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}>0
for
r<r_{\rm max}
. Furthermore, in both the
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{+}=0
cases, we have that
\frac{dV_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}<0
for
r>r_{\rm max}
, by using the relative largeness of
\ell(\ell+1)
, so we can conclude that
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}
has no additional critical points and Property 2. holds.

When
\kappa_{+}=0
and
q\widetilde{\omega}>0
, we have that
\frac{dV_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}(r_{+})<0
, so
V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}
must have a minimum at
1<r_{\rm min,+}<r_{\rm max}
. Furthermore, by applying the relative largeness of
\ell(\ell+1)
, we must have that
r_{\rm min,+}
approaches
r_{+}=1
when
L_{0}\to\infty
. In the case
q\widetilde{\omega}=0
,
\frac{dV_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr}(r_{+})=0
and
\frac{d^{2}V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}}{dr^{2}}(r_{+})>0
, so there is a minimum at
r=r_{+}
and there are no further critical points in
(r_{+},r_{c})
for suitably large
\ell
. We then conclude Property 3..
∎

Define the following modified potential function:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega\ell}(r):=

\displaystyle\>-\widehat{\omega}^{2}+V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r)+\Omega^{3}\frac{d^{2}\Omega}{dr^{2}}(r).

Lemma 5.6
.

The potential function
\mathcal{V}_{\omega\ell}
satisfies the estimates:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega\ell}(r(\rho_{+}))=

\displaystyle-(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}+\kappa_{+}^{2})+\left[-2q\widetilde{\omega}+\kappa_{+}\left(2\ell(\ell+1)+4\kappa_{+}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2})\right)\right]\rho_{+}

(5.19)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>\left[\ell(\ell+1)-q^{2}-r_{+}\kappa_{+}\left(6\ell(\ell+1)+r_{+}\kappa_{+})+(\ell+1)^{2}O(\kappa_{c}^{2})\right)\right]\rho_{+}^{2}

\displaystyle-2r_{+}\left[\ell(\ell+1)+3\ell(\ell+1)r_{+}\kappa_{+}+2r_{+}^{2}\kappa_{+}^{2}+(\ell+1)^{2}O(\kappa_{c}^{2})\right]\rho_{+}^{3}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>r_{+}^{2}\left[\ell(\ell+1)-r_{+}\kappa_{+}(2\ell(\ell+1)+r_{+}\kappa_{+})+(\ell+1)^{2}O(\kappa_{c}^{2})\right]\rho_{+}^{4},

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\omega\ell}(r(\rho_{c}))=

\displaystyle-(\widehat{\omega}^{2}+\kappa_{c}^{2})+\left[2q\widehat{\omega}+\kappa_{c}\left(2\ell(\ell+1)+4\kappa_{c}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2})\right)\right]\rho_{c}

(5.20)

\displaystyle\>+\left[\ell(\ell+1)-q^{2}-6\kappa_{c}r_{+}\ell(\ell+1)+\kappa_{+}O(\kappa_{c})+O(\kappa_{c}^{2})\right]\rho_{c}^{2}+O^{\ell}_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{3}).

Proof.

We have that:

\mathcal{V}_{\omega\ell}(r)=-\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-2{q}\widetilde{\omega}\rho_{+}+\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}-{q}^{2}\rho_{+}^{2}+\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\Omega\frac{d^{2}\Omega}{dr^{2}}+\frac{2}{3}\Lambda\right].

Furthermore,

r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\Omega\frac{d^{2}\Omega}{dr^{2}}=r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\frac{d}{dr}(\Omega^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr})=r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}-\frac{1}{4}\Omega^{-2}\left(\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{d^{2}\Omega^{2}}{dr^{2}}.

We now apply Lemma
2.2
and ignore higher order terms in
\kappa_{c}
to obtain (
5.19
).

The proof of (
5.20
) proceeds analogously, but is a little easier, as we do not keep precise track of
\kappa_{c}
.
∎

5.3.
Microlocal energy currents

In the remainder of §
5
, we will suppress the
\omega
-dependence appearing in the functions in (
5.13
), i.e. we denote
u(r)=u_{m\ell}(\omega,r)
,
H=H_{m\ell}(\omega,r)
and
V(r)=V_{\widehat{\omega}\ell}(r)
. We will analogously denote
v(r)=v_{m\ell}(\omega,r)
,
w(r)=w_{m\ell}(\omega,r)
and
\widetilde{V}(r)=\widetilde{V}_{\widetilde{\omega}\ell}(r)
.

We will derive
L^{2}
-type estimates for
u
in terms of
L^{2}
-type estimates for
H
by considering the following
microlocal energy currents
, which are frequency-space analogues of physical-space energy currents arising from vector field multipliers, that can be used to prove energy estimates to solutions to wave equations:

For smooth functions
h,y,f,g_{+},g_{\infty},\chi:(r_{+},r_{c})\to\mathbb{R}
, we define:

\displaystyle j_{1}^{h}[u]:=

\displaystyle\>h\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{u})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}|u|^{2},

\displaystyle j_{2}^{y}[u]:=

\displaystyle\>y(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V)|u|^{2})=y(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\widetilde{V})|u|^{2})

\displaystyle j_{3}^{f}[u]:=

\displaystyle f(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V)|u|^{2})+f^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{u})-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}|u|^{2},

\displaystyle\chi j^{T}[u]:=

\displaystyle\>-\widehat{\omega}\chi\textnormal{Re}\,(iu^{\prime}\overline{u}),

\displaystyle\chi j^{K}[u]:=

\displaystyle\>-\widetilde{\omega}\chi\textnormal{Re}\,(iu^{\prime}\overline{u}),

\displaystyle j^{g_{+}}_{+}[v]:=

\displaystyle\>g_{+}|v^{\prime}|^{2}-g_{+}\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|v|^{2},

\displaystyle j^{g_{\infty}}_{\infty}[w]:=

\displaystyle\>g_{\infty}|w^{\prime}|^{2}-g_{\infty}\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|w|^{2},

where we will either take
\chi\equiv 1
,
\chi=\chi_{K}
(in the case
\chi j^{K}[u]
) or
\chi=\chi_{T}
(in the case
\chi j^{T}[u]
).

Note that the functions
h,y,f,\chi,g_{+},g_{\infty}
will be frequency-independent. However, since we will be making different choices of functions for different frequency regimes, the resulting estimates could not be carried out directly in physical space, without a frequency decomposition. See however Appendix
A
, where analogues of the above energy currents are applied in physical space, without a (time-)frequency decomposition, to obtain integrated energy estimates modulo zeroth order terms, or restricted to large angular frequencies.

Lemma 5.7
.

The following identities hold:

\displaystyle\left(j_{1}^{h}[u]\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>h(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(V-\widehat{\omega}^{2})|u|^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}|u|^{2}+h\textnormal{Re}\,(u\overline{H}),

\displaystyle\left(j_{2}^{y}[u]\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>y^{\prime}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V)|u|^{2})-yV^{\prime}|u|^{2}+2y\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{H})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>y^{\prime}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\widetilde{V})|u|^{2})-y\widetilde{V}^{\prime}|u|^{2}+2y\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{H}),

\displaystyle\left(j_{3}^{f}[u]\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>2f^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}-fV^{\prime}|u|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}|u|^{2}+\textnormal{Re}\,(f^{\prime}u\overline{H}+2fu^{\prime}\overline{H}),

\displaystyle\left(\chi j^{T}[u]\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>-\widehat{\omega}\chi^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{iu^{\prime}}u)+\widehat{\omega}\chi\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H}),

\displaystyle\left(\chi j^{K}[u]\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>-\widetilde{\omega}\chi^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{iu^{\prime}}u)+\widetilde{\omega}\chi\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H}),

\displaystyle\left(j^{g_{+}}_{+}[v]\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>g_{+}^{\prime}|v^{\prime}|^{2}-\ell(\ell+1)(g_{+}\Omega^{2}r^{-2})^{\prime}|v|^{2}-2qg_{+}\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\textnormal{Re}\,(i\overline{v}v^{\prime})

\displaystyle+2g_{+}\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{v}v^{\prime})-\textnormal{Re}\,(2g_{+}v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H}),

\displaystyle\left(j^{g_{\infty}}_{\infty}[w]\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>g_{\infty}^{\prime}|w^{\prime}|^{2}-\ell(\ell+1)(g_{\infty}\Omega^{2}r^{-2})^{\prime}|w|^{2}-2qg_{\infty}\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\textnormal{Re}\,(i\overline{w}w^{\prime})

\displaystyle+2g_{\infty}\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{w}w^{\prime})-\textnormal{Re}\,(2g_{\infty}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H}).

Proof.

The expressions for the derivatives of the currents
j_{1}^{h}
,
j_{2}^{y}
,
j_{3}^{f}
,
\chi j^{T}
and
\chi j^{K}
follow straightforwardly by applying (
5.13
).

To obtain
\left(j^{g_{\infty}}_{\infty}[w]\right)^{\prime}
, we use that:

e^{-i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u^{\prime\prime}=e^{-i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}(e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}w)^{\prime\prime}\\
=w^{\prime\prime}+2i(\widehat{\omega}-q\rho_{c}(r_{*}))w^{\prime}+\left[iqr^{-2}\Omega^{2}-(\widehat{\omega}-q\rho_{c}(r_{*}))^{2}\right]w.

Hence,
w
satisfies the following ODE:

w^{\prime\prime}+2i(\widehat{\omega}-q\rho_{c}(r_{*}))w^{\prime}-[V-2q\widehat{\omega}\rho_{c}(r_{*})+q^{2}\rho_{c}^{2}(r_{*})-iqr^{-2}\Omega^{2}]w=e^{-i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}H.

We then observe that

V-2q\widehat{\omega}\rho_{c}(r_{*})+q^{2}\rho_{c}^{2}(r_{*})-iqr^{-2}\Omega^{2}=\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}+\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]-iqr^{-2}\Omega^{2}.

The expression for
\left(j^{g_{\infty}}_{\infty}[w]\right)^{\prime}
then follows by a straightforward application of the Leibniz rule.

Analogously, we obtain:

v^{\prime\prime}+2i(-\widetilde{\omega}-q\rho_{+}(r_{*}))v^{\prime}-[\widetilde{V}+2q\widetilde{\omega}\rho_{+}(r_{*})+q^{2}\rho_{+}^{2}(r_{*})-iqr^{-2}\Omega^{2}]v=e^{+i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}H

and we observe that

\widetilde{V}+2q\widetilde{\omega}\rho_{+}(r_{*})+q^{2}\rho^{2}_{+}(r_{*})-iqr^{-2}\Omega^{2}=\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}+\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]-iqr^{-2}\Omega^{2}.

The expression for
\left(j^{g_{+}}_{\infty}[v]\right)^{\prime}
then follows by a straightforward integration of the Leibniz rule.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

We have that

\displaystyle j^{T}[u](\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty),

\displaystyle j^{T}[u](-\infty)=

\displaystyle\>-\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}|u|^{2}(-\infty),

\displaystyle j^{K}[u](\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}|u|^{2}(\infty),

\displaystyle j^{K}[u](-\infty)=

\displaystyle\>-\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty).

In particular,
j^{T}[u](\infty)-j^{T}[u](-\infty)
or
j^{K}[u](\infty)-j^{K}[u](-\infty)
are non-negative definite if and only if

\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}>0.

Proof.

By (
5.17
) and (
5.18
), we have that

\displaystyle-\widehat{\omega}\lim_{r_{*}\to\infty}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu^{\prime}\overline{u})(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty),

\displaystyle-\widehat{\omega}\lim_{r_{*}\to-\infty}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu^{\prime}\overline{u})(r_{*})=

\displaystyle-\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}|u|^{2}(-\infty),

which gives the stated limits for
j^{T}[u]
. The limits for
j^{K}[u]
follow analogously.
∎

Definition 5.3
.

We refer to frequencies satisfying
\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}<0
as
superradiant frequencies
and frequencies satisfying
\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}>0
as
non-superradiant frequencies
.

5.4.
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}
-weighted energy estimates in frequency space

In this section, we derive
L^{2}
-estimates with
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}
-weights in the regions
\{r\geq R_{1}\}
and
\{r\leq r_{1}\}
, respectively, with
r_{1}\geq r_{+}
and
R_{1}\leq r_{c}
chosen, such that
\rho_{c}(R_{1})
and
\rho_{+}(r_{1})
are suitably small.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
p\in(0,2)
. For
1<R_{1}<r_{c}
with
\rho_{c}(R_{1})
suitably small, there exist a constant
c,C=c(p)>0
and a numerical constant
C>0
, such that:

c\int_{r_{*}(R_{1})}^{\infty}\left[(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}\\
\leq Cp^{-1}q^{2}\int_{r_{*}\left(\frac{R_{1}}{2-r_{c}^{-1}R_{1}}\right)}^{\infty}\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
+Cp^{-1}\ell(\ell+1)\int_{r_{*}\left(\frac{R_{1}}{2-r_{c}^{-1}R_{1}}\right)}^{r_{*}(R_{1})}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(5.21)

Furthermore, if
\ell\geq L
, with
L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
suitably large, depending on
q
, then:

c\int_{r_{*}(R_{1})}^{\infty}\left[(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}\\
\leq Cp^{-1}\ell(\ell+1)\int_{r_{*}\left(\frac{R_{1}}{2-r_{c}^{-1}R_{1}}\right)}^{r_{*}(R_{1})}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(5.22)

Proof.

Let
g_{\infty}=\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}=\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-\frac{p}{2}}
with
0<p<2
. By Lemma
2.2
, it follows that:

\displaystyle r^{-2}\Omega^{2}=

\displaystyle(\rho_{c}+2\kappa_{c})\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c}O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{2})+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{3}),

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})=

\displaystyle\>-2r^{-2}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})+\kappa_{c}r^{-2}O_{\infty}(\rho_{c})+r^{-2}O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{2}).

Furthermore, we can estimate:

2\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]=2r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\left[r\frac{d(1-2Mr^{-1}+Q^{2}r^{-2}-\frac{\Lambda}{3}r^{2})}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}r^{2}\right]=4r^{-2}\Omega^{2}r^{-1}(M-Q^{2}r^{-1})\\
=r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(O_{\infty}(\rho_{c})+\kappa_{c}O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{0})).

Then we integrate
\left(j^{g_{\infty}}_{\infty}[w]\right)^{\prime}
and apply Lemma
5.7
, using that the boundary conditions on
u
at
r_{*}=\infty
imply that
w^{\prime}=\Omega^{2}O_{\omega,\ell}(r^{-2})
, so the boundary terms
j^{g_{\infty}}_{\infty}[w](\pm\infty)
vanish. We obtain:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\left(\rho_{c}(p+O(\rho_{c}))+\kappa_{c}(p+O(\rho_{c}))\right)\chi_{R_{1}}+\chi^{\prime}_{R_{1}}\right](r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}\\
+(2-p)\ell(\ell+1)\left(\rho_{c}(1+O(\rho_{c}))+\kappa_{c}(1+O(\rho_{c}))\right)\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
=2q\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega^{2})^{2-p}\textnormal{Re}\,(i\overline{w}w^{\prime})\,dr_{*}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{2-p}(O_{\infty}(\rho_{c})+\kappa_{c}O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{0}))\textnormal{Re}\,(i\overline{w}w^{\prime})\\
+\ell(\ell+1)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{R_{1}}^{\prime}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(5.23)

We apply Young’s inequality to obtain the following: there exists a constant
C>0
, such that for
\mu\in(0,2)
:

2(|q|+C(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c}))(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\textnormal{Re}\,(i\overline{w}w^{\prime})|\leq\frac{p\mu}{2}(\rho_{c}+2\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+\frac{2}{p\mu}(q^{2}\rho_{c}+\frac{r^{-2}\Omega^{2}}{\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{c}})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\\
\leq\frac{p\mu}{2}(\rho_{c}+2\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+\frac{2}{p\mu}(|q|+C(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c}))^{2}\rho_{c}(1+O(\kappa_{c})+O(\rho_{c}))(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}.

(5.24)

Note that for fixed
p\in(0,2)
and
q\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists an
L\in\mathbb{N}
such that for
\ell\geq L
, we can absorb the second term on the very RHS of (
5.24
) into
(2-p)\ell(\ell+1)\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}
.

For general
\ell
, we will make use of a Hardy-type inequality to absorb integral of the terms on the very RHS of (
5.24
) that are non-vanishing when
q=0
.
First, observe that:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{c}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\chi_{R_{1}}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{d(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})}{d\rho_{c}}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{2-\frac{p}{2}}\chi_{R_{1}}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
=C\int_{\mathbb{R}}-\frac{d(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\chi_{R_{1}}|w|^{2}\,dr=\frac{C}{2-\frac{p}{2}}\int_{1}^{r_{c}}-\frac{d}{dr}\left((\Omega^{2}r^{-2})^{2-\frac{p}{2}}\right)\chi_{R_{1}}|w|^{2}\,dr.

We integrate by parts and estimate:

\int_{1}^{r_{c}}-\frac{d}{dr}\left((\Omega^{2}r^{-2})^{2-\frac{p}{2}}\right)\chi_{R_{1}}|w|^{2}\,dr=\int_{1}^{r_{c}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2})^{2-\frac{p}{2}}\frac{d}{dr}\left(\chi_{R_{1}}|w|^{2}\right)\,dr\\
\leq 2\int_{\mathbb{R}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4-p}\chi_{R_{1}}|w||w^{\prime}|\,dr_{*}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4-p}\chi_{R_{1}}^{\prime}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
\leq\frac{p(2-\frac{p}{2})}{8C}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\chi_{R_{1}}|w^{\prime}|^{2}\,dr_{*}+\frac{8C}{p(2-\frac{p}{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})^{-1}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{8-p}\chi_{R_{1}}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
+\int_{\mathbb{R}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4-p}\chi_{R_{1}}^{\prime}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}.

The second term on the very right-hand side above can be absorbed into the left-hand side for
p\in(0,4)
and sufficiently small
\rho_{c}(R_{1})
and
\mu
, so we conclude that:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho_{c}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\chi_{R_{1}}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}\leq\frac{p}{8}\int_{\mathbb{R}}r^{p-1}(\Omega^{2})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\chi_{R_{1}}|w^{\prime}|^{2}\,dr_{*}+C\int_{\mathbb{R}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4-p}\chi_{R_{1}}^{\prime}|w|^{2}\,dr_{*}.

Finally, the first term on the RHS above can be absorbed into the LHS of (
9.7
).
∎

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
p\in(0,2)
. For
1<r_{1}<r_{c}
with
\rho_{+}(r_{1})
suitably small, there exist a constant
c,C=c(p)>0
and a numerical constant
C>0
, such that:

c\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{1})}\left[(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}\\
\leq Cp^{-1}q^{2}\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(\frac{r_{i}}{2-r_{+}^{-1}r_{1}})}\rho_{+}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
+Cp^{-1}\ell(\ell+1)\int_{r_{*}(r_{1})}^{r_{*}(\frac{r_{1}}{2-r_{+}^{-1}r_{1}})}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\,dr_{*}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(5.25)

Furthermore, if
\ell\geq L
, with
L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
suitably large, depending on
q
, then:

c\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{1})}\left[(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}\\
\leq Cp^{-1}\ell(\ell+1)\int_{r_{*}(r_{1})}^{r_{*}(\frac{r_{1}}{2-r_{+}^{-1}r_{1}})}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\,dr_{*}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(5.26)

Proof.

We repeat the arguments in the proof of Proposition
5.21
, with
\rho_{+}
taking on the role of
\rho_{c}
,
v
taking on the role of
w
and with
g_{+}=\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}
.
∎

5.5.
Frequency domains

We will divide the frequency domain
\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\times(\mathbb{N}_{0})_{\ell}
into sub-domains, where we will apply different choices of microlocal energy currents from §
5.3
.
Let
L_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
be dimensionless constants. We will later choose
L_{0}
to be sufficiently large and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
to be suitably small. Define:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}:=

\displaystyle\>\left\{(\omega,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\,|\,\ell\geq L_{0}+1,r_{+}^{2}\widehat{\omega}^{2}\leq\alpha\ell(\ell+1)\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}:=

\displaystyle\>\left\{(\omega,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\,|\,\ell\geq L_{0}+1,\alpha\ell(\ell+1)<r_{+}^{2}\widehat{\omega}^{2}\leq\beta^{-1}\ell(\ell+1)\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,\rm{time}}:=

\displaystyle\>\left\{(\omega,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\,|\,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0},r_{+}^{2}\widehat{\omega}^{2}>\beta^{-1}(1+\ell(\ell+1))\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}:=

\displaystyle\>\left\{(\omega,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\,|\,\ell\leq L_{0},\>r_{+}^{2}\widetilde{\omega}^{2}\leq\gamma,\,\gamma<r_{+}^{2}\widehat{\omega}^{2}\leq\beta^{-1}(1+L_{0}(L_{0}+1))\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}:=

\displaystyle\>\left\{(\omega,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\,|\,\ell\leq L_{0},\>r_{+}^{2}\widetilde{\omega}^{2}>\gamma,\,\gamma<r_{+}^{2}\widehat{\omega}^{2}\leq\beta^{-1}(1+L_{0}(L_{0}+1))\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\infty}:=

\displaystyle\>\left\{(\omega,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\,|\,\ell\leq L_{0},\>r_{+}^{2}\widehat{\omega}^{2}\leq\gamma\right\}.

We moreover denote
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp}:=\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\flat}:=\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\infty}
.

Let
q_{1}>0
. We will restrict to
|q|<q_{1}
. Let
q_{0}>0
. We will further restrict to
q_{0}<|q|<q_{1}
when considering
\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
. We will allow any uniform constant in the estimates in the sections below to depend on
q_{1}
and we will specify when allow the constants to depend also on
q_{0}
.

The frequency domain
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp}
can be considered a high-frequency domain. Superradiant frequencies in
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp}
are entirely contained
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}
, because
\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}>0
in
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
.

The frequency domain
\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
consists of bounded superradiant and non-superradiant frequencies.

In the high-frequency domain
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp}
, we will only apply microlocal energy currents to derive the desired estimates for
u
. In the bounded frequency domain
\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
, we will instead appeal to Wronskian estimates and pointwise estimates of homogeneous solutions to (
5.13
). In particular, the analysis of
\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
is closely connected to the validity of mode stability on the real axis, away from
\widehat{\omega}=0
and
\widetilde{\omega}=0
, for (
2.16
).

We will treat each of the above frequency domains independently in the sections below.

6.
Integrated estimates in frequency space: large frequencies

In this section, we consider the large frequency domain
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp}
. We treat relatively large
\omega
-dominated frequencies
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
in §
6.1
, relatively large
\ell
-dominated frequencies
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}
in §
6.2
and frequencies where
\omega
and
\ell
are large and
\omega^{2}
is comparable to
\ell(\ell+1)

\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}
in §
6.3
.

In the remainder of §
6
, we will assume that
r_{+}=1
, for notational convenience.

6.1.
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
:
\omega
-dominated frequencies

In the frequency domain
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
, we can exploit that
\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V
is globally very large and therefore the estimates are not sensitive to the precise form of the potential
V
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
E\geq 2
and
1\leq s<2
. Then, for suitably small
\beta>0
, there exists a constant
c>0
, such that for
y:[r_{+},r_{c})_{r}\to\mathbb{R}
, with
y(r)=(1-r_{+}r_{c}^{-1})^{-2+s}(\rho_{+}(r)^{2-s}-\rho_{c}(r)^{2-s})
:

c\int_{\mathbb{R}}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\left(\rho_{c}^{1-s}+\rho_{+}^{1-s}\right)(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})|u|^{2})\,dr_{*}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2y\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\omega}\chi_{K}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\omega}\chi_{T}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}

(6.1)

Proof.

Consider the microlocal energy current
j_{2}^{y}[u]
. Note that

y^{\prime}(r)=(2-s)(1-r_{+}r_{c}^{-1})^{-2+s}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\left(\rho_{c}(r)^{1-s}+\rho_{+}(r)^{1-s}\right).

Integrating
(j_{2}^{y}[u])^{\prime}
then gives:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{\prime}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V)|u|^{2})-yV^{\prime}|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}=2(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty))-\int_{\mathbb{R}}y\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

Using that
\widehat{\omega}^{2}\geq\beta^{-1}(1+\ell(\ell+1))
and
1\leq s<2
, we can take
\beta
suitably small to ensure that there exists a constant
c>0
such that:

y^{\prime}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V)|u|^{2})-yV^{\prime}|u|^{2}\geq c(2-s)r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\left(\rho_{c}^{1-s}+\rho_{+}^{1-s}\right)\left[|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})|u|^{2}\right].

We therefore obtain

c\int_{\mathbb{R}}(2-s)r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\left(\rho_{c}^{1-s}+\rho_{+}^{1-s}\right)(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})|u|^{2})\,dr_{*}\\
=2(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty))-\int_{\mathbb{R}}y\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

We can integrate
E(\chi_{T}j^{T}[u])^{\prime}
or
E(\chi_{K}j^{K}[u])^{\prime}
with
E\geq 2
and
\beta
sufficiently large to estimate the boundary terms on the right-hand side and absorb the bulk terms involving
\chi^{\prime}_{T}
and
\chi_{K}^{\prime}
to conclude (
6.1
).
∎

From Proposition
6.1
, it follows moreover that we extend the domain of validity of the
r
-weighted and
(r-1)^{-1}
-weighted estimates from that are valid for large
\ell
to the frequency domain
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
(which includes bounded
\ell
).

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
E\geq 2
,
1\leq s<2
and
p\leq s
. Then, for
\beta
suitably small, there exists a constant
c>0
, such that for
y:[r_{+},r_{c})_{r}\to\mathbb{R}
, with
y(r)=(1-r_{+}r_{c}^{-1})^{-2+s}(\rho_{+}(r)^{2-s}-\rho_{c}(r)^{2-s})
:

c\int_{\mathbb{R}}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\left(\rho_{c}^{1-s}+\rho_{+}^{1-s}\right)(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})|u|^{2})\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{1})}\left[(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1))(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{r_{*}(R_{1})}^{\infty}\left[(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}\\
\leq-2\int_{\mathbb{R}}y\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\omega}\chi_{K}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\omega}\chi_{T}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(6.2)

Proof.

Let
s=p
and combine (
6.1
) with (
5.21
) and (
5.25
) and use that
\widehat{\omega}^{2}\gg\ell(\ell+1)
to absorb the terms appearing with a factor
q^{2}
and
\chi^{\prime}_{R_{1}}
or
\chi^{\prime}_{r_{1}}
on the right-hand side of (
5.21
) and (
5.25
).
∎

6.2.
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}
: large
\ell
, superradiant frequencies

This frequency regime contains the superradiant frequencies, so we cannot appeal to
j^{T}[u]
or
j^{K}[u]
currents to directly control boundary terms. Instead, we will exploit that
V-\omega^{2}
is very large near the maximum of the potential to absorb error terms generated by the
localized
currents:
\chi_{T}j^{T}[u]
and
\chi_{K}j^{K}[u]
. This can be thought of as a quantitative realization of the fact that
superradiant frequencies are not trapped
.

We will moreover need to couple estimates in
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}
with the
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}
-weighted estimates from Propositions
5.9
and
5.10
, which are valid for sufficiently large
\ell
. This is a characteristic feature of the fact that we wish to include the extremal cases
\kappa_{+}=0
and
\kappa_{c}=0
in our estimates, and it would be absent if we restricted to
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
.
12
12

12

In the setting of the neutral wave equation on Kerr in a near-extremal setting, the coupling also appears in
[
Gaj23a
]
, but it does not appear in the purely sub-extremal analysis of
[
DRSR16
]
.

Figure 2
.
A sketch of the graph of the potential
V
as a function of
\rho_{+}
in the frequency regime
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}
with
q\widehat{\omega}<0
and
q\widetilde{\omega}<0
,
\kappa_{c}
suitably small and sketches of the graphs of the functions
f
,
h
,
\chi_{T}
and
\chi_{K}
that appear in the currents
j_{2}^{h}[u]
,
j_{3}^{f}[u]
,
\chi_{T}j^{T}[u]
and
\chi_{K}j^{K}[u]
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
E\geq 2
,
\delta,\eta>0
and
1\leq p<2
. Then there exist functions
f,h:\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
f(r_{*})=-1+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}(r(r_{*}))
for
r_{*}\leq(r_{3})_{*}
and
f(r_{*})=1-(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}(r(r_{*}))
for
r_{*}\geq(R_{3})_{*}
, and
h
is compactly supported in
(r_{*}(r_{\sharp}-2\eta),r_{*}(r_{\sharp}+2\eta))
and there exists a constant
c>0
, such that for
L_{0}
suitably large and
\alpha>0
suitably small:

c\int_{(r_{2})_{*}}^{(R_{2})_{*}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left[\widehat{\omega}^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)+1\right]|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{1})}\Big[(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}|^{2}\Big]\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{r_{*}(R_{1})}^{\infty}\Big[(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}|^{2}\Big]\,dr_{*}\leq-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Re((f^{\prime}+h)u\overline{H}+2fu^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*},\\
+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\omega}\chi_{K}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\omega}\chi_{T}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(6.3)

Proof.

j_{3}^{f}[u]
-current estimates:

By taking
\eta>0
arbitrarily small,
L_{0}
appropriately large and applying Lemma
5.5
, we obtain the existence of a maximum of the potential
V
at some
r_{+}<r_{\rm max}<r_{c}
, with
|r_{\rm max}-r_{\sharp}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\eta
.

Let
r_{+}<r_{3}<r_{2}<r_{1}<R_{1}<R_{2}<R_{3}<r_{c}
