. We define the function
f:\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}\to\mathbb{R}
as follows:
let
a>0
,
s>0
and let
f(r_{*})=-1+(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(r(r_{*}))
for
r_{*}\leq r_{*}(r_{3})
,
f(r_{*})=1-(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(r(r_{*}))
for
r_{*}\geq r_{*}(R_{3})
and
f(r_{*})=\frac{2}{\pi}\arctan(a(r_{*}-r_{*}(r_{\rm max})))
, for
r_{*}(r_{2})\leq r\leq r_{*}(R_{2})
. Then, for
r_{2}\leq r\leq R_{2}
:

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>\frac{2a}{\pi(1+a^{2}(r_{*}-r_{*}(r_{\rm max}))^{2})}

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{4a^{3}((r_{*}-r_{*}(r_{\rm max}))}{\pi(1+a^{2}(r_{*}-r_{*}(r_{\rm max}))^{2})^{2}},

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime\prime}(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{4a^{3}(1-3a^{2}(r_{*}-r_{*}(r_{\rm max}))^{2})}{\pi(1+a^{2}(r_{*}-r_{*}(r_{\rm max}))^{2})^{3}},

so
-f^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
iff
|r_{*}-r_{*}(r_{\rm max})|<\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
.

For
r\leq r_{3}
and
r\geq R_{3}
we have that:

\displaystyle f^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>\frac{\delta}{2}\Omega^{2}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}-1}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})\geq\frac{\delta}{2}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})\quad(r\leq r_{3}),

\displaystyle f^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{\delta}{2}\Omega^{2}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}-1}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})\geq\frac{\delta}{2}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})\quad(r\geq R_{3}).

We can choose
f
in
(r_{3},r_{2})\cup(R_{2},R_{3})
such that
f
is a
C^{3}
function,
f^{\prime}>0
globally, and there exists a constant
C>0
such that globally:

\displaystyle|f^{\prime\prime\prime}(r_{*})|\leq

\displaystyle\>C|r_{*}|^{-3}.

See Figure
2
for an illustration of the graph of
f
.

We then integrate
(j_{3}^{f}[u])^{\prime}
to obtain:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}2f^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}-fV^{\prime}|u|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}=\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)+|u^{\prime}|^{2}(\infty)+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)+|u^{\prime}|^{2}(-\infty).\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(f^{\prime}u\overline{H}+2fu^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}

Note that the boundary conditions on
u
at
r_{*}=\pm\infty
imply that:

\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)+|u^{\prime}|^{2}(\infty)+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)+|u^{\prime}|^{2}(-\infty)=2(\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)).

From the proof of Lemma
5.5
and the behaviour of
f
near
r=r_{\rm max}
, it follows moreover that for
\ell\geq L_{0}
with
L_{0}
suitably large and
|q\widehat{\omega}|\ll\ell(\ell+1)
, which, for
L_{0}
suitably large, is guaranteed by
\widehat{\omega}^{2}\leq\beta^{-1}\ell(\ell+1)
, there exist constants
b,B>0
, such that

-fV^{\prime}\geq b(\widehat{\omega}^{2}+\ell(\ell+1))(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(1-r_{\max}r^{-1})^{2}+Bq\widetilde{\omega}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{2}}-Bq\widehat{\omega}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{2}}.

If
q\widehat{\omega}>0
or
q\widetilde{\omega}<0
, the right-hand side above will be negative for sufficiently small
\rho_{c}
and
\kappa_{c}
or
\rho_{+}
and
\kappa_{+}
, respectively. This reflects the possible existence of a minimum of
V
for
\kappa_{c}
suitably small (depending on
\ell
) at a sufficiently large value of
r
when
q\widehat{\omega}>0
and at a small value of
r-1
when
q\widetilde{\omega}<0
and
\kappa_{+}
is suitably small, corresponding to points 1. and 3. of Lemma
5.5
, respectively. See also Figure
2
in the case
q\widehat{\omega}<0
.

Since
-f^{\prime\prime\prime}
has a good sign near
r=r_{\max}
and decays sufficiently fast for
\rho_{c}
or
\rho_{+}
suitably small, we can in fact estimate:

-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\geq b(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})r^{-2}\Omega^{2}[(\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})(1-r_{\max}r^{-1})^{2}+1]+Bq\widetilde{\omega}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{2}}-Bq\widehat{\omega}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{2}}.

Hence, there exist a constants
c>0
, depending on
r_{2},R_{2}
, such that:

c\int_{\mathbb{R}}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})|u^{\prime}|^{2}\,dr_{*}+c\int_{r_{*}(r_{2})}^{r_{*}(R_{2})}\left[(\omega^{2}+\ell(\ell+1))(1-r_{\max}r^{-1})^{2}+1\right]|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
=2(\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty))-B\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{2})}|q\widetilde{\omega}|\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}-B\int_{r_{*}(R_{2})}^{\infty}|q\omega|\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(f^{\prime}u\overline{H}+2fu^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

It remains to control the boundary terms and the integrals over
(-\infty,r_{*}(r_{2}))
and
(r_{*}(R_{2}),\infty)
on the right-hand side.
We control the latter by coupling the above
j_{3}^{f}[u]
-current estimate with the
(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}
-weighted estimates (
5.22
) and (
5.26
) from Propositions
5.9
and
5.10
, respectively, with
p\geq 1
. Here, we use again that
|q\widehat{\omega}|\ll\ell(\ell+1)
and
|q\widetilde{\omega}|\ll\ell(\ell+1)
. We obtain:

c\int_{\mathbb{R}}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})|u^{\prime}|^{2}\,dr_{*}+c\int_{r_{*}(r_{2})}^{r_{*}(R_{2})}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left[(\widehat{\omega}^{2}+\ell(\ell+1))(1-r_{\max}r^{-1})^{2}+1\right]|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{1})}\left[(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{r_{*}(R_{1})}^{\infty}\left[(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}=2(\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty))\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(f^{\prime}u\overline{H}+2fu^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(6.4)

Now we turn to the boundary terms
2(\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty))
. Due to the fact that
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}
includes superradiant frequencies, we cannot simply control the boundary terms with the microlocal energy currents
j^{T}[u]
or
j^{K}[u]
; see Lemma
5.8
. Instead, we consider
localized
currents
E\chi_{T}j^{T}[u]
or
E\chi_{K}j^{K}[u]
, with
E\geq 2
a constant, which will introduce additional error terms in the support of
\chi^{\prime}_{K}
and
\chi^{\prime}_{T}
that will subsequently need to be controlled via a
j_{1}^{h}[u]
-current supported near the maximum of
V
; see Figure
2
for an illustration.

j_{2}^{h}[u]
-current estimates:

We construct the function
h:[r_{+},r_{c})\to[0,1]
as follows: let
h
be a smooth function such that
h(r)=1
for
r\in(r_{\sharp}-\eta,r_{\sharp}+\eta)
and
h(r)=0
for
r\leq r_{\sharp}-2\eta
or
r\geq r_{\sharp}+2\eta
, with
\eta>0
suitably small. We can moreover choose
h
such that there exists a numerical constant
C>0
, such that:

|h^{\prime\prime}(r)|\leq C\eta^{-2}.

By integrating
(j_{2}^{h}[u])^{\prime}
and applying Lemma
5.7
, we then obtain:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(V-\omega^{2})|u|^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}h\textnormal{Re}\,(u\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(6.5)

Using that
\ell\geq L_{0}
with
L_{0}\gg 1
and
\omega^{2}\ll\ell(\ell+1)
(i.e. taking
\alpha
suitably small), we conclude that for
r\in[r_{\sharp}-2\eta,r_{\sharp}+2\eta]
, we can estimate:

V(r)-\omega^{2}\geq\frac{1}{8}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})(r_{\sharp})\ell(\ell+1)

(6.6)

and hence, for
r\in(r_{\sharp}-\eta,r_{\sharp}+\eta)
:

h(V(r)-\widehat{\omega}^{2})\geq\frac{1}{8}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})(r_{\sharp})\ell(\ell+1).

By combining (
6.5
) with (
6.4
), we can absorb the term
-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime\prime}
into the left-hand side of (
6.4
), using that, by construction,
r_{\rm max}\notin\textnormal{supp}\,h^{\prime\prime}
and taking
L_{0}
sufficiently large, depending on
c
and
\eta
.

We then obtain:

c\int_{(r_{2})_{*}}^{(R_{2})_{*}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)+1)|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}+c\int_{\mathbb{R}}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})|u^{\prime}|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{1})}\left[(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{r_{*}(R_{1})}^{\infty}\left[(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\right]\,dr_{*}=2(\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty))\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,((f^{\prime}+h)u\overline{H}+2fu^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(6.7)

j^{T}[u]
- and
j^{K}[u]
-current estimates:

Finally, we integrate
E(\chi_{T}j^{T}[u])^{\prime}
or
E(\chi_{K}j^{K}[u])^{\prime}
and use the defining properties of the cut-off functions
\chi_{K}
and
\chi_{T}
from §
2.10
to obtain:

E(\omega^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty))=-E\int_{r_{*}(r_{\sharp}-\eta)}^{r_{*}(r_{\sharp}+\eta)}(\widetilde{\omega}\chi_{K}^{\prime}+\widehat{\omega}\chi_{T}^{\prime})\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{iu^{\prime}}u)\,dr_{*}+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\widetilde{\omega}\chi_{K}+\widehat{\omega}\chi_{T})\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}

(6.8)

We can estimate

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\omega}\chi_{K}^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{iu^{\prime}}u)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>\frac{c}{2}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+2C^{2}\eta^{-2}c^{-1}\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\omega}\chi_{T}^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{iu^{\prime}}u)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>\frac{c}{2}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+2C^{2}\eta^{-2}c^{-1}\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}

and combine (
6.8
) with (
6.7
), taking
\alpha
suitably small and
L_{0}
suitably large in order to conclude (
6.3
).
∎

6.3.
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}
: trapped frequencies

In the frequency domain
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}
, we proceed analogously to
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}
, but we use that the corresponding frequencies are
non
-superradiant to integrate
E(j^{T}[u])^{\prime}
or
E(j^{K}[u])^{\prime}
without the need to use cut-off functions
\chi_{K}
and
\chi_{T}
and absorb error terms caused by
\chi_{K}^{\prime}\neq 0
and
\chi^{\prime}_{T}\neq 0
.

When combining the estimates in the different frequency regimes, however, it will be convenient to consider
E\chi_{r_{1}}(j^{K}[u])^{\prime}+E(1-\chi_{r_{1}})(j^{T}[u])^{\prime}
, with
\chi_{r_{1}}
a cut-off function with derivative supported close to
r=r_{+}
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
E\geq 2
,
s>0
and
1\leq p<2
. Then there exists a function
f:\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
f(r_{*})=-1+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{s}(r(r_{*}))
for
r_{*}\leq(r_{3})_{*}
and
f(r_{*})=1-(r^{-1}\Omega)^{s}(r(r_{*}))
for
r_{*}\geq(R_{3})_{*}
, and there exists a constant
c>0
, such that for
L_{0}
suitably large and
\alpha>0
suitably small:

c\int_{(r_{2})_{*}}^{(R_{2})_{*}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left[(1-r_{\rm max}r^{-1})^{2}(\widehat{\omega}^{2}+\ell(\ell+1))+1\right]|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{1})}\Big[(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}|^{2}\Big]\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{r_{*}(R_{1})}^{\infty}\Big[(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}|^{2}\Big]\,dr_{*}\leq-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Re(f^{\prime}u\overline{H}+2fu^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*},\\
+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\omega}\textnormal{Re}\,(i\chi_{r_{1}}u\overline{H})\,dr_{*}+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\omega}\textnormal{Re}\,(i(1-\chi_{r_{1}})u\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

(6.9)

Proof.

We first proceed exactly as in the proof of Proposition
6.3
, by coupling
j_{3}^{f}[u]
-current,
j_{+}^{g_{+}}[v]
-current and
j_{\infty}^{g_{\infty}}[w]
-current estimates to obtain (
6.4
).

Since
\omega\widetilde{\omega}>0
in
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}
, we can then immediately integrate
E(j^{T}[u])^{\prime}
or
E(j^{K}[u])^{\prime}
with
E\geq 2
and apply Lemma
5.8
to estimate the boundary terms on the right-hand side. However, for the sake of later convenience when passing to physical space, we nevertheless add cut-off functions:
E(\chi_{r_{1}}j^{K}[u])^{\prime}
and
E((1-\chi_{r_{1}})j^{T}[u])^{\prime}
. We then have to control the following additional term:

E\int_{R}(\widehat{\omega}-\widetilde{\omega})\chi_{r_{1}}^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{iu^{\prime}}u)\,dr_{*}.

We then estimate

2|(\widehat{\omega}-\widetilde{\omega})\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{iu^{\prime}}u)|\leq\eta|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\eta^{-1}|\widehat{\omega}-\widetilde{\omega}|^{2}|u|^{2}.

(6.10)

We then take
\eta>0
suitably small and use that
|\widehat{\omega}-\widetilde{\omega}|=|q|(1-r_{c}^{-1})
is bounded,
\chi^{\prime}_{r_{1}}
is supported away from
r=r_{\max}
and
\widetilde{\omega}^{2}
is large to absorb both terms on the right-hand side of (
6.10
) and conclude (
6.9
).

Observe however that, in contrast with the proof of Proposition
6.3
, we cannot remove the factor
(1-r_{\max}r^{-1})^{2}
appearing in (
6.4
), since that relied on (
6.6
), which used the assumption
\ell(\ell+1)\gg\widehat{\omega}^{2}
, which is not valid in
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}
where
\ell(\ell+1)\sim\widehat{\omega}^{2}
.
∎

7.
Integrated estimates in frequency space: bounded frequencies

In this section, we will restrict to
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
. An important role will be played by
homogeneous solutions

u_{+}
and
u_{\infty}
to (
5.13
) (with
H\equiv 0
), satisfying the following boundary conditions:

\lim_{r_{*}\to-\infty}e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u_{+}(r_{*})=\lim_{r_{*}\to\infty}e^{-i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u_{\infty}(r_{*})=1.

We will refer to
u_{+}
as the
event-horizon-normalized
homogeneous solution and
u_{\infty}
as the
infinity-normalized
homogeneous solution (when
\kappa_{c}=0
).

We define the corresponding Wronskian as follows:

\mathfrak{W}(\widehat{\omega},\ell):=\mathcal{W}(u_{+},u_{\infty})=u_{+}(r_{*})\frac{du_{\infty}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*})-u_{\infty}(r_{*})\frac{du_{+}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*}).

Since (
5.13
) does not have any first-order terms,
\mathfrak{W}
is independent of
r_{*}
.

We will restrict to
|q|\geq q_{0}
, where
q_{0}>0
can be taken arbitrarily small.
In the remainder of §
7
, we will allow any constant denoted
C
and
c
, as well as the constants implicit in the big-O notation, to depend on
L_{0}
,
\beta
,
q_{0}
and
q_{1}
.
If there is a further dependence on other constants, we will denote this in the subscript.

We will apply Green’s formula (
7.50
) in §
7.5
and §
7.6
to estimate solutions
u
to (
5.13
) that satisfy the boundary conditions (
5.17
) and (
5.18
) by homogeneous solutions
u_{+}
and
u_{\infty}
. An important ingredient for these estimates is a uniform estimate for
|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}
.

The structure of the present section is as follows:

•

In §
7.1
we approximate general solutions
u
to (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
by appropriate special functions by partioning the
r
-interval
(r_{+},r_{c})
into subintervals. These approximations are uniformly valid as
\widetilde{\omega},\widehat{\omega}\to 0
.

•

In §
7.2
and §
7.3
, we derive upper-bound estimates for the coefficients appearing in the approximations from the previous step in the case that
u=u_{+}
or
u=u_{\infty}
. This results in uniform estimates for
u=u_{+}
and
u_{\infty}
. In the cases
(\widehat{\omega},\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
and
(\widehat{\omega},\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\infty}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
, we moreover derive lower bounds for these coefficients. These lower bounds form a key ingredients in the Wronskian estimates. The key technique for deriving lower bound is a mathematically rigorous realization of the
matched asymptotics method
employed in black hole spacetimes; see also the discussion in §
1.2.3
. This method will be discussed more generally in upcoming work.

•

In §
7.4
, we derive estimates for
|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}
that are uniform and do not degenerate as
\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c},\widetilde{\omega},\widehat{\omega}\to 0
by combining the coefficient estimates from the previous step for
(\widehat{\omega},\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\infty}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}
with a mode stability argument for
(\widehat{\omega},\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
.

•

Finally, we use Green’s formula (
7.50
) in §
7.5
and §
7.6
and apply the homogeneous and Wronskian estimates from the previous step to derive
(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}
-weighted
L^{2}
-estimates for solutions
u
to (
5.13
) that satisfy the boundary conditions (
5.17
) and (
5.18
)

In the remainder of this §
7
, we fix
r_{+}=1
.

7.1.
Asymptotics of homogeneous solutions

We will first derive uniform estimates for homogeneous solutions
u_{+}
and
u_{\infty}
to (
5.13
) in the frequency range
\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
that remain valid as
|\widehat{\omega}|\to 0
or
|\widetilde{\omega}|\to 0
.

It will be convenient to employ the Couch–Torrence radial coordinates
s_{+}
and
s_{c}
, introduced in §
2.5
. Via the Lemma
7.1
below, we will reformulate (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
to bring it in a form amenable to a uniform analysis in
\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Set
r_{+}=1
. Let

\displaystyle U_{+}(s_{+}):=

\displaystyle\>(s_{+}+1)(r^{-1}\Omega u)(r_{*}(s_{+})),

\displaystyle U_{c}(s_{c}):=

\displaystyle\>(s_{c}+1)(r^{-1}\Omega u)(r_{*}(s_{c})).

Then
u
satisfies (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
and if and only if:

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}U_{+}}{ds_{+}^{2}}=

\displaystyle\>\rho_{+}^{4}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}{\mathcal{V}}U_{+},

(7.1)

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}U_{c}}{ds_{c}^{2}}=

\displaystyle\>\rho_{c}^{4}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}\mathcal{V}U_{c},

(7.2)

with
\mathcal{V}
introduced in Lemma
5.6
.

We can write:

\displaystyle\rho_{+}^{4}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}{\mathcal{V}}=

\displaystyle(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-2}\left[-(\widetilde{\omega}_{+}^{2}+\kappa_{+}^{2})+2\nu_{+}s_{+}^{-1}+\left(\mu_{+}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)s_{+}^{-2}+(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{-3})\right],

(7.3)

\displaystyle\rho_{c}^{4}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}\mathcal{V}=

\displaystyle(1+2\kappa_{c}s_{c})^{-2}\left[-(\widehat{\omega}^{2}_{c}+\kappa_{c}^{2})+2\nu_{c}s_{c}^{-1}+\left(\mu_{c}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)s_{c}^{-2}+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{c}+\kappa_{+})O_{\infty}(s_{c}^{-3})\right],

(7.4)

with the constants
\widehat{\omega}_{+},\widetilde{\omega}_{+},\mu_{+},\mu_{c},\nu_{+},\nu_{c}
defined above satisfying:

\displaystyle\widehat{\omega}^{2}_{+}+\kappa_{+}^{2}=

\displaystyle\>\widetilde{\omega}^{2}+\kappa_{+}^{2}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\omega}^{2}_{c}+\kappa_{c}^{2}=

\displaystyle\>\widehat{\omega}^{2}+O(\kappa_{c}),

\displaystyle\mu_{+}=

\displaystyle\>\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)+\frac{1}{4}-q^{2}-6\widetilde{\omega}\widehat{\omega}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2})},

\displaystyle\mu_{c}=

\displaystyle\>\sqrt{\ell(\ell+1)+\frac{1}{4}-q^{2}-6\widetilde{\omega}\widehat{\omega}+O(\kappa_{c})+O(|\widehat{\omega}|)},

\displaystyle\nu_{+}=

\displaystyle\>-\widetilde{\omega}(q+2\widetilde{\omega})+\kappa_{+}\ell(\ell+1)+O(\kappa_{c}^{2}),

\displaystyle\nu_{c}=

\displaystyle\>\widehat{\omega}(q-2\widehat{\omega})+\kappa_{c}\ell(\ell+1)+O(\kappa_{c})+O(|\widehat{\omega}|).

In particular, when
\kappa_{c}=\kappa_{+}=0
:

\displaystyle\rho_{+}^{4}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}{\mathcal{V}}=

\displaystyle-\widetilde{\omega}^{2}+2\widetilde{\omega}(q-2\widehat{\omega})s_{+}^{-1}+(\ell(\ell+1)-q^{2}-6\widetilde{\omega}\widehat{\omega})s_{+}^{-2}

(7.5)

\displaystyle+2\widehat{\omega}(q-2\widehat{\omega})s_{+}^{-3}-\widehat{\omega}^{2}s_{+}^{-4},

\displaystyle\rho_{c}^{4}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}\mathcal{V}=

\displaystyle-\widehat{\omega}^{2}-2\widehat{\omega}(q+2\widetilde{\omega})s_{c}^{-1}+\left(\ell(\ell+1)-q^{2}-6\omega\widetilde{\omega}\right)s_{c}^{-2}

(7.6)

\displaystyle+2\widetilde{\omega}(q-2\widehat{\omega})s_{c}^{-3}-\widetilde{\omega}^{2}s_{c}^{-4}.

Proof.

The function
u
satisfies (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
if and only if

\frac{d^{2}(\Omega u)}{dr^{2}}+\Omega^{-4}\left(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V-\Omega^{3}\frac{d^{2}\Omega}{dr^{2}}\right)(\Omega u)=0,

or equivalently,

\frac{d^{2}(\Omega u)}{dr^{2}}+\Omega^{-4}\left(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\widetilde{V}-\Omega^{3}\frac{d^{2}\Omega}{dr^{2}}\right)(\Omega u)=0.

Note that

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}(\Omega u)}{dr^{2}}=

\displaystyle\>r^{-3}\frac{d^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega u)}{d\rho_{c}^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}(\Omega u)}{dr^{2}}=

\displaystyle\>=r^{-3}\frac{d^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega u)}{d\rho_{+}^{2}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega u)}{d\rho_{c}^{2}}=

\displaystyle\>(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}\left(-\widehat{\omega}^{2}+V+\Omega^{3}\frac{d^{2}\Omega}{dr^{2}}\right)(r^{-1}\Omega u),

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega u)}{d\rho_{+}^{2}}=

\displaystyle\>(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}\left(-\widetilde{\omega}^{2}+\widetilde{V}+\Omega^{3}\frac{d^{2}\Omega}{dr^{2}}\right)(r^{-1}\Omega u).

Finally, note that

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{ds_{c}^{2}}((s_{c}+1)r^{-1}\Omega u)=

\displaystyle\>\rho_{c}^{3}\frac{d^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega u)}{d\rho_{c}^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{ds_{+}^{2}}((s_{+}+1)r^{-1}\Omega u)=

\displaystyle\>\rho_{+}^{3}\frac{d^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega u)}{d\rho_{+}^{2}},

to conclude (
7.1
) and (
7.2
).

We apply Lemma
2.2
to write:

\rho_{+}^{4}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2})^{-2}=\frac{\rho_{+}^{4}}{(2\kappa_{+}\rho_{+}+(1-6\kappa_{+}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2}))\rho_{+}^{2}+(-2+6\kappa_{+}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2}))\rho_{+}^{3}+\left(1-2\kappa_{+}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2})\right)\rho_{+}^{4})^{2}}\\
=\frac{1}{(1-6\kappa_{+}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2})+(-2+6\kappa_{+}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2}))\rho_{+}+\left(1-2\kappa_{+}+O(\kappa_{c}^{2})\right)\rho_{+}^{2})^{2}}\\
=(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-2}(1+s^{-1})^{4}(1+\kappa_{c}^{2}O_{\infty}(s^{0})).

After applying (
5.19
), we then obtain (
7.3
).

We obtain in a similar manner:

\rho_{c}^{4}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2})^{-2}=(1+2\kappa_{c}s_{c})^{-2}(1+O_{\infty}(\kappa_{c})+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c})).

We can similarly derive (
7.4
) after applying (
5.20
).

When
\kappa_{c}=\kappa_{+}=0
, we have that
\rho_{c}^{4}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}=\Omega^{-4}
and
\rho_{+}^{4}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-2}=r^{4}
and the expressions simplify greatly.
∎

In order to obtain uniform estimates for solutions
u
to (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
as
|\widetilde{\omega}|\to 0
or
|\widehat{\omega}|\to 0
, we divide the
r
-domain
(1,r_{c})
into four subintervals, where we will approximate
u
in different ways.

Let
\digamma_{0}>0
be a constant, which we will later take to be suitably large. Consider the regions
I,II,III,IV\subset(1,r_{c})
, which are defined as follows:

•

I
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{16}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\leq s_{+}<

\displaystyle\>\infty

\displaystyle(|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>

\displaystyle\digamma_{0}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{16}}\kappa_{+}^{-\frac{1}{4}}(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq s_{+}<

\displaystyle\>\infty

\displaystyle(|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq

\displaystyle\digamma_{0}).

•

II
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{+}\leq

\displaystyle\>2\digamma_{0}^{\frac{3}{8}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}

\displaystyle(\kappa_{+}>

\displaystyle\>0\quad\textnormal{and}\quad|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{+}<

\displaystyle\>\infty

\displaystyle(\kappa_{+}=

\displaystyle\>0).

•

III
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{c}\leq

\displaystyle\>2\digamma_{0}^{\frac{3}{8}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}

\displaystyle(\kappa_{c}>

\displaystyle\>0\quad\textnormal{and}\quad|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{4}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{c}<

\displaystyle\>\infty

\displaystyle(\kappa_{c}=

\displaystyle\>0).

•

IV
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{16}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}\leq s_{c}<

\displaystyle\>\infty

\displaystyle(|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}>

\displaystyle\digamma_{0}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{16}}\leq s_{c}<

\displaystyle\>\infty

\displaystyle(|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}\leq

\displaystyle\digamma_{0}).

The lemma below shows in particular that Regions I–IV cover the full
r
-interval
(r_{+},r_{c})
for
\kappa_{c}
and
\kappa_{+}
suitably small and we identify intervals contained in two intersecting regions.

Lemma 7.2
.

The following set inclusions hold: for
\digamma_{0}
suitably large and
\kappa_{c}
suitably small, depending on
\gamma
and
q_{0}
, the following set inclusions hold:

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\leq s_{+}\leq 2\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\right\}\subset

\displaystyle\>I\cap II,

(7.7)

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{c}\leq\frac{4}{3}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\}\subset

\displaystyle\>I\cap III\quad

\displaystyle(|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq\digamma_{0}\kappa_{+}),

(7.8)

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{c}\leq\frac{4}{3}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\}\subset

\displaystyle\>II\cap III\quad

\displaystyle(|\widetilde{\omega}|>\digamma_{0}\kappa_{+}),

(7.9)

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}\leq s_{c}\leq 2\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}\right\}\subset

\displaystyle\>III\cap IV,

(7.10)

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{+}\leq\frac{4}{3}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\}\subset

\displaystyle\>IV\cap II\quad

\displaystyle(|\widehat{\omega}|\leq\digamma_{0}\kappa_{c}),

(7.11)

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{+}\leq\frac{4}{3}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\}\subset

\displaystyle\>III\cap II\quad

\displaystyle(|\widehat{\omega}|>\digamma_{0}\kappa_{c}).

(7.12)

Proof.

The inclusions (
7.7
) and (
7.10
) follow immediately. To derive (
7.8
), we apply (
2.11
) with
r_{+}=1
and obtain for
\kappa_{c}
suitably small and
\digamma_{0}
suitably large:

\left\{\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{c}\leq\frac{4}{3}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\}\\
=\left\{\frac{1+r_{c}^{-2}(1-\frac{4}{3}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}})}{\frac{4}{3}(1-r_{c}^{-1})\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}-r_{c}^{-1}}\leq s_{+}\leq\frac{1+r_{c}^{-2}(1-\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}})}{\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}(1-r_{c}^{-1})|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}-r_{c}^{-1}}\right\}\\
\subseteq\left\{\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{+}<\frac{5}{4}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\right\}

(7.13)

For
|\widetilde{\omega}|\leq\digamma_{0}\kappa_{+}
, with
\digamma_{0}
suitably large and
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{+}
, the set on the right-hand side of the inclusion in (
7.13
) is contained in I, because:

\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{12}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\kappa_{+}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\geq\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{16}}(|\widehat{\omega}|+|\kappa_{c}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa_{+}^{-\frac{1}{4}},

so (
7.8
) holds.

For
|\widetilde{\omega}|>\digamma_{0}\kappa_{+}
and
|\widehat{\omega}|>\digamma_{0}\kappa_{c}
or
\kappa_{c}=0
, with
\digamma_{0}
suitably large and
\kappa_{c}
suitably small, we moreover have that:

\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\geq\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{4}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}

and when
\kappa_{c}>0
, also:

\frac{5}{4}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\leq\frac{5}{4}\digamma_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{4}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\leq 2F_{0}^{\frac{3}{8}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1},

so the set on the right-hand side of the inclusion in (
7.13
) is also contained in II. This concludes (
7.9
).

The inclusions (
7.11
) and (
7.12
) follow analogously with the roles of
s_{+}
and
s_{c}
and
|\widetilde{\omega}|
and
|\widehat{\omega}|
interchanged.
∎

7.1.1.
Region I

We first approximate solutions
u
to (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
by purely oscillating exponentials via a standard WKB approximation.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
u
be a solution to (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
. Then, there exist constants
A_{1},A_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
and a suitably small
\delta>0
, such that for
s_{+}>\frac{1}{2}\delta^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}
, we can express:

u(r_{*})=A_{1}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}(1+\varepsilon_{1}(r_{*}))+A_{2}e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}(1+\varepsilon_{2}(r_{*})),

and there exists a uniform constant
C>0
(independent of
\delta
), such that:

\displaystyle|\varepsilon_{i}|(r_{*})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+|\kappa_{+}|)+C|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}s_{+}^{-1},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{i}}{dr_{*}}\right|(r_{*})\leq

\displaystyle\>Cr^{-2}\Omega^{2}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}.

In particular,
|\varepsilon_{i}|(r_{*})\leq C\delta
if
|\widetilde{\omega}|>\digamma_{0}\kappa_{+}
and
\digamma_{0}>1
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we will take
\delta>0
to be suitably small. Let
\zeta(r_{*}):=\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\sqrt{\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\widetilde{V}(r_{*}^{\prime})}\,dr_{*}^{\prime}
. Then

\zeta^{\prime}:=\frac{d\zeta}{dr_{*}}=\sqrt{\widetilde{\omega}^{2}-\widetilde{V}(r_{*})}
and
\zeta^{\prime\prime}=\frac{d^{2}\zeta}{dr_{*}^{2}}
and we obtain:

\frac{d^{2}}{d\zeta^{2}}((\zeta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}u)=-(1-\vartheta)(\zeta^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}u,

(7.14)

with

\vartheta(\zeta):=\frac{1}{2}(\zeta^{\prime})^{-\frac{3}{2}}\frac{d}{dr_{*}}(\zeta^{\prime\prime}\zeta^{\prime-\frac{3}{2}})=-2(\zeta^{\prime})^{-\frac{3}{2}}\frac{d^{2}}{dr_{*}^{2}}(\zeta^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}})

Let
W_{1}(\zeta)=e^{-i\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\zeta}
and
W_{2}(\zeta)=e^{+i\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\zeta}
. Then
W_{i}
are solutions to (
7.14
) with
\vartheta\equiv 0
. Note that
\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})=2\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})
.

We can then apply
[
Olv97
]
[Theorem 6.2.2] to obtain:

u(r_{*}(\zeta))=\widetilde{A}_{1}(\zeta^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-i\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\zeta}(1+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}(\zeta))+\widetilde{A}_{2}(\zeta^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{+i\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\zeta}(1+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}(\zeta)),

with

|\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}|(\zeta)\leq e^{\frac{1}{2}\int^{\infty}_{\zeta(r_{*})}|\vartheta|(\eta)\,d\eta}-1,\quad\frac{1}{2}\left|\frac{d\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}}{d\zeta}\right|(r_{*})\leq e^{\frac{1}{2}\int^{\infty}_{\zeta(r_{*})}|\vartheta|(\eta)\,d\eta}-1.

Note that for
s_{+}\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}
and
\delta>0
suitably small, we can estimate
\zeta^{\prime}\geq\frac{1}{2}|\widetilde{\omega}|
. We can therefore estimate:

\int^{\infty}_{\zeta(r_{*})}|\vartheta|(\eta)\,d\eta=\int^{\infty}_{r_{*}}|\zeta^{\prime}||\vartheta|(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}\leq\int_{1}^{r(r_{*})}|\zeta^{\prime}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left|\frac{d}{dr}((\zeta^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}})^{\prime})\right|\,dr^{\prime}\\
\leq C\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+|\kappa_{+}|+(r(r_{*})-1))\leq C\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+|\kappa_{+}|).

We conclude that

|\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}|(\zeta)\leq C\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+|\kappa_{+}|),\\
\left|\frac{d\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}}{d\zeta}\right|(r_{*})\leq C\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+|\kappa_{+}|).

To conclude the estimates in the proposition, we note that

(\zeta^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}}(r_{*})e^{\mp i\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\zeta(r_{*})}=|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-q\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}s_{+}^{-1}+\kappa_{+}\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}O(s_{+}^{-1})+O(|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-2}s_{+}^{-2}))\\
\times e^{\mp i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}\pm iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}(s_{+})(r_{*}^{\prime})^{-2}\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}

and we rescale
\tilde{A}_{i}
and
\tilde{\varepsilon_{i}}
appropriately to obtain
A_{i}
and
\varepsilon_{i}
.
∎

The estimates in Proposition
7.3
cover Region I, in the case
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}
if
\delta\leq\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{16}}
.

7.1.2.
Region I:
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}

When
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}
, we can improve the approximation in §
7.1.1
by considering hypergeometric functions, which approximate
U_{+}
well in a larger
r
-interval.

Define

\zeta(s_{+}):=2\kappa_{+}s_{+}(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-1}=1-(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-1}.

Note that
\zeta(s_{+})\in(\frac{2\kappa_{+}}{r_{c}-1+2\kappa_{+}},1)
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Assume that
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{+}
and that
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}
. Let
U_{+}
be a solution to (
7.1
). Let
\delta>\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}\widehat{\omega}|
. Then there exist constants
B_{1},B_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
and a uniform constant
C>0
, such that for
s_{+}\geq\delta^{-1}\kappa_{+}^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{3}{4}}
:

(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}}U_{+}(s)=B_{1}(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})(1+\varepsilon_{1}(s_{+}))\\
+B_{2}(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(G_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})+\varepsilon_{2}(s_{+})\right),

with

•

F_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}
and
G_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}
proportional to hypergeometric functions and are precisely defined in Lemma
C.6
,

•

\underline{\sigma}=(\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b})
satisfying

\displaystyle\sigma_{a}=

\displaystyle-q+O(\kappa_{c})+\kappa_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|O(|\widehat{\omega}|),

\displaystyle\sigma_{b}=

\displaystyle\>q+\kappa_{+}^{-1}\widetilde{\omega}+\kappa_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|O(|\widehat{\omega}|),

•

\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
satisfying the estimates:

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{1}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta,

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{2}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta|F_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}|(2\kappa_{+}s_{+}),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{1}}{ds_{+}}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta\frac{1}{s_{+}(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})}

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{2}}{ds_{+}}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta\frac{1}{s_{+}(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})}|F_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}|(2\kappa_{+}s_{+}).

Proof.

Rescale
s_{+}
as follows:
\tilde{s}:=2\kappa_{+}s_{+}
. Then (
7.1
) is equivalent to (
C.30
), if we set:

w:=\kappa_{+}^{-1}\widetilde{\omega}_{+}=\kappa_{+}^{-1}\widetilde{\omega}+O(\kappa_{c}),\quad\mu:=\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell},\quad v=\kappa_{+}^{-1}\nu_{+}

and take

\vartheta(\tilde{s})=\kappa_{+}\kappa_{c}\frac{1}{(1+\tilde{s})^{2}}O_{\infty}(\tilde{s}^{-3})+\kappa_{+}|\widehat{\omega}|\frac{1}{(1+\tilde{s})^{2}}O_{\infty}(\tilde{s}^{-3})+\left(\mu^{2}_{+}-\frac{1}{4}\beta_{\ell}^{2}\right)\frac{1}{(1+\tilde{s})^{2}}O_{\infty}(\tilde{s}^{-2}).

Take

\displaystyle\sigma_{a}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}+\,\textnormal{sign}\,\left(-q+\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-\mu_{+}^{2}+\kappa_{+}^{-1}\nu_{+}+\frac{1}{4}\kappa_{+}^{-2}\widetilde{\omega}_{+}^{2}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{b}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}-\,\textnormal{sign}\,\left(-q+\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-\mu_{+}^{2}+\kappa_{+}^{-1}\nu_{+}+\frac{1}{4}\kappa_{+}^{-2}\widetilde{\omega}_{+}^{2}}.

and use that for
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{+}
:

\displaystyle\mu_{+}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}=

\displaystyle\>\ell(\ell+1)-q^{2}+O(|\widehat{\omega}||\widetilde{\omega}|)+O(\kappa_{c}^{2}),

\displaystyle\kappa_{+}^{-1}\nu_{+}=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{\widetilde{\omega}}{\kappa_{+}}(q+2\widetilde{\omega})+\ell(\ell+1)+O(\kappa_{c}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{\kappa_{+}}q+\ell(\ell+1)-2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\widetilde{\omega}\widehat{\omega}+O(\kappa_{c})

to obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{a}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}+\,\textnormal{sign}\,\left(-q+\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}\right)\sqrt{\left(\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}-q\right)^{2}+O(\kappa_{c})+O(|\widehat{\omega}|)},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>q+O(\kappa_{c})+\kappa_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|O(|\widehat{\omega}|)

\displaystyle\sigma_{b}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}-\,\textnormal{sign}\,\left(-q+\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}\right)\sqrt{\left(\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{+}}{2\kappa_{+}}-q\right)^{2}+O(\kappa_{c})+\frac{|\widetilde{\omega}|}{\kappa_{+}^{-1}}O(|\widehat{\omega}|)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-q+\kappa_{+}^{-1}\widetilde{\omega}+O(\kappa_{c})+\kappa_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|O(|\widehat{\omega}|).

Then we can estimate:

\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta|(\eta)\,d\eta\leq C\kappa_{+}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)\log^{2}(2+\tilde{s})\tilde{s}^{-1}(\tilde{s}+1)^{-2}+C\left(\mu_{+}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\beta_{\ell}^{2}\right)\log^{2}(2+\tilde{s})(\tilde{s}+1)^{-2}\\
\leq C(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)\log^{2}(2+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})s_{+}^{-1}(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-2}+C(\kappa_{c}^{2}+|\widehat{\omega}||\widetilde{\omega}|)\log^{2}(2+\kappa_{+}s_{+})(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-2}

and similarly

\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\log^{2}(2+\eta)\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta|(\eta)\,d\eta\leq C\log\left(2+\frac{1}{2\kappa_{+}s_{+}}\right)(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-2}\\
\times\log^{2}(2+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})\left[(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)s_{+}^{-1}+\kappa_{c}^{2}+|\widehat{\omega}||\widetilde{\omega}|\right].

Suppose that
2\kappa_{+}s_{+}\geq 1
. Then

\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\log^{2}(2+\eta)\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta|(\eta)\,d\eta\leq C\left[(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)s_{+}^{-1}+\kappa_{c}^{2}+|\widehat{\omega}||\widetilde{\omega}|\right]\leq C\delta.

Suppose that
\delta^{-1}\kappa_{+}^{-1}(\kappa_{+}\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}\kappa_{c})^{\frac{3}{4}}\leq s_{+}\leq(2\kappa_{+})^{-1}
. Then

\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta|(\eta)\,d\eta\leq C\delta+C\delta\log(2+\delta(\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}\widehat{\omega}|)^{-1})(\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq C\delta.

We can now apply Proposition
C.7
to obtain the desired estimates.
∎

Proposition
7.4
covers Region I, in the case that
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{+}
and
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}
, provided that
\delta\geq 2\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{16}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}
.

7.1.3.
Region II:
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}

In this section, we derive approximations that are valid when
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}
, and in particular, when
\kappa_{+}=0
. Note that boundedness of
|\widetilde{\omega}|
implies smallness of
\kappa_{+}
in this regime:
\kappa_{+}\leq C\digamma_{0}^{-1}
.

Corollary 7.5
.

Assume that
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{+}
and that
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}
. Then
U_{+}
is a solution to (
7.1
) if and only if

\frac{d^{2}U_{+}}{d\xi^{2}}=\left[-\frac{1}{4}-\sigma\xi^{-1}+\left(\frac{1}{4}\beta_{\ell}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\xi^{-2}+\vartheta(\xi)\right]U_{+},

(7.15)

with

\displaystyle\xi(s_{+})=

\displaystyle-2w_{+}s_{+},\quad\textnormal{where}

\displaystyle w_{+}=

\displaystyle\>\widetilde{\omega}(1+O(\digamma_{0}^{-1})),

\displaystyle\sigma=

\displaystyle\>-q-2\widetilde{\omega}+O(\digamma_{0}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\vartheta(\xi)=

\displaystyle\>\left(\kappa_{+}O(|\widehat{\omega}|)+O(\kappa_{c})\right)\xi^{-2}+w_{+}(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})O(\xi^{-3})+w_{+}^{-1}\kappa_{+}O_{\infty}(\xi).

Furthermore, for
\kappa_{c}+\digamma_{0}^{-1}<\delta
, there exist constants
B_{1},B_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
and a uniform constant
C
, such that we can write:

U_{+}(s_{+})=B_{1}W_{-i\sigma,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(i\xi(s_{+}))(1+\varepsilon_{1}(s_{+}))+B_{2}(M_{-i\sigma,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(i\xi(s_{+}))+\varepsilon_{2}(s_{+})),

with
\varepsilon_{i}(\xi)
, with
i\in\{1,2\}
, satisfying the following bounds for
s_{+}\in(\delta^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{8}}(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c}),\frac{1}{|\widetilde{\omega}|}\xi_{\kappa_{+}})
, with
\xi_{\kappa_{+}}=\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}(\kappa_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}
if
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\xi_{\kappa_{+}}=\infty
if
\kappa_{+}=0
:

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{1}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta,

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{2}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta|W_{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(i\xi(s_{+}))|,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{1}}{ds_{+}}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta|\widetilde{\omega}|,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{2}}{ds_{+}}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta|\widetilde{\omega}||W_{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(i\xi(s_{+}))|.

For
\kappa_{+}=0
and
0<s_{+}<|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}
, we can moreover estimate for
i\in\{1,2\}
:

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{i}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>C|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}s_{+}^{-2},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{i}}{ds_{+}}\right|(s_{+})\leq

\displaystyle\>Cs_{+}^{-2}.

Proof.

We assume that
s_{+}\leq\kappa_{+}^{-1}
and expand:

(1+2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-2}=1-4(\kappa_{+}s_{+})+12(\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{2}+O_{\infty}((\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{3})

and apply the above expansion together with (
7.1
) and (
7.3
) to obtain:

\frac{d^{2}U_{+}}{ds_{+}^{2}}=\Bigg[-w_{+}^{2}+2\left(\nu_{+}-4\kappa_{+}\left(\mu_{+}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)+\kappa_{+}O(|\widehat{\omega}|)+\kappa_{+}O(\kappa_{c})\right)s_{+}^{-1}\\
+\left(\mu_{+}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}+\kappa_{+}O(|\widehat{\omega}|)+\kappa_{+}O(\kappa_{c})\right)s_{+}^{-2}+(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{-3})+\kappa_{+}O_{\infty}(s_{+})\Bigg]U_{+},

with
w_{+}=\widetilde{\omega}+O(\kappa_{+})
. Changing variables from
s_{+}
to
\xi
then results in (
7.15
).

In order to apply Corollary
C.4
, we need to estimate:

\int^{\xi_{0}}_{|\xi|}\log^{2}(2+\eta^{-2})(\eta^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d|\eta|.

Let
\xi_{\kappa_{+}}=\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}(\kappa_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}
if
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\xi_{\kappa_{+}}=\infty
if
\kappa_{+}=0
and let
|\xi|>\delta^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{7}{8}}(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})
. Then, for suitably large
\digamma_{0}
, we have that
|\xi|<|\xi_{\kappa_{+}}|
, and we can estimate:

\int^{\xi_{\kappa_{+}}}_{|\xi|}\log^{2}(2+\eta^{-2})(\eta^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d|\eta|\leq\int^{\xi_{\kappa_{+}}}_{|\xi|}\log^{2}(2+y^{-2})(y^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\\
\times(\kappa_{+}y^{-2}+|w_{+}|(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})y^{-3}+\kappa_{+}|w_{+}|^{-1}y)\,dy\\
\leq C\kappa_{+}\log^{3}(2+\xi^{-2})+C\log^{2}(2+\xi^{-2})|w_{+}|(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})\xi^{-1}+C(\kappa_{+}|w_{+}|^{-1})\xi_{\kappa_{+}}^{2}\leq C\delta+C\digamma_{0}^{-1}.

Now we apply Corollary
C.4
to obtain the desired estimates.

In the case
\kappa_{+}=0
, we obtain for
|\xi|>1
:

\int^{\xi_{0}}_{|\xi|}\log^{2}(2+\eta^{-2})(\eta^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d|\eta|\leq C|w_{+}|\xi^{-2}.

∎

Corollary
7.5
provides estimates in Region II in the case that
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{+}
,
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}
for
\delta>2|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{8}}(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{\frac{3}{4}}F_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}
and
\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa_{+}^{-1/2}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{1/2}>2\digamma_{0}^{\frac{3}{8}}
, the latter which is guaranteed if
\delta>4\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}
. Note that for
\delta>0
to be small, where therefore need either
|\widetilde{\omega}|
or
|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c}
to be sufficiently small (depending
\digamma_{0}
, which is assumed to be large)!

7.1.4.
Region III:
|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}

In Region III, we interchange the roles of
s_{+}
and
s_{c}
,
\widetilde{\omega}
and
-\widehat{\omega}
, and
\kappa_{+}
and
\kappa_{c}
to obtain approximations for
U_{c}
. In this case, there is no need for the assumption
\kappa_{+}\leq\kappa_{c}
.

Corollary 7.6
.

Assume that
|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}
if
\kappa_{c}>0
. Let
U_{c}
be a solution to (
7.2
) if and only if

\frac{d^{2}U_{c}}{d\xi^{2}}=\left[-\frac{1}{4}-\sigma\xi^{-1}+\left(\frac{1}{4}\beta_{\ell}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\xi^{-2}+\vartheta(\xi)\right]U_{c},

(7.16)

with

\displaystyle\xi(s_{c}):=

\displaystyle-2w_{c}s_{c},\quad\textnormal{where}

\displaystyle w_{c}=

\displaystyle\>\widehat{\omega}(1+O(\digamma_{0}^{-1})),

\displaystyle\sigma:=

\displaystyle-q+2\widetilde{\omega}+O(\digamma_{0}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\vartheta(\xi)=

\displaystyle\>\widetilde{\omega}(O(\kappa_{c})+O(|\widehat{\omega}|))\xi^{-2}+w_{c}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c})O(\xi^{-3})+w_{c}^{-1}\kappa_{c}O_{\infty}(\xi).

Furthermore, for
\kappa_{c}+\digamma_{0}^{-1}<\delta
, there exist constants
D_{1},D_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
and a uniform constant
C
, such that we can write:

U_{c}(s_{c})=D_{1}W_{-i\sigma,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(i\xi(s_{c}))(1+\varepsilon_{1}(s_{c}))+D_{2}(M_{-i\sigma,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(i\xi(s_{c}))+\varepsilon_{2}(s_{c})),

with
\varepsilon_{i}(\xi)
, with
i\in\{1,2\}
, satisfying the following bounds for
s_{c}\in(\delta^{-1}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{8}}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}),|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}\xi_{\kappa_{c}})
, with
\xi_{\kappa_{c}}=\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}(\kappa_{c}^{-1}|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}
if
\kappa_{c}>0
and
\xi_{\kappa_{c}}=\infty
if
\kappa_{c}=0
:

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{1}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta,

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{2}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta|W_{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(i\xi(s_{+}))|,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{1}}{ds_{c}}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta|\widetilde{\omega}|,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{2}}{ds_{c}}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta|\widetilde{\omega}||W_{\sigma,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(i\xi(s_{c}))|.

For
\kappa_{c}=0
and
0<s_{c}<|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}
, we can moreover estimate for
i\in\{1,2\}
:

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{i}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>C|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}s_{c}^{-2},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{i}}{ds_{c}}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>Cs_{c}^{-2}.

Proof.

We repeat the proof of Corollary
7.6
with
s_{c}
taking on the role of
s_{+}
.
∎

Corollary
7.6
provides estimates in Region III in the case that:
|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}
for
\delta>2|\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{8}}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c})^{\frac{3}{4}}F_{0}^{\frac{1}{3}}
and
\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}\kappa_{c}^{-1/2}|\widehat{\omega}|^{1/2}>2\digamma_{0}^{\frac{3}{8}}
, the latter which is guaranteed if
\delta>4\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}
. Note that in this case, the above conditions are compatible with smallness of
\delta
if
|\widehat{\omega}|
is small or if
|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}
is small.

7.1.5.
Region IV

In this region, we approximate
u
with exponentials, as in Region I.

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
u
be a solution to (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
. Then, there exist constants
A_{1},A_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
and a suitably small
\delta>\kappa_{c}
, such that for
s_{c}>\frac{1}{2}\delta^{-1}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}
, we can express:

u(r_{*})=E_{1}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}(1+\varepsilon_{1}(r_{*}))+E_{2}e^{-i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}(1+\varepsilon_{2}(r_{*})),

and there exists a uniform constant
C>0
(independent of
\delta
), such that:

\displaystyle|\varepsilon_{i}|(r_{*})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\widehat{\omega}^{-2}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(|\widehat{\omega}|+|\kappa_{c}|)+C|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}s_{c}^{-1},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{i}}{dr_{*}}\right|(r_{*})\leq

\displaystyle\>Cr^{-2}\Omega^{2}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}.

In particular,
|\varepsilon_{i}|(r_{*})\leq C\delta
if
|\widehat{\omega}|>\digamma_{0}\kappa_{c}
and
\digamma_{0}>1
.

Proof.

We repeat the proof of Proposition
7.3
, with
s_{+}
replaced by
s_{c}
and
-\widehat{\omega}
taking the role of
\widetilde{\omega}
.
∎

The estimates in Proposition
7.7
cover Region I, in the case
|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}
if
\delta\leq\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{16}}
.

7.1.6.
Region IV:
|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}

An analogue of Proposition
7.4
is valid when
s_{c}
takes on the role of
s_{+}
,
\kappa_{c}
takes on the role of
\kappa_{+}
,
\mu_{+}
takes on the role of
\mu_{c}
and
-\widehat{\omega}
takes on the role of
\widetilde{\omega}
. In this case, there is no need for the assumption
\kappa_{+}\leq\kappa_{c}
.

Proposition 7.8
.

Assume that
|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}
. Let
U_{c}
be a solution to (
7.2
). Then there exist constants
D_{1},D_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
and a uniform constant
C>0
, such that for
\delta>\kappa_{c}
and
s_{c}>\delta^{-1}
:

(2\kappa_{c}s_{c})^{-\frac{1}{2}}U_{c}(s_{c})=D_{1}(2\kappa_{c}s_{c})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(2\kappa_{c}s_{c})(1+\varepsilon_{1}(s_{c}))+D_{2}\left((2\kappa_{c}s_{c})^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(2\kappa_{c}s_{c})+\varepsilon_{2}(s_{c})\right),

with
F_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}
and
G_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}
defined in Lemma
C.6
,
\underline{\sigma}=(\sigma_{a},\sigma_{b})
satisfying

\displaystyle\sigma_{a}=

\displaystyle\>q+O(\kappa_{c})

\displaystyle\sigma_{b}=

\displaystyle-q-\kappa_{+}^{-1}\widehat{\omega}+O(\kappa_{c})

and

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{1}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta,

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{2}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta|F_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}|(2\kappa_{c}s_{c}),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{1}}{ds_{c}}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta s_{c}^{-1}(s_{c}+1)^{-1}

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{2}}{ds_{c}}\right|(s_{c})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\delta s_{c}^{-1}(s_{c}+1)^{-1}|F_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}|(2\kappa_{c}s_{c}).

Proof.

In this case
\tilde{s}=2\kappa_{c}s_{c}
and:

\vartheta(\tilde{s})=(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)\frac{1}{(1+\tilde{s})^{2}}O_{\infty}(\tilde{s}^{-2})+\frac{\kappa_{c}}{(1+\tilde{s})^{2}}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c})O_{\infty}(\tilde{s}^{-3}).

We then obtain:

\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}y\log^{2}(2+y)|\vartheta(y)|\,dy\leq C\kappa_{c}\log^{2}(2+\tilde{s})(1+\tilde{s}^{-1})(1+\tilde{s})^{-2}\leq C\log^{2}(2+2\kappa_{c}s_{c})(\kappa_{c}+s_{c}^{-1})(1+2\kappa_{c}s_{c})^{-2}.

The rest of the proof proceeds as the proof of Proposition
7.4
.
∎

Proposition
7.8
covers Region IV, in the case that
|\widehat{\omega}|\kappa_{c}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}
, provided that
\delta\geq 2\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{16}}
.

7.2.
Matching asymptotic estimates: small
|\widetilde{\omega}|

We will consider the following
hierarchy of smallness of constants
:

\kappa_{c}\ll\digamma_{0}^{-1}\ll\delta\ll 1,

with moreover
\kappa_{+}\geq\kappa_{c}
. In order for all the constants
\delta
in §
7.1
to be small, we need either
|\widehat{\omega}|
or
|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}
to be small. Since we are only assuming that
|\widetilde{\omega}|
is small in the present section, this means that we need the additional assumption
\kappa_{+}\ll 1
.

Proposition 7.9
.

Let
(\widehat{\omega},\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
. Then the event-horizon-normalized solution
u_{+}
to (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
satisfies the estimates in §§
7.1.1
–§§
7.1.5
, with coefficients
A_{i}
,
B_{i}
,
D_{i}
,
E_{i}
that satisfy the following upper bounds: there exists a constant
C=C(q_{0},q_{1},L_{0},\beta)>0
such that

\displaystyle|A_{1}|=

\displaystyle\>1,\quad A_{2}=0,

(7.17)

\displaystyle|B_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C,\quad B_{2}=0,

(7.18)

\displaystyle|D_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{+}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}},

(7.19)

\displaystyle|D_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{+}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{+}|+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-1})),

(7.20)

\displaystyle|E_{i}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{+}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{+}|+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-1}))\quad i\in\{1,2\}.

(7.21)

Furthermore, for
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}
,
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{+}
and both
\kappa_{+}
and
\gamma
suitably small, there exists a constant

C=C(q_{0},q_{\rm max},L_{0},\gamma)>0
such that

|E_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{C}(\kappa_{+}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{+}|+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-1})).

(7.22)

Proof.

Recall the boundary condition:

\lim_{r_{*}\to\infty}e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u_{+}(r_{*})=1.

Estimates for
A_{i}
:
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}

By Proposition
7.3
and the boundary conditions on
u_{+}
, we immediately obtain
A_{1}=1
and
A_{2}=0
.

Estimates for
B_{i}
:
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}

Let
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}
. Then we apply Proposition
7.4
to obtain for
s_{+}\geq\delta^{-1}\kappa_{+}^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{3}{4}}
:

(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}}(s_{+}+1)(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}u_{+}(r_{*}(s_{+}))=B_{1}(2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))(1+\varepsilon_{1}(s_{+}))\\
+B_{2}\left((2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))+\varepsilon_{2}(s_{+})\right),

with
\mu=\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}
.

Note that by Lemma
2.2
:

(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}}(s_{+}+1)(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}=1+\kappa_{+}^{-1}O(s_{+}^{-1}).

Then, by (
2.4
) and (
C.19
):

(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})=(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{\frac{1}{2}(-1-i\frac{\widetilde{\omega}}{\kappa_{+}})}+\kappa_{+}^{-1}O(s_{+}^{-1})\\
=(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{i\frac{\widetilde{\omega}}{2\kappa_{+}}}+\kappa_{+}^{-1}O(s_{+}^{-1})\\
=e^{i\frac{\widetilde{\omega}}{2\kappa_{+}}\log(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})}+\kappa_{+}^{-1}O(s_{+}^{-1})\\
=e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+i\frac{\widetilde{\omega}}{\kappa_{+}}O_{\infty}((\kappa_{+}^{-1}s_{+}^{-1})^{0})}+\kappa_{+}^{-1}O(s_{+}^{-1}).

Since
G_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}
can be written as a linear combination of
F_{\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}
and
F_{-\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}
and
(2\kappa_{+}s_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{-\underline{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}
features the factor
e^{+i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}
, we can conclude can conclude that
B_{2}=0
and
|B_{1}|=1
.

Estimates for
B_{i}
:
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}

In order to obtain estimates for
B_{i}
, we will consider the intersection between Region I and Region II, where both Proposition
7.3
and Corollary
7.5
apply. Let
\frac{1}{2}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}<s_{+}<2\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}
. Write
\mu=\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}
. Then by (
2.4
) and (
C.6
):

W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi(s_{+}))=e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\xi(s_{+}))\frac{\pi}{2}\sigma}e^{-i\frac{\xi(s_{+})}{2}}|\xi|^{-i\sigma}(1+O(|\xi|^{-1}))\\
=e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\frac{\pi}{2}\sigma}e^{+iws_{+}^{-1}}|2w|^{-i\sigma}2s_{+}^{-i\sigma}(1+|\widetilde{\omega}|O(|s_{+}|^{-1}))\\
=e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\frac{\pi}{2}\sigma}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}(1+O(\digamma_{0}^{-1})+O_{\infty}(\kappa_{+}s_{+}))}|2w|^{-i\sigma}2s_{+}^{-i\sigma}(1+|\widetilde{\omega}|O(|s_{+}|^{-1})).

Since
M_{-i\sigma,\mu}
can be written as a linear combination of
W_{-i\sigma,\mu}
and
W_{+i\sigma,\mu}
, it will feature the factor
e^{+i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}}
, so we can conclude that
B_{2}=0
and
|B_{1}|=e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\frac{\pi}{2}\sigma}|A_{1}|=e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\frac{\pi}{2}\sigma}
.

Estimates for
D_{i}
:
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}

We will estimate
D_{i}
in terms of
B_{i}
by exploiting the intersection of Regions I and III in the case
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}
. This step involves matching asymptotic expressions of hypergeometric functions to asymptotic expressions of Whittaker functions.

Let
\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{c}\leq\frac{4}{3}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}
, which is contained in
I\cap III
by (
7.8
). Then
2\kappa_{+}s_{+}\ll 1
, since
|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}\ll 1
and
s_{c}\ll 1
. We can therefore apply Proposition
7.4
, (
C.21
), (
C.22
) and (
C.19
) to obtain for
\mu=2\beta_{\ell}\neq 0
:

U_{+}(s_{+})=B_{1}F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))\\
=B_{1}\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\Bigg[\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b})}G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))\\
+\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}G_{\underline{\sigma},-\mu}(2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0}))\Bigg]\\
=B_{1}\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\Bigg[\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b})}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}s_{+}^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}\\
+\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}s_{+}^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0}))\Bigg]\\
=B_{1}\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\Bigg[\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b})}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}s_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu}\\
+\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}s_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu}(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0}))\Bigg],

where we used that
s_{c}=s_{+}^{-1}(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0}))
to arrive at the final step.

We can express the above expressions for
U_{+}(s_{+})
in terms of Whittaker functions
M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi(s_{c}))
and
M_{-i\sigma,-\mu}(i\xi(s_{c}))
and use the smallness of
|\xi(s_{c})|=|2w_{c}s_{c}|
together with (
C.8
) to obtain:

U_{c}(s_{c})=\frac{s_{c}+1}{s_{+}+1}U_{+}(s_{+}(s_{c}))=B_{1}\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\\
\times\Bigg[\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b})}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,\widehat{\omega}\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)}M_{-i\sigma,-\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})\\
+\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,\widehat{\omega}\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0}))\Bigg].

(7.23)

We apply (
C.9
)–(
C.11
) again to express
M_{-i\sigma,-\mu}
in terms of
W_{-i\sigma,\mu}
and
M_{-i\sigma,\mu}
and obtain for
\mu\neq 0
:

U_{c}(s_{c})=B_{1}\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(2\mu)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}\\
\times(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)}W_{\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0}))\\
+B_{1}\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}\\
\times\Bigg[1-(2\kappa_{+})^{2\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{2\mu}e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})i\pi\mu)}\frac{\Gamma^{2}(-2\mu)}{\Gamma^{2}(2\mu)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}\Bigg]\\
\times M_{\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0})).

When
\mu=0
, we instead apply (
C.23
) to obtain:

U_{+}(s_{+})=B_{1}F_{\underline{\sigma},0}(2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))\\
=-B_{1}\frac{\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}G_{0,\sigma}(2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))\Bigg[\log(2\kappa_{+}(s_{+}+1))(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0}))\\
+\left(2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}\right)\Bigg]\\
=-B_{1}\frac{\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg[\log(2\kappa_{+}s_{c}^{-1})(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0}))\\
+\left(2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}\right)\Bigg]

Therefore:

U_{c}(s_{c})=\frac{s_{c}+1}{s_{+}+1}U_{+}(s_{+}(s_{c}))=B_{1}\frac{\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg[(\log(s_{c})+\log(2\kappa_{+})^{-1}(1+\delta O_{\infty}(s_{+}^{0}))\\
-\left(2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}\right)\Bigg].

On the other hand, we can apply (
C.8
) and (
C.12
) to obtain:

\displaystyle M_{-i\sigma,0}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})=

\displaystyle\>e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})i\frac{\pi}{4}}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\delta O(s_{c}^{0})),

\displaystyle W_{-i\sigma,0}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})i\frac{\pi}{4}}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[\log(-2i\widehat{\omega}s_{c})+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}-i\sigma)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-i\sigma)}+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\delta O(s_{c}^{0})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})i\frac{\pi}{4}}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg[\log(s_{c})+\log|2\widehat{\omega}|-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}-i\sigma)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-i\sigma)}

\displaystyle+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\delta O(s_{c}^{0})\Bigg].

Hence,

U_{c}(s_{c})=-B_{1}\frac{\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}e^{+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})i\frac{\pi}{4}}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma\right)(1+\delta O(s_{c}^{0}))W_{-i\sigma,0}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})\\
+B_{1}\frac{\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}e^{+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})i\frac{\pi}{4}}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\times\Bigg[\log(2\kappa_{+})^{-1}(1+\delta O(s_{c}^{0}))-\log|2\widehat{\omega}|+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}-4\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}\Bigg]\\
\times M_{-i\sigma,0}(-2iws_{c})

Since Corollary
7.6
also applies for the
s_{+}
-values under consideration, we conclude that for
\mu\neq 0
:

\displaystyle D_{1}=

\displaystyle\>B_{1}\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(2\mu)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)}(1+O(\delta)),

\displaystyle D_{2}=

\displaystyle\>B_{1}\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\Bigg[1-(2\kappa_{+})^{2\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{2\mu}e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})i\pi\mu}\frac{\Gamma^{2}(-2\mu)}{\Gamma^{2}(2\mu)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O(\delta)\Bigg]\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle D_{2}=

\displaystyle\>B_{1}\frac{\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}e^{+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})i\frac{\pi}{4}}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}(2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\Bigg[\log(2\kappa_{+})^{-1}(1+\delta O(s_{c}^{0}))-\log|2\widehat{\omega}|+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}

\displaystyle-4\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}+O(\delta)\Bigg]\quad(\mu=0),

where in the expression for
D_{1}
, we used that
\lim_{\mu\to 0}\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(2\mu)}=-1
.

Since all the
\Gamma
-factors in the expressions for
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
can be bounded uniformly when
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\digamma_{0}
, we obtain
|D_{1}|\leq C|\kappa_{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}+\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}
and
|D_{2}|\leq C|\kappa_{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}
when
\mu\neq 0
and
|D_{2}|\leq C|\kappa_{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\log|\kappa_{+}|^{-1}
when
\mu=0
.

When
\mu\notin i(0,\infty)
, we also obtain a lower bound:
|D_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{C}|\kappa_{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}
when
\mu\neq 0
and
|D_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{C}|\kappa_{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\log\kappa_{+}^{-1}
when
\mu=0
.

To obtain a lower bound for
|D_{2}|
when
\mu\in i(0,\infty)
, we need to keep more careful track of the ratios of
\Gamma
-factors. Let:

g(\widetilde{\omega},q,\mu,\kappa_{+}):=(2\kappa_{+})^{2\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{2\mu}e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})i\pi\mu}\\
\times\frac{\Gamma^{2}(-2\mu)}{\Gamma^{2}(2\mu)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)}.

Then we conclude that
|D_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{C}|\kappa_{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}}
if
|1-g|\geq b
for some constant
b>0
.

Note that for
\mu\in i(0,\infty)
:

|g|(\widetilde{\omega},q,\mu,\kappa_{+})=e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu}\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu-\sigma))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu+\sigma))}\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu-\sigma_{a}))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu+\sigma_{a}))}\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu-\sigma_{b}))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu+\sigma_{b}))}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We will use that:
\widehat{\omega}=q+O(\widetilde{\omega})
,
\sigma=q+O(\kappa_{c})
,
\sigma_{a}=-q+O(\kappa_{c})+\kappa_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|O(|\widehat{\omega}|)
and
\sigma_{b}=q+\kappa_{+}^{-1}\widetilde{\omega}+O(\kappa_{c})+\kappa_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|O(|\widehat{\omega}|)
to estimate further:

|g|(\widetilde{\omega},q,\mu,\kappa_{+})=e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu}\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu-q))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu+q))}\right)\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu-\sigma_{b}))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu+\sigma_{b}))}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+O(\kappa_{c})+O(\widehat{\omega}).

By Lemma
C.9
, we have that :

e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu}\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu+q))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu-q))}\right)\in\begin{cases}(e^{-3\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu},e^{-\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu})\quad q<0,\\
(e^{\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu},e^{3\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu})\quad q>0.\end{cases}

Furthermore,
\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu-\sigma_{b}))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu+\sigma_{b}))}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\in(e^{-\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu},e^{\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu})
, so
|g|
must be bounded away from
1
and we obtain the desired estimate for
|1-g|
, with a constant
B
that depends on
\textnormal{Im}\,\mu
and
\digamma_{0}
.

The non-vanishing of the leading-order term in
D_{2}
is closely related to the properties of the stationary
(\widehat{\omega}=0)
solutions in Reissner–Nordström; see
[
Gaj26
]
[Proposition 3.1].

Estimates for
D_{i}
:
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}

We will estimate
D_{i}
in terms of
B_{i}
by exploiting the intersection of Regions II and III in the case
|\widetilde{\omega}|\kappa_{+}^{-1}>\digamma_{0}
. This step involves matching asymptotic expressions of Whittaker functions to asymptotic expressions of different Whittaker functions.

Let
\frac{3}{4}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq s_{c}\leq\frac{4}{3}\digamma_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{4}}
, which is a subset of
II\cap III
by (
7.9
). Then we apply Corollary
7.5
together with (
C.9
)–(
C.11
) to obtain for
\mu\neq 0
:

U_{+}(s_{+})=B_{1}W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{+}s_{+})(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))=B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right)}(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{+}s_{+})\\
+B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))M_{-i\sigma,-\mu}(-2iw_{+}s_{+})\\
=B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right)}(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}|2\widetilde{\omega}s_{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}\\
+B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)}|2\widetilde{\omega}s_{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}\\
=B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right)}(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}s_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu}\\
+B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}s_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu}.

We can express the above expressions for
U_{+}(s_{+})
in terms of Whittaker functions
M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi(s_{c}))
and
M_{-i\sigma,-\mu}(i\xi(s_{c}))
and use the smallness of
|\xi(s_{c})|
, together with the expansions in Lemma
C.3
to obtain:

U_{c}(s_{c})=\frac{s_{c}+1}{s_{+}+1}U_{+}(s_{+}(s_{c}))\\
=B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right)}(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0})e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\frac{i\pi}{4}}e^{-(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\frac{i\pi}{2}\mu}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu}M_{-i\sigma,-\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})\\
+B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\frac{i\pi}{4}}e^{+(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\frac{i\pi}{2}\mu}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu}M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c}).

(7.24)

Now observe that (
7.24
) has the same form as (
7.23
), with the following substitutions:

\displaystyle B_{1}\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\mapsto

\displaystyle\>B_{1},

\displaystyle e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{4}}\mapsto

\displaystyle\>e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\frac{i\pi}{4}},

\displaystyle e^{\pm\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{2}\mu}\mapsto

\displaystyle\>e^{\pm(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\frac{i\pi}{2}\mu},

\displaystyle|2\kappa_{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}\pm\mu}\mapsto

\displaystyle\>|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}\pm\mu},

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b}\right)\mapsto

\displaystyle\>\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right),

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a}\right)\mapsto

\displaystyle\>1.

Now let
\mu=0
. Then we apply instead (
C.12
) to obtain:

U_{+}(s_{+})=B_{1}W_{-i\sigma,0}(-2iw_{+}s_{+})(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))\\
=-\frac{B_{1}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}M_{-i\sigma,0}(-2iw_{+}s_{+})\left(\log(-2\widetilde{\omega}s_{+})(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}\right)\\
=\frac{B_{1}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega})\frac{i\pi}{4}}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(-\log(-(2\widetilde{\omega})^{-1})+\log(s_{c})(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}-2\gamma_{\rm Euler}\right)

Hence,

U_{c}(s_{c})=-B_{1}e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))i\frac{\pi}{4}}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\delta O(s_{+}^{0}))W_{-i\sigma,0}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})\\
-B_{1}e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))i\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}\left[\log(-(2\widetilde{\omega})^{-1})-\log(2\widehat{\omega})+2\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}+4\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\delta O(s_{+}^{0})\right]\\
\times M_{-i\sigma,0}(-2iw_{c}s_{c}).

Repeating the matched asymptotics argument in the hypergeometric case for
\mu\neq 0
with the above substitutions and applying the above expression for
U_{c}(s_{c})
in the
\mu\neq 0
case, we are left with:

\displaystyle D_{1}=

\displaystyle\>B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(2\mu)}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu}e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))i\frac{\pi}{4}}e^{-(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\frac{i\pi}{2}\mu}(1+O(\delta)),

\displaystyle D_{2}=
