\displaystyle\>B_{1}\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu}e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))i\frac{\pi}{4}}e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\frac{i\pi}{2}\mu}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\Bigg[1-|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{2\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{2\mu}e^{-(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))i\pi\mu}\frac{\Gamma^{2}(-2\mu)}{\Gamma^{2}(2\mu)}\frac{\Gamma^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}{\Gamma^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right)}+O(\delta)\Bigg]\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle D_{2}=

\displaystyle\>-B_{1}e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))i\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{|2\widehat{\omega}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}\Bigg[\log(-(2\widetilde{\omega})^{-1})-\log(2\widehat{\omega})

\displaystyle\>+2\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}+4\gamma_{\rm Euler}+O(\delta)\Bigg]\quad(\mu=0),

where we used that
\lim_{\mu\to 0}\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(2\mu)}=-1
.

Since all the gamma factors in the expressions for
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
can be bounded uniformly, we obtain
|D_{1}|\leq C|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}+\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}
and
|D_{2}|\leq C|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}
when
\mu\neq 0
and
|D_{2}|\leq C|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\log|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}
when
\mu=0
.

When
\mu\notin i(0,\infty)
, we also obtain a lower bound:
|D_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{C}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}
when
\mu\neq 0
and
|D_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{C}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\log|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}
when
\mu=0
.

To obtain a lower bound for
|D_{2}|
when
\mu\in i(0,\infty)
, we need to keep more careful track of the ratios of gamma factors, as in the case of matched asymptotics with hypergeometric functions.

Let
\mu\in i(0,\infty)
and define in this case:

g(\widetilde{\omega},\sigma,\mu):=|2\widetilde{\omega}|^{2\mu}|2\widehat{\omega}|^{2\mu}e^{-(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))i\pi\mu}\frac{\Gamma^{2}(-2\mu)}{\Gamma^{2}(2\mu)}\frac{\Gamma^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma\right)}{\Gamma^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma\right)}.

Then, by the fact that:
\widehat{\omega}=-q+O(\widetilde{\omega})
and
\sigma=q+O(\kappa_{c})
, we obtain:

|g|(\widetilde{\omega},q,\mu,\kappa_{+})=e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu}\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu-\sigma))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu+\sigma))}\\
=e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu}\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu+q))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu-q))}+O(|\widetilde{\omega}|).

Furthermore, by Lemma
C.9
:

e^{(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu}\frac{\cosh(\pi(\mu+q))}{\cosh(\pi(\mu-q))}\in\begin{cases}(e^{(1+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu},e^{(2+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu})\quad(q>0),\\
(e^{-(2+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu},e^{-(1+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widetilde{\omega}))\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\mu})\quad(q<0)\end{cases}

We conclude that
|1-g|\geq b
for some constant
b>0
that depends on
q
,
\mu
and
\widetilde{\omega}
.

Estimates for
E_{i}
:

We will match the Whittaker asymptotics in Region III to the complex exponentials in Region IV.

By Lemma
C.3
, we have that:

u(r_{*})=(s_{c}+1)^{-1}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}U_{c}(s_{c}(r_{*}))\\
=(s_{c}+1)^{-1}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}[D_{1}W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})+D_{2}M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})]\\
=(s_{c}+1)^{-1}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg[\left(D_{1}+D_{2}\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu-i\sigma)}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\pi(\sigma-i\mu-\frac{1}{2})}\right)W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})\\
+D_{2}\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})}W_{+i\sigma,\mu}(-2iw_{c}s_{c})\Bigg].

Let
\frac{\frac{1}{2}(1+r_{c}^{-2})\kappa_{c}^{-\frac{1}{4}}+r_{c}^{-1}}{(1-r_{c}^{-1})+\frac{1}{2}r_{c}^{-1}\kappa_{c}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\leq s_{c}\leq\frac{2(1+r_{c}^{-2})\kappa_{c}^{-\frac{1}{4}}+r_{c}^{-1}}{(1-r_{c}^{-1})+2r_{c}^{-1}\kappa_{c}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}
, which corresponds to a subset of
III\cap IV
by (
7.10
). Then
s_{c}\gg 1
,
|\xi(s_{c})|\gg 1
and
\kappa_{c}s_{c}\ll 1
and by (
2.6
) and by Lemma
2.2
:

\displaystyle(s_{c}+1)^{2}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}=

\displaystyle\>1+O(\kappa_{c}^{\frac{3}{4}})+O(s_{c}^{-1}),

\displaystyle r_{*}(s_{c})=

\displaystyle\>s_{c}(1+\kappa_{c}^{\frac{3}{4}}O(s_{c}^{0}))+\log s_{c}O(s_{c}^{0})

so we can apply the large
|\xi|
Whittaker asymptotics in Lemma
C.3
to write:

u(r_{*})=\left(D_{1}+D_{2}\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu-i\sigma)}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\pi(\sigma-i\mu-\frac{1}{2})}\right)e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}(1+\delta O(s_{c}^{0}))\\
+D_{2}\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})}e^{-i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}(1+\delta O(s_{c}^{0})).

We conclude that Proposition
7.7
holds with:

\displaystyle E_{1}=

\displaystyle\>(D_{1}+D_{2}\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu-i\sigma)}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})\pi(\sigma-i\mu-\frac{1}{2})}+O(\delta),

\displaystyle E_{2}=

\displaystyle\>D_{2}\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\widehat{\omega})}(1+O(\delta)).

The upper and lower bounds for
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
therefore follow immediately from the upper and lower bounds for
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
above.
∎

Proposition 7.10
.

(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
. Then the infinity-normalized solution

) with

satisfies the estimates in §§

–§§

with coefficients

,

such that

\displaystyle|E_{1}|=

\displaystyle\>1,\quad E_{2}=0,

(7.25)

\displaystyle|D_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C,\quad D_{2}=0,

\displaystyle|B_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{+}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}},

(7.27)

\displaystyle|B_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{+}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{+}|+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-1})),

(7.28)

\displaystyle|A_{i}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}(\kappa_{+}+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{+}|+|\widetilde{\omega}|)^{-1}))\quad i\in\{1,2\}.

(7.29)

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of the upper bound estimates in Proposition

, but we start at
r=r_{c}
. In addition, we apply need to apply the uniform estimates on hypergeometric functions from Corollary

to arrive at estimates for

and

and

.
∎

7.3.
Matching asymptotic estimates: small
|\widehat{\omega}|

We will consider the following hierarchy of smallness:

\kappa_{c}\ll\digamma_{0}^{-1}\ll\delta\ll 1.

We will also assume that
\kappa_{+}\geq\kappa_{c}
. Note that we do not need to impose a smallness condition on
\kappa_{+}
when
|\widehat{\omega}|
is small to guarantee smallness of the constants
\delta
in §
7.1
.

We can state analogues of the propositions in §
7.3
in the case, including lower bounds when
\kappa_{c}
and
|\widehat{\omega}|
are suitably small. We can simply repeat the arguments in §
7.3
with the following exchanges of variables and constants:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow-\widehat{\omega},

\displaystyle\kappa_{c}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\kappa_{+},

\displaystyle s_{c}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow s_{+},

\displaystyle r_{*}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow-r_{*}.

Proposition 7.11
.

Let
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\infty}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
. Then the infinity-normalized solution
u_{\infty}
to (

) with
H\equiv 0
satisfies the estimates in §§
7.1.1
–§§
7.1.5
, and with coefficients
A_{i}
in Region I,
B_{i}
in Region I and II,
D_{i}
in Region III,
E_{i}
in Region IV that satisfy the following upper bounds: there exists a constant

such that

\displaystyle|E_{1}|=

\displaystyle\>1,\quad E_{2}=0,

(7.30)

\displaystyle|D_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C,\quad D_{2}=0,

(7.31)

\displaystyle|B_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}},

(7.32)

\displaystyle|B_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{c}|+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{-1})),

(7.33)

\displaystyle|A_{i}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{c}|+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{-1}))\quad i\in\{1,2\}.

(7.34)

Furthermore, for
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\infty}

suitably small, there exists a constant

|A_{2}|\geq\frac{1}{C}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{c}|+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{-1})).

(7.35)

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
. Then the event-horizon-normalized solution
u_{+}
to (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
satisfies the estimates in §§
7.1.1
–§§
7.1.5
with coefficients
A_{i}
,
B_{i}
,
D_{i}
,
E_{i}
that satisfy the following upper bounds: there exists a constant
C=C(q_{0},q_{\rm max},L_{0},\beta)>0
such that

\displaystyle|A_{1}|=

\displaystyle\>1,\quad A_{2}=0,

(7.36)

\displaystyle|B_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C,\quad B_{2}=0,

(7.37)

\displaystyle|D_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}},

(7.38)

\displaystyle|D_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{c}|+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{-1})),

(7.39)

\displaystyle|E_{i}|\leq

\displaystyle\>C|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}(\kappa_{c}+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\kappa_{c}|+|\widehat{\omega}|)^{-1}))\quad i\in\{1,2\}.

(7.40)

7.4.
Wronskian estimates

In this section, we will combine the lower-bound estimates in Propositions
7.9
,
7.10
,
7.11
and
7.12
together with appropriate Wronskian estimates in the frequency regime
\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
that follow from an application of the integral transformations introduced in
[
TdC20
]
in the extremal Kerr setting.

In the lemma below, we show that for non-superradiant frequencies we can immediately obtain an estimate for
|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}
by considering
j^{T}[u_{\infty}]
.

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
(\widehat{\omega},\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
and assume that
\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}>0
. Then:

|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma}}.

(7.41)

Proof.

We can write:

\displaystyle u_{+}(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>{\alpha}_{1,+}u_{\infty}(r_{*})+{\alpha}_{2,+}\overline{u}_{\infty}(r_{*}).

(7.42)

Hence,

-\mathfrak{W}=\mathcal{W}(u_{\infty},u_{+})\\
={\alpha}_{1,+}u_{\infty}(\infty)\frac{du_{\infty}}{dr_{*}}(\infty)+{\alpha}_{2,+}u_{\infty}(\infty)\frac{d\overline{u_{\infty}}}{dr_{*}}(\infty)-({\alpha}_{1,+}u_{\infty}(\infty)+{\alpha}_{2,+}\overline{u}_{\infty}(\infty))\frac{du_{\infty}}{dr_{*}}(\infty)\\
={\alpha}_{2,+}\left(u_{\infty}\frac{d\overline{u_{\infty}}}{dr_{*}}-\overline{u}_{+}\frac{du_{\infty}}{dr_{*}}\right)(\infty)=-2i\widehat{\omega}{\alpha}_{2,+}.

(7.43)

Furthermore, by considering
j^{K}[u_{+}]:=-\widetilde{\omega}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu_{+}^{\prime}\overline{u}_{+})
and using that
\frac{d}{dr_{*}}j^{K}[u_{+}]=0
, we obtain:

\widetilde{\omega}^{2}=\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u_{+}|^{2}(-\infty)=\widetilde{\omega}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu_{+}^{\prime}\overline{u}_{+})(-\infty)=\widehat{\omega}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu_{+}^{\prime}\overline{u}_{+})(\infty)\\
=-\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}\textnormal{Re}\,(({\alpha}_{1,+}-{\alpha}_{2,+})(\overline{{\alpha}_{1,+}}+\overline{{\alpha}_{2,+}}))=\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}(|{\alpha}_{2,+}|^{2}-|{\alpha}_{1,+}|^{2})\\
\leq\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}|{\alpha}_{2,+}|^{2}.

Hence,

|\mathfrak{W}|^{2}=4\widehat{\omega}^{2}|{\alpha}_{2,+}|^{2}\geq\widetilde{\omega}\widehat{\omega}\geq\gamma.\qed

We now consider superradiant frequencies in
\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
, which satisfy
\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}<0
. We first restrict to the case
\kappa_{+}=\kappa_{c}=0
. We obtain an upper bound estimate for
|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}
by applying the arguments in
[
TdC20
]
with some slight modifications.

Theorem 7.14
.

Let
\kappa_{c}=\kappa_{+}=0
and
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
. Assume that
\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}<0
. Then there exists a constant
K=K(\gamma,L_{0})>0
such that:

|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\leq K.

(7.44)

Proof.

In the case of (
5.13
) with
\Lambda=0
and
|Q|=M=1
, the ODE for
R=r^{-1}u
can be written as follows:

\frac{d}{dr}\left((r-1)^{2}\frac{d}{dr}R\right)-\left[\frac{(\beta-\alpha(r-1)-\gamma(r-1)^{2})^{2}}{(r-1)^{2}}+L\right]R=r\Omega^{-2}H,

(7.45)

with

\displaystyle\alpha=

\displaystyle-i(\omega+\widetilde{\omega})=-2i\left(\omega-\frac{q}{2}\right),

\displaystyle\beta=

\displaystyle\>i\widetilde{\omega},

\displaystyle\gamma=

\displaystyle-i\omega,

\displaystyle L=

\displaystyle\>\ell(\ell+1).

Note that
\gamma
here is unrelated to the
\gamma
appearing in the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
. Equation (
7.45
) is an inhomogeneous doubly-confluent Heun equation; see for example
[
NIS
]
[§31.12].

In the case of the radial ODE on extremal Kerr with
M=1
, on the other hand,
R
also satisfies the form (
7.45
), but with parameters:

\displaystyle\alpha_{\rm Kerr}=

\displaystyle-2i\omega,

\displaystyle\beta_{\rm Kerr}=

\displaystyle\>2i\widetilde{\omega},

\displaystyle\gamma_{\rm Kerr}=

\displaystyle-i\omega,

\displaystyle L_{\rm Kerr}=

\displaystyle\>\lambda+\omega^{2}-2m\omega,

with
\widetilde{\omega}=\omega-\frac{m}{2}
; see
[
TdC20
]
[Eq. (3.2)]. With the translations
a\leftrightarrow Q
and
(\alpha_{\rm Kerr},\beta_{\rm Kerr},L_{\rm Kerr})\leftrightarrow(\alpha,\beta,L)
, the confluent Heun operators are therefore are the same, so we can directly apply methods from the extremal Kerr setting.

It then follows from
[
TdC20
]
[Proposition 3.1] that

\mathbb{U}(x):=\lim_{y\to 0}(x^{2}+2)^{\frac{1}{2}}(x-2)^{\alpha}\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{-2\gamma(x+iy-1)(r-1)}(r-1)^{\alpha}e^{\beta(r-1)^{-1}}e^{-\gamma r}R(r)\,dr

is well-defined as a limit with respect to
L^{2}_{x}((2,\infty))
and is a smooth solution to the ODE

\frac{d^{2}\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}^{2}}+\mathbb{V}\mathbb{U}=\frac{(x-1)(x-2)}{x^{2}+2}\mathbb{H},

with
\frac{dx_{*}}{dx}=\frac{x^{2}+2}{(x-1)(x-2)}
,

\mathbb{H}(x)=\lim_{y\to 0}(x^{2}+2)^{\frac{1}{2}}(x-2)^{\alpha}\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{-2\gamma(x+iy-1)(r-1)}(r-1)^{\alpha}e^{\beta(r-1)^{-1}}e^{-\gamma r}(r\Omega^{-2}H)\,dr

and

\mathbb{V}(x)=(x^{2}+2)^{-4}(x-1)\Big[4\beta\gamma(x^{2}+2)^{2}(x-2)(x-1)-\alpha^{2}(x^{2}+2)^{2}(x-1)\\
-L(x^{2}+2)^{2}(x-2)-(x-2)(3x^{3}+2x(x-6)+4)\Big].

Note that
\mathbb{V}
is real-valued and that in the extremal Kerr case with
M=1
, one obtains the same expression for
\mathbb{V}
, but with
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,L
replaced by
\alpha_{\rm Kerr},\beta_{\rm Kerr},\gamma_{\rm Kerr},L_{\rm Kerr}
. Filling in the above expressions for
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,L
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\omega^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(2)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{9}\left(\omega-\frac{q}{2}\right)^{2}.

Furthermore, for
\omega\widetilde{\omega}<0
,
[
TdC20
]
[Proposition 3.1] gives the following asymptotic behaviour:

\displaystyle x^{\frac{1}{4}}e^{8\sqrt{-\omega\widetilde{\omega}}x^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left[\frac{d\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}}-4i\sqrt{-\omega\widetilde{\omega}}x^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{U}\right](x_{*})=

\displaystyle\>O(x^{-\frac{1}{2}})\quad\textnormal{as $x_{*}\to\infty$},

\displaystyle\left[\frac{d\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}}+\frac{i}{3}\left(\omega+\frac{q}{2}\right)\mathbb{U}\right](x_{*})\to

\displaystyle\>0\quad\textnormal{as $x_{*}\to-\infty$},

\displaystyle|e^{8\sqrt{-\omega\widetilde{\omega}}x^{\frac{1}{2}}}x^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{U}|^{2}(\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{\pi}{4|\omega|}\left|\frac{\widetilde{\omega}}{\omega}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}|u(1)|^{2}.

Defining
\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}]:=(\omega+\frac{q}{2})\textnormal{Re}\,(i\frac{d\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}}\overline{\mathbb{U}})
, and using that
\mathbb{V}
is real, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx_{*}}\left(\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}]\right)=\left(\omega+\frac{q}{2}\right)\frac{(x-1)(x-2)}{x^{2}+a^{2}}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{i\mathbb{H}}\mathbb{U}).

By applying the above boundary conditions on
\mathbb{U}
, we moreover obtain for
\omega\widetilde{\omega}<0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](\infty)=

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](-\infty)=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{1}{2}\left(\left|\frac{d\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}}\right|^{2}(-\infty)+\frac{1}{3}\left(\omega+\frac{q}{2}\right)^{2}|\mathbb{U}|^{2}(-\infty)\right)=-\frac{2}{9}\left(\omega-\frac{q}{2}\right)^{2}|\mathbb{U}|^{2}(-\infty).

In contrast with the currents
j^{T}[u]
for solutions
u
to (
5.13
) in the superradiant frequency regime, see Lemma
5.8
, we have positivity of
\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](\infty)-\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](-\infty)
.

Furthermore,

|\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](2)|+|\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](\infty)|\leq\left|\left(\omega+\frac{q}{2}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x_{*}}}\frac{(x-1)(x-2)}{x^{2}+a^{2}}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{i\mathbb{H}}\mathbb{U})\,dx_{*}\right|

In view of the above identities and estimates for
\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}]
the proof of
[
TdC20
]
[Proposition 5.4] applies without modification, and the corresponding result can be used to obtain a proof of the Wronskian estimate in
[
TdC20
]
[Theorem 5.1] with minor modifications. We then conclude (
7.44
).
∎

Lemma 7.15
.

Let
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
. Let
\delta>0
. Let
u
be a solution to (
5.13
) with
H\equiv 0
. Let
u_{+,0}
and
u_{\infty,0}
be the event-horizon-normalized and infinity-normalized solutions in the case
\kappa_{c}=\kappa_{+}=0
.
Let
\epsilon>0
. Then, for
\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}\geq 0
, there exist
a_{+},a_{\infty}\in\mathbb{C}
and uniform constants
C>0
and
\delta>0
, such that for
r\in(r_{+}+\epsilon,\frac{1}{r_{c}^{-1}+\epsilon})
, we can write:

u(r_{*}(r))=a_{+}(u_{+,0}(r_{*}(r))+\varepsilon_{1}(r))+a_{\infty}(u_{\infty}(r_{*}(r))+\varepsilon_{2}(r)),

(7.46)

with
\varepsilon_{i}(r)\leq C(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c})K\epsilon^{-3}
, where
K
is the constant appearing in (
7.44
).

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
7.1
, we we can write:

\frac{d^{2}(\Omega u)}{dr^{2}}+\Omega^{-4}\left(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V-\Omega^{3}\frac{d^{2}\Omega}{dr^{2}}\right)(\Omega u)=0.

We can split up the right-hand side above in the following way:

\frac{d^{2}(\Omega u)}{dr^{2}}+\left[\Omega^{-4}\left(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V-\Omega^{3}\frac{d^{2}\Omega}{dr^{2}}\right)\right]_{\kappa_{+}=\kappa_{c}=0}\Omega u=\vartheta\Omega u.

Then,
|\vartheta(r)|\leq C(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c})(\rho_{+}^{-4}+\rho_{c}^{-4})
.

We will now apply Proposition
C.2
with
W_{1}(r):=((1-r^{-1})u_{+,0})(r)
and
W_{2}=((1-r^{-1})u_{\infty,0})(r)
. Note first that by Theorem
7.14
:

|\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})|=|\mathfrak{W}|\geq\frac{1}{K}.

Furthermore, by applying the upper bound estimates in Propositions
7.9
,
7.10
,
7.11
and
7.12
, we conclude moreover that
|W_{i}|\leq C(1-r^{-1})
and
|\frac{dW_{i}}{dr}|\leq C(1-r^{-1})^{-1}
, so we can take
P_{0}=Q=P_{1}\equiv 1
and
f\equiv 1
in Proposition
C.2
.

Finally, observe that

|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\int_{r_{+}+\epsilon}^{r_{c}-\epsilon^{-1}}|\vartheta|(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}\leq C(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c})|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\epsilon^{-3}.

We conclude that

\Omega u(r_{*}(r))=a_{+}\left[((1-r^{-1})u_{+,0})(r)+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}(r)\right]+a_{\infty}\left[((1-r^{-1})u_{\infty,0})(r)+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}(r)\right],

with
|\tilde{\varepsilon}_{i}|(r)\leq C\epsilon^{-3}(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c})(1-r^{-1})
. Since in the interval
(r_{+}+\epsilon,\frac{1}{r_{c}^{-1}+\epsilon})
, we have:

\left|(1-r^{-1})-\Omega(r)\right|\leq C(\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}),

we obtain (
7.46
) after defining
\varepsilon_{1}(r):=\Omega^{-1}(r)\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}(r)+\left(\Omega^{-1}(1-r^{-1})-1\right)u_{+,0}(r_{*}(r))
and
\varepsilon_{2}(r):=\Omega^{-1}(r)\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}(r)+\left(\Omega^{-1}(1-r^{-1})-1\right)u_{\infty,0}(r_{*}(r))
.
∎

Proposition 7.16
.

Let
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
. Assume that
\widehat{\omega}\widetilde{\omega}<0
. Let
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{+}\leq\kappa_{1}
. For suitably small
\kappa_{1}>0
, there exists a constant
K=K(\gamma,L_{0},\kappa_{1})>0
such that:

|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\leq 2K.

(7.47)

Proof.

Consider
u_{+}
and let
\delta>0
. Then by using that
|\widetilde{\omega}|>\gamma
, we can apply
Proposition
7.3
to obtain for
\epsilon=\gamma\delta
,
\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}>0
suitably small and
r-r_{+}\leq 2\epsilon
:

u_{+}(r_{*}(r))=e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{r(0)}^{r}\rho_{+}(r^{\prime})\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}(1+\delta O((r-r_{+})^{0})).

In this case, we can combine the above expression with Lemma
7.15
and take
\kappa_{+}+\kappa_{c}
suitably small to conclude that
\alpha_{2}=0
and
\alpha_{1}=1
.

Hence, we obtain for
r_{+}+\epsilon\leq r\leq\frac{1}{r_{c}^{-1}+\epsilon}
:

u_{+}(r_{*})=u_{+,0}(r_{*}(r))+\delta O((r-r_{+})^{0})=\left[\alpha_{1,+,0}u_{\infty,0}(r_{*}(r))+\alpha_{2,+,0}\overline{u_{\infty,0}}(r_{*}(r))\right]+\delta O((r-r_{+})^{0}),

for some complex constants
\alpha_{1,+,0}
and
\alpha_{2,+,0}
; see also (
7.42
).
Now, we use that
|\widehat{\omega}|>\gamma
to apply Proposition
7.7
to obtain for
\epsilon=\gamma\delta
,
\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}>0
suitably small and
r_{+}+\epsilon\leq r\leq\frac{1}{r_{c}^{-1}+\epsilon}
:

\displaystyle u_{\infty}(r_{*}(r))=

\displaystyle\>(1+\delta O(s_{c}^{0}))u_{\infty,0}(r_{*}(r)),

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{\infty}(r_{*}(r))=

\displaystyle\>(1+\delta O(s_{c}^{0}))\overline{u}_{\infty,0}(r_{*}(r)).

Hence, we can express globally:

u_{+}(r_{*})=\alpha_{1,+,0}(1+O(\delta))u_{\infty}(r_{*}(r))+\alpha_{2,+,0}(1+O(\delta))\overline{u_{\infty}}(r_{*}(r)).

By the identity (
7.43
), we can therefore conclude that
\mathfrak{W}=-2i\widehat{\omega}\alpha_{2,+,0}(1+O(\delta))
. Hence,
|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\leq K(1+O(\delta))\leq 2K
, for suitably small
\delta>0
.

∎

If
\kappa_{+}>\kappa_{1}
, we cannot infer (
7.47
) immediately. Instead, it follows from the additional assumption of the following condition:

Condition 1

(Quantitative mode stability away from extremality)
.

Let
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
. Let
\kappa_{+}>\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{=}0
, with
\kappa_{1}
the constant from Proposition
7.16
. Then there exists a constant
\tilde{K}=\tilde{K}(\gamma,L_{0},\kappa_{1})>0
such that:

|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\leq\tilde{K}.

(7.48)

We now conclude both an upper bound and a lower bound on the Wronskian
\mathfrak{W}
, valid for all bounded frequencies
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
.

Corollary 7.17
.

Let
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
and assume that
|q|<q_{1}
. There exists a suitably small
\kappa_{1}>0
and a constant
K=K(L_{0},\gamma,\beta,q_{1},\kappa_{1})>0
, such that for all
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{+}\leq\kappa_{1}
:

\frac{1}{K}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\frac{1}{1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log((1+|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}(1+|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-1})}\\
\leq|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\leq K(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\frac{1}{1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log((1+|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}(1+|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-1})}.

(7.49)

If Condition
1
holds, then the assumption
\kappa_{+}\leq\kappa_{1}
can be dropped.

Proof.

If
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}
, then (
7.49
) follows directly from Proposition
7.16
.

If
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}
, then we write

u_{+}(r_{*})={\alpha}_{1,+}u_{\infty}(r_{*})+{\alpha}_{2,+}\overline{u}_{\infty}(r_{*}).

and we apply (
7.43
) to obtain

|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}=\frac{1}{2|{\alpha}_{2,+}||\widetilde{\omega}|}.

Using that
|\alpha_{2,+}|=|E_{2}|
and
|\widehat{\omega}|\geq||q|-|\widetilde{\omega}||\geq|q_{0}|-\gamma>0
for suitably small
\gamma
, we can apply (
7.22
) to conclude (
7.49
).

If
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\infty}
, we write

u_{\infty}(r_{*})={\alpha}_{1,\infty}u_{+}(r_{*})+{\alpha}_{2,\infty}\overline{u}_{+}(r_{*}).

and we have that:

|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}=\frac{1}{2|{\alpha}_{2,\infty}||\widehat{\omega}|}.

We then use that
|\alpha_{2,\infty}|=|A_{2}|
and
|\widehat{\omega}|\geq|q_{0}|-\gamma>0
and we apply (
7.35
) to conclude (
7.49
).
∎

7.5.
Green’s formula estimates: homogeneous part

For suitably rapidly decaying
H(r_{*})
as
r_{*}\to\pm\infty
, we can apply Green’s formula to express:

u(r_{*})=\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}u_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})H(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}+\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{r_{*}}^{\infty}u_{\infty}(r_{*}^{\prime})H(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}.

(7.50)

The validity of the above formula in an
L^{2}_{r,\omega}
sense will be justified by first assuming smoothness and compact support in
r_{*}
of
H
to derive estimates and then applying a standard density argument.

In this section, we will derive
L^{\infty}
-estimates for the following products that relate to (
7.50
):

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}|u_{+}|(r_{*})|u_{\infty}|(r_{*}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}|v_{+}^{\prime}|(r_{*})|u_{\infty}|(r_{*}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}|u_{+}|(r_{*})|v_{\infty}^{\prime}|(r_{*}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}|u_{+}|(r_{*})|w_{\infty}^{\prime}|(r_{*}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}|w_{+}^{\prime}|(r_{*})|u_{\infty}|(r_{*}^{\prime}).

with different values of
r_{*},r_{*}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, where we define in analogy with (
5.14
) and (
5.15
):

\displaystyle v_{\square}=

\displaystyle\>e^{i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u_{\square},

\displaystyle w_{\square}=

\displaystyle\>e^{-i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u_{\square},

where
\square\in\{+,\infty\}
.

Proposition 7.18
.

Let
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
.

(1)

Let
0\leq\rho_{+}\leq\rho_{+}(R)
with
1<R<r_{c}
. Then, there exists a
\kappa_{1}>0
and a constant
C=C(q_{0},q_{1},L_{0},\beta,\gamma,R)>0
, such that for all
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{1}
and either
\kappa_{1}\leq\kappa_{1}
or
\kappa_{1}>\kappa_{1}
with the additional assumption of Condition
1
:

\displaystyle|u_{+}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+})),

(7.51)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{dv_{+}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\Omega^{2}(1+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+})^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}

(7.52)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}),

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}|u_{\infty}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))|

\displaystyle+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\rho_{+})^{-1}|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\left|\frac{dv_{\infty}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))\right|

(7.53)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}(1+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\rho_{+})^{-1}(1+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}.

(2)

Let
0\leq\rho_{c}\leq\rho_{c}(R)
with
1<R<r_{c}
. Then there exists a constant

C=C(q_{0},q_{1},L_{0},\beta,\gamma,R)>0
, such that:

\displaystyle|u_{\infty}(r_{*}(\rho_{c}))|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-1}\rho_{c})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-1}\rho_{c})),

(7.54)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{dw_{\infty}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*}(\rho_{c}))\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-1}\Omega^{2}(1+(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-1}\rho_{c})^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}

(7.55)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log(1+(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-1}\rho_{c})),

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}|u_{+}(r_{*}(\rho_{c}))|

\displaystyle+(|\widehat{\omega}|+\rho_{c})^{-1}|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\left|\frac{dw_{+}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*}(\rho_{c}))\right|

(7.56)

\displaystyle\leq C|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}

\displaystyle\>(1+|\widehat{\omega}|^{-1}\rho_{c})^{-1}(1+(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{-1}\rho_{c})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}.

Proof.

We consider separately Regions I, II, III and IV and the upper bound estimates on the coefficients
A_{i},B_{i},D_{i}
that are derived in §
7.2
and §
7.3
. We combine these with the expressions of
u_{+}
and
u_{\infty}
in terms of special functions in §
7.1
and estimates on these special functions that follow from the expansions in Lemma
C.3
and Lemma
C.6
.
∎

7.6.
Green’s formula estimates: inhomogeneous part

In this section, we will always assume that
\kappa_{+}\geq\kappa_{c}
and
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{1}
, with
\kappa_{1}>0
the constant appearing in Proposition
7.18
. The goal is to prove the following weighted
L^{2}
-estimate for solutions
u
to (
5.13
):

Theorem 7.19
.

Let
\epsilon>0
and
\max\{0,1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}<p<\min\{2,1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(q_{0},q_{\rm max},\gamma,\beta,L_{0},p,\epsilon)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\ell\leq L_{0}}

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z},|m|\leq\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{1}^{r_{c}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1+\epsilon}r^{-2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|u|^{2}\,dr

(7.57)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\left(\Omega^{2}|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}+\rho_{+}^{1-\epsilon}\xi^{2}|rG_{\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\right)\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\left(\Omega^{2}|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}+\rho_{c}^{1-\epsilon}\xi^{2}|rG_{\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\right)\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau.

We introduce:

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{+}(r_{*}):=

\displaystyle\>e^{-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}H(r_{*}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\infty}(r_{*}):=

\displaystyle\>e^{-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}H(r_{*}).

We will first rewrite the Green’s formula (
7.50
) in terms of
v_{+}
,
v_{\infty}
,
w_{+}
,
w_{\infty}
and
\widetilde{H}
.

Lemma 7.20
.

Let
u
be a solution to (
5.13
) and let
p,p^{\prime},R_{*}\in\mathbb{R}
and assume that
H\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{*})
. Then we can express for
r_{*}\leq R_{*}
:

u(r_{*})=\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{+})(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(y)\widetilde{H}_{+}(y)dy\\
+\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}w_{\infty})(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}e^{i\widehat{\omega}y}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}(y)\widetilde{H}_{\infty}(y)dy\\
+\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}f_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime},r_{*},\widetilde{\omega})g_{+,1}(r_{*}^{\prime},\widetilde{\omega})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}+\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}f_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime},r_{*},\widetilde{\omega})g_{+,2}(r_{*}^{\prime},\widetilde{\omega})\,dr_{*}^{\prime},

(7.58)

where for
r_{*}\leq R_{*}
:

\displaystyle f_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime},r_{*},\widetilde{\omega})=

\displaystyle\>\begin{cases}-\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{+})^{\prime}(r_{*}^{\prime})}{\mathfrak{W}}\quad&r_{*}^{\prime}\leq r_{*},\>r_{*}<R_{*},\\
\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{\infty})^{\prime}(r_{*}^{\prime})}{\mathfrak{W}}\quad&r_{*}^{\prime}>r_{*},\>r_{*}<R_{*}\\
\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}w_{\infty})^{\prime}(r_{*}^{\prime})}{\mathfrak{W}}\quad&r_{*}\geq R_{*}\end{cases},

\displaystyle g_{+,1}(r_{*}^{\prime},\widetilde{\omega})=

\displaystyle\>\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}^{\prime}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(y)\widetilde{H}_{+}(y)dy,

\displaystyle g_{+,2}(r_{*}^{\prime},\widetilde{\omega})=

\displaystyle\>\int_{r_{*}^{\prime}}^{\infty}e^{i\widehat{\omega}y}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}(y)\widetilde{H}_{\infty}(y)dy.

For
r_{*}\geq R_{*}
, we can express:

u(r_{*})=\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}w_{\infty})(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}e^{i\widehat{\omega}y}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}(y)\widetilde{H}_{\infty}(y)dy\\
+\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{+})(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\widetilde{H}_{+}(y)dy\\
+\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}f_{\infty}(r_{*}^{\prime},r_{*},\widehat{\omega})g_{\infty,1}(r_{*}^{\prime},\widehat{\omega})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}+\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}f_{\infty}(r_{*}^{\prime},r_{*},\widehat{\omega})g_{\infty,2}(\widehat{\omega})\,dr_{*}^{\prime},

(7.59)

where for
r_{*}\leq R_{*}
:

\displaystyle f_{\infty}(r_{*}^{\prime},r_{*},\widehat{\omega})=

\displaystyle\>\begin{cases}-\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}w_{\infty})^{\prime}(r_{*}^{\prime})}{\mathfrak{W}}\quad&r_{*}^{\prime}\geq r_{*},\,r_{*}>R_{*},\\
\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}w_{+})^{\prime}(r_{*}^{\prime})}{\mathfrak{W}}\quad&r_{*}^{\prime}<r_{*},\,r_{*}>R_{*},\\
\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{+})^{\prime}(r_{*}^{\prime})}{\mathfrak{W}}\quad&r_{*}\leq R_{*}\end{cases},

\displaystyle g_{\infty,1}(r_{*}^{\prime},\widehat{\omega})=

\displaystyle\>\int_{r_{*}^{\prime}}^{\infty}e^{i\widehat{\omega}y}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}(y)\widetilde{H}_{\infty}(y)dy,

\displaystyle g_{\infty,2}(r_{*}^{\prime},\widehat{\omega})=

\displaystyle\>\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}^{\prime}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(y)\widetilde{H}_{+}(y)dy.

Proof.

Let
p,p^{\prime},R_{*}\in\mathbb{R}
. We introduce the notation:
v_{+,p}:=(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{+}
,
v_{\infty,p}:=(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{\infty}
,
w_{\infty,p^{\prime}}=(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}w_{\infty}
,
\widetilde{H}_{+,p}=(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\widetilde{H}_{+}
and
\widetilde{H}_{\infty,p^{\prime}}=(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}\widetilde{H}_{\infty}
. Then we can write for
r_{*}\leq R_{*}
:

u(r_{*})=\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}v_{+,p}(r_{*}^{\prime})e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}^{\prime}}\widetilde{H}_{+,p}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}+\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{r_{*}}^{R_{*}}v_{\infty,p}(r_{*}^{\prime})e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}^{\prime}}\widetilde{H}_{+,p}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}\\
+\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}w_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(r_{*}^{\prime})e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}^{\prime}}\widetilde{H}_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}\\
=\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}v_{+,p}(r_{*}^{\prime})\frac{d}{dr_{*}^{\prime}}\left[\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}^{\prime}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{+,p}(y)dy\right]\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{r_{*}}^{R_{*}}v_{\infty,p}(r_{*}^{\prime})\frac{d}{dr_{*}^{\prime}}\left[\int_{r_{*}^{\prime}}^{R_{*}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{+,p}(y)dy\right]\,dr_{*}^{\prime}\\
-\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}w_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(r_{*}^{\prime})\frac{d}{dr_{*}^{\prime}}\left[\int_{r_{*}^{\prime}}^{\infty}e^{i\widehat{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(y)dy\right]\,dr_{*}^{\prime}\\
=\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})v_{+,p}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{+,p}(y)dy+\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})w_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}e^{i\widehat{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(y)dy\\
-\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}v_{+,p}^{\prime}(r_{*}^{\prime})\left[\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}^{\prime}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{+,p}(y)dy\right]\,dr_{*}^{\prime}+\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{r_{*}}^{R_{*}}v^{\prime}_{\infty,p}(r_{*}^{\prime})\left[\int_{r_{*}^{\prime}}^{R_{*}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{+,p}(y)dy\right]\,dr_{*}^{\prime}\\
+\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}w^{\prime}_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(r_{*}^{\prime})\left[\int_{r_{*}^{\prime}}^{\infty}e^{i\widehat{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(y)dy\right]\,dr_{*}^{\prime}\\
=\frac{u_{\infty}(r_{*})v_{+,p}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{+,p}(y)dy+\frac{u_{+}(r_{*})w_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(r_{*})}{\mathfrak{W}}\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}e^{i\widehat{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{\infty,p^{\prime}}(y)dy\\
+\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}f_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime},r_{*},\widetilde{\omega})g_{+,1}(r_{*}^{\prime},\widetilde{\omega})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}+\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}f_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime},r_{*},\widetilde{\omega})g_{+,2}(r_{*}^{\prime},\widetilde{\omega})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}.

This concludes (
7.58
). To obtain (
7.59
), we repeat the above argument with
v_{+}
replaced by
w_{\infty}
,
v_{\infty}
replaced by
w_{+}
,
w_{\infty}
replaced by
v_{+}
and the roles of
\widehat{\omega}
and
-\widetilde{\omega}
interchanged.
∎

In order to obtain suitable weighted
L^{2}
-estimates for
u
, satisfying the identities in (
7.58
) and (
7.59
), we will need the following lemma:

Lemma 7.21
.

Let
\epsilon\geq 0
,
\delta>0
,
p,p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
and
R_{*}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tilde{R}>1
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(\delta,R_{*})>0
such that for all suitably regular and decaying functions
f:(1,\infty)_{r}\times\mathbb{R}_{y}\times\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\to\mathbb{C}
and
g:\mathbb{R}_{y}\times\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\to\mathbb{C}
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\>\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(r)r^{-2}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}f(r,y,\omega)g(y,\omega)\,dy\right|^{2}\,drd\omega

(7.60)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left[\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(r)r^{-2}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|f|^{2}(r,y,\omega)\,dydr\right]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\left[\sup_{-\infty<y\leq r_{*}(\tilde{R})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|g|^{2}(y,\omega)\,d\omega\right],

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\>\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(r)r^{-2}\left|\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}f(r,y,\omega)g(y,\omega)\,dy\right|^{2}\,drd\omega

(7.61)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left[\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}(r)\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}(\rho_{c}(y)+2\kappa_{c})^{-1}\rho_{c}^{-\delta}(y)|f|^{2}(r,y,\omega)\,dydr\right]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\left[\sup_{R_{*}\leq y<\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|g|^{2}(y,\omega)\,d\omega\right].

We can also estimate:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\>\int_{\tilde{R}}^{r_{c}}\rho_{c}^{1+\epsilon}(r)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(r)r^{-2}\left|\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}f(r,y,\omega)g(y,\omega)\,dy\right|^{2}\,drd\omega

(7.62)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C\left[\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\tilde{R}}^{\infty}\rho_{c}^{1+\epsilon}(r)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(r)r^{-2}\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}(\rho_{c}(y)+2\kappa_{c})^{-1}\rho_{c}^{-\delta}(y)|f|^{2}(r,y,\omega)\,dydr\right]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\left[\sup_{r_{*}(\tilde{R})<y<\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|g|^{2}(y,\omega)\,d\omega\right],

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\>\int_{\tilde{R}}^{\infty}\rho_{c}^{1+\epsilon}(r)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(r)r^{-2}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}f(r,y,\omega)g(y,\omega)\,dy\right|^{2}\,drd\omega

(7.63)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}

\displaystyle\>\left[\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\tilde{R}}^{\infty}\rho_{c}^{1+\epsilon}(r)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(r)r^{-2}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|f|^{2}(r,y,\omega)\,dydr\right]

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\left[\sup_{-\infty<y<R_{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|g|^{2}(y,\omega)\,d\omega\right].

Proof.

We first apply Cauchy–Schwarz in the
y
-direction to obtain:

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{-2}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}f(r,y,\omega)g(y,\omega)\,dy\right|^{2}\,drd\omega\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\left[\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{-2}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|f|^{2}(r,y,\omega)\,dydr\right]\\
\times\left[\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})\rho_{+}^{\delta}(y)|g|^{2}(y,\omega)\,dy\right]\,d\omega.

We now take estimate the RHS above further taking the supremum in
\omega
of the terms in square brackets involving
|f|^{2}
and interchanging the order of integration in the integrals of
|g|^{2}
:

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\left[\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{-2}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|f|^{2}(r,y,\omega)\,dydr\right]\\
\times\left[\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})\rho_{+}(y)^{\delta}|g|^{2}(y,\omega)\,dy\right]\,d\omega\\
\leq\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{R}}\left[\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{-2}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|f|^{2}(r,y,\omega)\,dydr\right]\\
\times\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})\rho_{+}(y)^{\delta}|g|^{2}(y,\omega)\,d\omega dy\\
=\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{R}}\left[\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{-2}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|f|^{2}(r,y,\omega)\,dydr\right]\\
\times\int_{1}^{r(R_{*})}\Omega^{-2}(r)(\rho_{+}(r)+2\kappa_{+})\rho_{+}^{\delta}(r)\,dr^{\prime}\cdot\sup_{-\infty<y<R_{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}|g|^{2}(y,\omega)\,d\omega.

We conclude that (
7.60
) holds by using that the integral of
\Omega^{-2}(r)(\rho_{+}(r)+2\kappa_{+})\rho_{+}(r)^{\delta}
is finite for
\delta>0
. The estimate (
7.61
) follows by repeating the above argument, but with
(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})\rho_{+}(y)^{\delta}
replaced by
(\rho_{c}(y)+2\kappa_{c})\rho_{c}(y)^{\delta}
and adjusting the integration domain of
y
.

We also immediately obtain (
7.62
) and (
7.63
) by interchanging the roles of
\rho_{+}
and
\rho_{c}
everywhere above.
∎

In the lemma below, we apply Plancherel’s theorem to estimate integrals of the inhomogeneity
H
in
\mathbb{R}_{\omega}
in terms of integrals in
\tau
of
F_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}
and
G_{\widetilde{A}}
, which appear on the right-hand side of (
5.1
).

Lemma 7.22
.

Let
p,p^{\prime}\in[0,\infty)
and
R_{*}\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, there exists a constant
C=C(p,p^{\prime},R_{*})>0
such that:

\sup_{-\infty<r_{*}<R_{*}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z},|m|\leq\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(y)e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{+}(y,\omega)\,dy\right|^{2}\,d\omega\\
\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r(R_{*})\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\mathbbm{h}^{-2}\left[\Omega^{2}|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}+\rho_{+}^{1-\delta}\xi^{2}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau.

(7.64)

Furthermore,

\sup_{R_{*}\leq r_{*}<\infty}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\left|\int_{r_{*}}^{\infty}e^{+i\widehat{\omega}y}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}\widetilde{H}_{\infty}(y)\,dy\right|^{2}\,d\omega\\
\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r(R_{*})\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}^{-2}\left[\Omega^{2}|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}+\rho_{c}^{1-\delta}\xi^{2}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau.

(7.65)

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{H}_{F}
denote the part of
\widetilde{H}_{+}
coming from
F_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}
and let
\widetilde{H}_{G}
denote the part of
\widetilde{H}_{+}
coming from
G_{\widetilde{A}}
, so
\widetilde{H}_{+}=\widetilde{H}_{F}+\widetilde{H}_{G}
. We will first assume that
F_{\xi,\tilde{A}}
and
\xi G_{\tilde{A}}
are compactly supported in
r_{*}
. After establishing, (
7.64
) and (
7.65
), the general case then follows from a standard density argument.

By applying an inverse Fourier transform, we obtain:

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(y)e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{F}(y,\omega)\,dy\right|^{2}\,d\omega\\
=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{t}}\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}e(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(y)e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y+i\omega t}r\Omega^{2}(F_{\xi,\widetilde{A}})_{\ell m}(y,t)\,dydt\right|^{2}\,d\omega.

Recall that
\tau(t,r_{*})=t+r_{*}-\int_{1}^{r(r_{*})}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}
and consider
u(t,r_{*}):=t-r_{*}
. Then

d\tau\wedge du=\left(\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\Omega^{2}-2\right)dt\wedge dr_{*}=-\mathbbm{h}dt\wedge dr_{*},

so

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{t}}\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(y)e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y+i\omega t}r\Omega^{2}(F_{\xi,\widetilde{A}})_{\ell m}(y,t)\,dydt\right|^{2}\\
=\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{u(\tau,r_{*})}^{\infty}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(\tau,u)e^{iqr_{*}(\tau,u)+i\omega u}\mathbbm{h}^{-1}r\Omega^{2}(F_{\xi,\widetilde{A}})_{\ell m}(\tau,u)\,dud\tau\right|^{2}\\
=\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}e^{i\omega u}f(\tau,u)\,dud\tau\right|^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+\tau)^{2}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}e^{-i\omega u}f(\tau,u)\,du\right|^{2}\,d\tau.

with

f(\tau,u):=\mathbf{1}_{u\geq u(\tau,r_{*})}\left[(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}e^{iqr_{*}+i\omega u}\mathbbm{h}^{-1}r\Omega^{2}(F_{\xi,\widetilde{A}})_{\ell m}\right](\tau,u).

By Placherel’s theorem, we have that

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}e^{i\omega u}f(\tau,u)\,du\right|^{2}\,d\omega=2\pi\int_{\mathbb{R}_{u}}|f|^{2}(\tau,u)\,du=2\pi\int_{1}^{r(r_{*})}\Omega^{-2}|f|^{2}(\tau,u(r,\tau))\,dr.

Putting everything together, we obtain:

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(y)e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}\widetilde{H}_{+}(y,\omega)\,dy\right|^{2}\,d\omega\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{1}^{r(r_{*})}(1+\tau)^{2}\Omega^{-2}r|f|^{2}(\tau,u(r,\tau))\,drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{1}^{r(r_{*})}(1+\tau)^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}\mathbbm{h}^{-2}\left|r(F_{\xi,\widetilde{A}})_{\ell m}\right|^{2}\,drd\tau.

We conclude (
7.64
) by using that
F_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}
is supported in
\{0\leq\tau\leq 1\}
. We can repeat the above argument with a coordinate change to
(\tau,v)
,
v=t+r_{*}
and with the roles of
\rho_{+}
and
\rho_{c}
interchanged, to derive (
7.65
).

We estimate the contribution of
\widetilde{H}_{G}
by simply using Cauchy–Schwarz and Plancherel’s theorem together: for
r_{*}\leq R_{*}
:

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\left|\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(y)e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}y}(\widetilde{H}_{G})_{\ell m}(y,\omega)\,dy\right|^{2}\,d\omega\leq C\int_{1}^{r}\rho_{+}^{-1+\delta}\,r^{-2}dr\\
\times\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}}\int_{-\infty}^{R_{*}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{2}\rho_{+}^{1-\delta}|(\widetilde{H}_{G})_{\ell m}|^{2}\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
\leq C_{\delta,R_{*}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{1}^{r(R_{*})}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{-2}\rho_{+}^{1-\delta}|r^{3}G_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\rho_{+}d\tau.

We conclude(
7.64
) by using that
d\rho_{+}=r^{-2}dr
. The estimates for (
7.65
) proceed analogously, with
\rho_{c}
taking on the role of
\rho_{+}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.19
.

We apply the formula (
7.58
), together with Lemmas
7.21
and
7.22
to obtain:

\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\ell\leq L_{0}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z},|m|\leq\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|u|^{2}\,r^{-2}dr\lesssim\\
\left[\sup_{\omega\in\mathbb{R},\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\ell\leq L_{0},|m|\leq\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{4}U_{i}\right]\Bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\left[\Omega^{2}|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}+\rho_{+}^{1-\delta}r^{-2}\xi^{2}|r^{3}G_{\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\left[\Omega^{2}|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}+\rho_{c}^{1-\delta}r^{-2}\xi^{2}|r^{3}G_{\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma dr\,d\tau\Bigg],

where
\delta>0
can be taken arbitrarily small and

\displaystyle U_{1}:=

\displaystyle\>\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}\frac{|u_{\infty}u_{+}|^{2}}{|\mathfrak{W}|^{2}}(r_{*}(r))(1+(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{p^{\prime}-p})\,r^{-2}dr,

\displaystyle U_{2}:=

\displaystyle\>\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{-2}{|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|u_{\infty}|^{2}(r_{*}(r))\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r)}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{+})^{\prime}(y)|^{2}}\,dydr,

\displaystyle U_{3}:=

\displaystyle\>\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|u_{+}|^{2}(r_{*}(r))r^{-2}\int_{r_{*}(r)}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{\infty})^{\prime}(y)|^{2}\,dydr,

\displaystyle U_{4}:=

\displaystyle\>\int_{1}^{\tilde{R}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|u_{+}|^{2}(r_{*}(r))r^{-2}\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}(\rho_{c}(y)+2\kappa_{c})^{-1}\rho_{c}^{-\delta}(y)|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}w_{\infty})^{\prime}(y)|^{2}\,dydr.

We will first estimate
U_{1}
. Using that
p<2+\epsilon
and applying (
7.51
) and (
7.53
), we obtain for
\epsilon>0
and
p<p^{\prime}+\epsilon
:

U_{1}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\rho_{+}(\tilde{R})}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-2}(1+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{-1}\rho_{+})^{-2}(1+\log^{2}(1+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}))(1+(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{p^{\prime}-p})\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|}\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(1+\rho_{+}^{p^{\prime}-p})\,dr+\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{\rho_{+}(\tilde{R})}\rho_{+}^{-1+\epsilon}(1+\log^{2}\rho_{+})(1+\rho_{+}^{p^{\prime}-p})\,dr\lesssim|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\epsilon}+|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\epsilon+p^{\prime}-p}+1.

We now consider
U_{2}
. We first apply (
7.51
) and (
7.52
) to obtain for
p>\delta
and
\rho_{+}\leq|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}
:

\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r)}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{+})^{\prime}(y)|^{2}\,dy\\
\lesssim\int_{1}^{r}\rho_{+}^{1-\delta}(r^{\prime})\left|\frac{d}{dr}\left((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{+}\right)\right|^{2}(r_{*}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-2}\int_{0}^{\rho_{+}(r)}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}+1-\delta}(1+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+})^{-3+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\log^{2}(1+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}))\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-2}\int_{0}^{\rho_{+}(r)}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}+1-\delta}\,d\rho_{+}\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-2}(\rho_{+}(r)+\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}+2-\delta}.

For
\rho_{+}>|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}
and
p>\delta
, we obtain instead:

\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r)}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{+})^{\prime}(y)|^{2}\,dy\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-2}\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}+1-\delta}\,d\rho_{+}\\
+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}^{\rho_{+}(r)}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}-2+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}(1+\log^{2}\rho_{+})\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim\begin{cases}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{p-\delta-\delta^{\prime}}\quad p\leq 1+\delta-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},\\
(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\rho_{+}^{p-1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta-\delta^{\prime}}(r)\quad p>1+\delta-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}.\end{cases}

for some sufficiently small
\delta^{\prime}>0
(when
\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\neq 0
, we can in fact set
\delta^{\prime}=0
).

We can now estimate
U_{2}
by applying (
7.53
) to obtain for
\epsilon>\delta+\delta^{\prime}
and
\max\{\delta,1+\delta-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}<p
:

U_{2}\lesssim\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-2}(\rho_{+}(r)+\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}+2-\delta}\,d\rho_{+}\\
+\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{\rho_{+}(\tilde{R})}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}-1+\epsilon-\delta-\delta^{\prime}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\,d\rho_{+}\lesssim|\widetilde{\omega}|^{2+\epsilon-\delta}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-2}+\rho_{+}(\tilde{R})^{\epsilon-\delta-\delta^{\prime}}\\
\lesssim|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\epsilon-\delta-\delta^{\prime}}+1.

Suppose now that
p>\delta
and
p<1+\delta-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
. Then we can estimate instead:

U_{2}\lesssim\widetilde{\omega}^{-2}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{p-\delta-\delta^{\prime}-1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-2}\rho_{+}^{4+\epsilon+\delta^{\prime}-p-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\,d\rho_{+}\\
+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{p-\delta-\delta^{\prime}-1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{\rho_{+}(\tilde{R})}\rho_{+}^{-\frac{p}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\,d\rho_{+}\lesssim|\widetilde{\omega}|^{\epsilon-\delta-\delta^{\prime}}+1.

To obtain boundedness of
U_{2}
, we therefore need restrict to
\epsilon\geq\delta+\delta^{\prime}
and
p>\delta
.

Consider
U_{3}
. Note first that we can apply (
7.53
) to obtain for
\rho_{+}(r)\geq|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}
:

\int_{r_{*}(r)}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{\infty})^{\prime}(y)|^{2}\,dy\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}\int_{\rho_{+}(r)}^{\rho_{+}(R)}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}\int_{\rho_{+}(r)}^{\rho_{+}(R)}\max\{(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})^{p-2-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta},\rho_{+}^{p-2-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}\}\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim\begin{cases}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}(\rho_{+}(r)+\kappa_{+})^{p-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}\quad p<1+\delta+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\\
(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}\quad p>1+\delta+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},\\
(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}\log\rho_{+}^{-1}(r)\quad p=1+\delta+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}.\end{cases}

We will first suppose that
p<1+\delta+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
. Then we moreover obtain for
\rho_{+}(r)\leq|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}
and
p<2+\delta
:

\int_{r_{*}(r)}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{\infty})^{\prime}(y)|^{2}\,dy\\
=\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}^{\rho_{+}(R)}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{\infty})^{\prime}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))|^{2}r^{2}\Omega^{-2}\,d\rho_{+}\\
+\int_{\rho_{+}(r)}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{-2}\rho_{+}^{-1-\delta}|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{\infty})^{\prime}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))|^{2}r^{2}\Omega^{-2}\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{p-2-\delta}+\int_{\rho_{+}(r)}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}-2}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}-1-\delta}\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{p-2-\delta}+(\rho_{+}(r)+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\rho_{+}(r)^{\frac{p}{2}-1-\delta}\leq(\rho_{+}(r)+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\rho_{+}(r)^{\frac{p}{2}-1-\delta}.

If
p>1+\delta+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
and
p<2+\delta
, we similarly obtain:

\int_{r_{*}(r)}^{R_{*}}(\rho_{+}(y)+2\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-\delta}(y)|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p}{2}}v_{\infty})^{\prime}(y)|^{2}\,dy\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}+(\rho_{+}(r)+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\rho_{+}(r)^{\frac{p}{2}-1-\delta}

Hence, for
p<2+\delta
and
p<1+\delta+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
, we apply (
7.51
) to obtain for
\epsilon>\delta
:

U_{3}\lesssim\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}-1-\delta}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|u_{+}|^{2}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))\,d\rho_{+}\\
+\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}^{\rho_{+}(\widetilde{R})}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon-\frac{p}{2}}(\rho_{+}(r)+\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}|u_{+}|^{2}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\epsilon-\delta}+\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}^{\rho_{+}(\widetilde{R})}\rho_{+}^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\epsilon-\frac{p}{2}}(\rho_{+}(r)+\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log^{2}\rho_{+})\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\epsilon-\delta-\delta^{\prime}},

with
\delta^{\prime}=0
if
\beta_{\ell}\neq 0
.

For
p>1+\delta+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
and
p<\min\{1+\epsilon+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},2+\delta\}
, we obtain:

U_{3}\lesssim\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{\frac{p}{2}-1}\rho_{+}^{\frac{p}{2}-1-\delta}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|u_{+}|^{2}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))\,d\rho_{+}\\
+\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}^{\rho_{+}(\widetilde{R})}(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon-\frac{p}{2}}(\rho_{+}(r)+\kappa_{+})^{-\frac{p}{2}}|u_{+}|^{2}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))\,d\rho_{+}\\
+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|u_{+}|^{2}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\epsilon-\delta}+\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}^{\rho_{+}(\widetilde{R})}\rho_{+}^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\epsilon-\frac{p}{2}}(\rho_{+}(r)+\kappa_{+})^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{\ell}0}\log^{2}\rho_{+})\,d\rho_{+}\\
+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}}\rho_{+}^{1-\frac{p}{2}+\epsilon}(\rho_{+}(r)+\kappa_{+})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\epsilon-\delta}+(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{-p+1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\epsilon-\delta^{\prime}},

with
\delta^{\prime}=0
if
\beta_{\ell}\neq 0
and
\delta^{\prime}>0
and
p<1+\epsilon-\delta^{\prime}+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
if
\beta_{\ell}=0
.

We conclude that
U_{3}
is uniformly bounded if
p<2+\delta
,
p<1+\epsilon+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta^{\prime}
,
\epsilon\geq\delta+\delta^{\prime}
and
\epsilon>\delta
. In other words, for
\epsilon>0
and
p<\max\{1+\epsilon+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},2+\epsilon\}
, there exist
\delta,\delta^{\prime}>0
, with
\epsilon-\delta>0
and
\delta^{\prime}>0
suitably small, such that
U_{3}
is uniformly bounded.

Finally, we turn to
U_{4}
. We proceed as in the estimates for
U_{2}
(in the case
\rho_{+}\geq|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+}
), but with the roles of
\rho_{+}
and
\rho_{c}
and
|\widetilde{\omega}|
and
|\widehat{\omega}|
interchanged. By (
7.54
) and (
7.55
) have that for
p^{\prime}>\delta
:

\int_{R_{*}}^{\infty}(\rho_{c}(y)+2\kappa_{c})^{-1}\rho_{c}^{-\delta}(y)|((r^{-1}\Omega)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}}w_{\infty})^{\prime}(y)|^{2}\,dy\\
\lesssim\begin{cases}(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{p^{\prime}-\delta-\delta^{\prime}}\quad p^{\prime}\leq 1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\delta,\\
(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\rho_{c}^{p^{\prime}-1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta-\delta^{\prime}}(R)\lesssim(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\quad p^{\prime}>1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\delta,\end{cases}

We will first suppose that
p^{\prime}>\delta
,
p^{\prime}>1+\delta^{\prime}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
,
p\leq 2+\epsilon
and
p<1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\epsilon
and apply (
7.51
) and (
7.56
) to obtain:

U_{4}\lesssim(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|u_{+}|^{2}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))\,d\rho_{+}\\
+(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{\rho_{+}(\tilde{R})}\rho_{+}^{1+\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|\mathfrak{W}|^{-2}|u_{+}|^{2}(r_{*}(\rho_{+}))\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}\int_{0}^{|\widetilde{\omega}|}\rho_{+}^{1-\frac{p}{2}+\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\,d\rho_{+}+\int_{|\widetilde{\omega}|}^{\rho_{+}(\tilde{R})}\rho_{+}^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\frac{p}{2}+\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\,d\rho_{+}\\
\lesssim(|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{2+\epsilon-p}+\rho_{+}(\tilde{R})^{1+\epsilon+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-p}.

Now suppose that
p^{\prime}>\delta^{\prime}
,
p^{\prime}\leq 1+\delta^{\prime}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
,
p\leq 2+\epsilon
and
p<1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\epsilon
. Then we can repeat the above computation with an additional factor
(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{p^{\prime}-1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta-\delta^{\prime\prime}}
to obtain:

U_{4}\lesssim(|\widehat{\omega}|+\kappa_{c})^{p^{\prime}-1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta-\delta^{\prime\prime}}((|\widetilde{\omega}|+\kappa_{+})^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-1}|\widetilde{\omega}|^{2+\epsilon-p}+\rho_{+}(\tilde{R})^{1+\epsilon+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-p})

Uniform boundedness of
U_{4}
therefore only follows if
p^{\prime}>\delta^{\prime}
,
p^{\prime}>1+\delta^{\prime}-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
,
p\leq 2+\epsilon
and
p<1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\epsilon
, with
\delta^{\prime}>0
arbitrarily small.

Combining the above estimates on
U_{1}
–
U_{4}
, we obtain the following restrictions on
p,p^{\prime}
:
\delta<p<\delta+\min\{2,1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}
,
p^{\prime}>\max\{0,1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\tilde{\delta},p-\epsilon\}
, where
\epsilon-\delta>0
can be made arbitrarily small. We take
\delta=\frac{\epsilon}{2}
and restrict:

\min\{0,1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}<p<\min\{2,1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

Then take
p^{\prime}=p
to guarantee
p^{\prime}>\max\{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},p-\epsilon\}
and conclude (
7.57
).

The estimate (
7.65
) follows in an entirely analogous manner, by interchanging the roles of
\rho_{+}
and
\rho_{c}
and
\widehat{\omega}
and
-\widetilde{\omega}
.
∎

8.
Integrated energy estimates in frequency space: combining all frequency regimes

We can split
u=u_{\ell\leq L_{0}}+u_{\ell>L_{0}}
and observe that
u_{\ell\leq L_{0}}
and
u_{\ell>L_{0}}
each independently satisfy (
5.13
) with
H_{m\ell}
replaced by
\mathbf{1}_{\ell\leq L_{0}}H_{m\ell}
and
\mathbf{1}_{\ell>L_{0}}H_{m\ell}
, respectively. This is not necessary, but highlights features that are particular to large angular frequencies or bounded angular frequencies.

In the remainder of
\lx@sectionsign\ref {sec:iedcomb}
, we will fix
r_{+}=1
. We will moreover assume that
u
arises from a sufficiently integrable
\uppsi
.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
E\geq 2
,
\delta>0
,
1\leq p<2
and
\ell>L_{0}
and write
u=u_{\ell>L_{0}}
. Then there exist constants
C,c=C(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},R_{1},R_{2},R_{3},L_{0},q_{1},\alpha,\beta,\delta),c(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},R_{1},R_{2},R_{3},L_{0},q_{1},\alpha,\beta,\delta)>0
, such that:

c\int_{(r_{2})_{*}}^{(R_{2})_{*}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left[(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}}+(1-r_{\rm max}r^{-1})^{2})(\widehat{\omega}^{2}+\ell(\ell+1))+1\right]|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{1})}\Big[(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|v|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2})\Big]\,dr_{*}\\
+c\int_{r_{*}(R_{1})}^{\infty}\Big[(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2})\Big]\,dr_{*}\leq+\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{3})*}\textnormal{Re}\,(2u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}-\int_{(R_{3})_{*}}^{\infty}\textnormal{Re}\,(2u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\omega}\chi_{r_{1}}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\omega}(1-\chi_{r_{1}})\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}+C\int_{(r_{3})_{*}}^{(R_{3})_{*}}(|u|+|u^{\prime}|)\cdot|H|^{\prime}\,dr_{*}+\int_{(r_{2})_{*}}^{(R_{2})_{*}}|H|^{2}\,dr_{*}\\
+C\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{3})*}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}||H|\,dr_{*}+C\int_{(R_{3})*}^{\infty}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}||H|\,dr_{*}.

(8.1)

Proof.

The proposition concerns the frequency ranges
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}
,
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
. We combine Propositions
6.3
,
6.4
and Corollary
6.2
and use that the leading-order behaviours of
y
and
f
agree when
r\downarrow 1
and
r\uparrow r_{c}
and that
|y+1|,|f+1|\leq C(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}
near
r=r_{+}
and
|y-1|,|f-1|\leq C(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}
near
r=r_{c}
.

Since the cut-off functions appearing in front of
j^{T}[u]
and
j^{K}[u]
are not identical in each frequency regime, but they do agree close to
r=r_{+}
and
r=r_{c}
, we moreover apply Young’s inequality to obtain:

\int_{(r_{2})_{*}}^{(R_{2})_{*}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}}|\widehat{\omega}||u|\cdot|H|\,dr_{*}\leq\int_{(r_{2})_{*}}^{(R_{2})_{*}}\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}}\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{-2}|H|^{2}\,dr_{*},

with
\mathbf{1}
the indicator function, and we absorb the
\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}
term to the left-hand side, using that there is no degenerate factor
(1-r_{\rm max}r^{-1})^{2}
when
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
.

In the cases
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm angular}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm trap}}
, we need an additional step to control
(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}
and
(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}
. Note first that:

\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}\leq 2|u^{\prime}+i\widetilde{\omega}u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}\leq 2|v^{\prime}|^{2}+Cq^{2}\rho_{+}^{2}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2}.

Hence,

(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}\leq(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p-\delta}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+2(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}|^{2}\\
+Cq^{2}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p-\delta}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|u|^{2}.

Similarly,

(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}\leq(r^{1}\Omega)^{p-\delta}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+2(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}|^{2}\\
+Cq^{2}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p-\delta}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|u|^{2}.

Since the factor
(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p-\delta}
can be made arbitrarily small for
r_{1}-1
and
R_{2}^{-1}-r_{c}^{-1}
suitably small, we can absorb the right-hand sides of the above inequalities into the left-hand sides of the estimates in Proposition
6.4
and Corollary
6.2
.
∎

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
|q|\geq q_{0}>0
,
E\geq 2
,
\delta>0
and
\ell\leq L_{0}
and write
u=u_{\ell\leq L_{0}}
. Let
\epsilon>0
and
\max\{0,1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}<p<\min\{2,1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}+\epsilon
. For suitably small
\kappa_{1}>0
there exist constants

C,c=C(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},R_{1},R_{2},R_{3},L_{0},q_{0},q_{1},p,\kappa_{1}),c(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},R_{1},R_{2},R_{3},L_{0},q_{0},q_{1},p,\kappa_{1})>0
, such that for all
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{+}\geq\kappa_{c}
with either
\kappa_{+}\leq\kappa_{1}
or
\kappa_{+}>\kappa_{1}
with the additional assumption of Condition
1
:

c\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{(r_{2})_{*}}^{(R_{2})_{*}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}+\ell(\ell+1)+1)|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
+c\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{-\infty}^{r_{*}(r_{1})}\left[\rho_{+}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|v^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1))\rho_{+}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\right]|v|^{2}\\
+\rho_{+}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2})\Big]\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
+c\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{r_{*}(R_{1})}^{\infty}\Big[\rho_{c}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|w^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\ell(\ell+1)+\widehat{\omega}^{2})\rho_{c}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|w|^{2}\\
+\rho_{c}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}(\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}+|u^{\prime}|^{2})\Big]\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{3})*}\textnormal{Re}\,(2u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}d\omega-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{(R_{3})_{*}}^{\infty}\textnormal{Re}\,(2u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}v^{\prime}e^{-i\widetilde{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})^{\prime}\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{R_{1}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{p}w^{\prime}e^{i\widehat{\omega}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
+E\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\omega}\chi_{K}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}d\omega+E\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\omega}\chi_{T}\textnormal{Re}\,(iu\overline{H})\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{(r_{3})_{*}}^{(R_{3})_{*}}(|u|+|u^{\prime}|)\cdot|H|\,dr_{*}d\omega+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{-\infty}^{(r_{3})*}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}||H|\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\flat}}\int_{(R_{3})*}^{\infty}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}|u^{\prime}||H|\,dr_{*}d\omega\\
+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\left(\Omega^{2}|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}+\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1-2\epsilon}\xi^{2}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\right)\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau.

(8.2)

Proof.

The proposition concerns the frequency ranges
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
. In the case of
\mathcal{F}_{\sharp,{\rm time}}
, the estimate (
8.2
) follows directly from Corollary
6.2
.

We will consider the microlocal energy current
j_{2}^{y}[u]
with
y(r)=(1-r_{+}r_{c}^{-1})^{-2+s}(\rho_{+}(r)^{2-s}-\rho_{c}(r)^{2-s})
. Note that

y^{\prime}(r)=(2-s)r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(1-r_{+}r_{c}^{-1})^{-2+s}\left(\rho_{c}^{1-s}(r)+\rho_{+}^{1-s}(r)\right).

Integrating
(j_{2}^{y}[u])^{\prime}
then gives:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{\prime}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V)|u|^{2})-yV^{\prime}|u|^{2}\,dr_{*}=2(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty))-\int_{\mathbb{R}}y\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}.

There exist constants
c,C>0
such that for
s\geq 1
:

y^{\prime}(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\widehat{\omega}^{2}-V)|u|^{2})-yV^{\prime}|u|^{2}\\
\geq c(2-s)r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\left(\rho_{c}^{1-s}+\rho_{+}^{1-s}\right)(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\widehat{\omega}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{r_{*}\geq 0}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{r_{*}\leq 0})|u|^{2})\\
-Cr^{-2}\Omega^{2}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})|u|^{2}.

Now let
s=2-\delta
. Since moreover
\ell\leq L_{0}
, we can apply Theorem
7.19
where
\epsilon
is replaced by
2\epsilon
, to obtain for
\max\{0,1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}<p<\min\{2,1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}+\frac{3\epsilon}{2}
:

c\int_{\mathbb{R}}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\left(\rho_{c}^{-1+\delta}+\rho_{+}^{-1+\delta}\right)(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\widehat{\omega}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{r_{*}\geq 0}+\widetilde{\omega}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{r_{*}\leq 0})|u|^{2})=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}y\textnormal{Re}\,(u^{\prime}\overline{H})\,dr_{*}\\
+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p-\epsilon}\Omega^{2}\left(|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}+\rho_{+}^{1-\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1-\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-2}\xi^{2}|G_{\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\right)\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau,

with
\mathbf{1}
the indicator function.

The above estimate can then be combined with (
5.21
), (
5.25
) and Theorem
7.19
(with
\epsilon
replaced by
2\epsilon
) to control integrals of
|u|^{2}
, to obtain (
8.2
) with additionally the boundary term
2(\widetilde{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)+\widehat{\omega}^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty))
on the right-hand side. We then control the boundary terms by integrating
E(\chi_{T}j^{T}[u])^{\prime}
and
E(\chi_{K}j^{K}[u])^{\prime}
and apply Theorem
7.19
to absorb the non-trivial terms with a factor
\chi^{\prime}_{T}
and
\chi_{K}^{\prime}
and conclude (
8.2
).
∎

9.
Integrated energy estimates: physical space analysis

We will now prove Theorem
3.1
. The outline of this section is as follows:

•

We will first establish (
3.1
) for future-integrable
\psi
in §
9.1
.

•

Then, we will drop the assumption of future integrability and
derive
future-integrability of
\psi
in the case
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
in §
9.2
to conclude that (
3.1
) holds for
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
.

•

Finally, we will show that (
3.1
) also holds for
\kappa_{+}=0
or
\kappa_{c}=0
by making use of the fact that the constant
C
in (
3.1
) is uniform in
\kappa_{+}
and
\kappa_{c}
. We then conclude the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

9.1.
Integrated energy estimates for future-integrable solutions

We first apply Plancherel’s theorem in combination with Propositions
8.1
and
8.2
.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
\ell_{0},L_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and assume that
\psi
is a future-integrable solution to (
2.16
) with
A=\widehat{A}=-Qr^{-1}d\tau
that is supported on angular frequencies
\ell\geq\ell_{0}
. Let
\kappa_{+}\geq\kappa_{c}
and
\kappa_{c}\leq\kappa_{1}
, with
\kappa_{1}>0
. Assume moreover that
|q|\geq q_{0}>0
. Let
1<r_{H}<r_{I}<r_{c}
and consider the constants
\delta,\epsilon>0
. Let
\max\{0,1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell_{0}}\}<p<\min\{2,1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell_{0}}\}+\epsilon
if
\psi=\psi_{\leq L_{0}}
and
1<p<\min\{2,1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell_{0}}\}+\epsilon
if
\psi=\psi_{>L_{0}}
.

Then, for
\kappa_{1}
suitably small, there exist a constant
C=C(\epsilon,p,r_{H},r_{I},\mathbbm{h},\delta,\kappa_{1})>0
, such that:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|Y_{*}\psi|^{2}+|\psi|^{2}+\upzeta(r)(|T\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\rho_{+}^{2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\Omega^{-2}(\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{H}}|K_{+}\psi|^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq r_{I}}|K_{c}\psi|^{2})\\
+\rho_{+}^{2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\Omega^{-2}(|\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1-2\epsilon},\mathbf{1}_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}\}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}+\Omega^{2}(1-\upzeta)|T(rG_{\widehat{A}})|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau\Bigg].

(9.1)

If
\psi=\psi_{\leq L_{0}}
, with
L_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then we can omit the factor
\upzeta
in (
9.1
) at the expense of adding
L_{0}
-dependence in the constant
C
. If
\psi=\psi_{>L_{0}}
for suitably large
L_{0}
, we can set
\epsilon=0
.

Proof.

Recall from (
5.4
)–(
5.9
) that we can express

\displaystyle\widetilde{\uppsi}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{+}^{-1}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\uppsi,

\displaystyle G_{\widetilde{A}}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{+}^{-1}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}G_{\widehat{A}},

\displaystyle F_{\widetilde{A},\xi}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{+}^{-1}r_{*}-iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}F_{\widehat{A},\xi},

or, alternatively,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\uppsi}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{c}^{-1}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{2}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\uppsi,

\displaystyle G_{\widetilde{A}}=
