\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{c}^{-1}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{2}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}G_{\widehat{A}},

\displaystyle F_{\widetilde{A},\xi}=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{c}^{-1}r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{c}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}-iq\int_{2}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}F_{\widehat{A},\xi}.

We first restrict to
\psi=\psi_{\ell}
and omit the subscript
\ell
and derive estimates that are uniform in
\ell
, so that we can sum over
\ell
at the end of the argument.

We use that the Fourier transform operator:
\mathfrak{F}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and its inverse
\mathfrak{F}^{-1}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
are unitary operators and hence preserve inner products in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. We first apply (
8.1
) with
\ell>L_{0}
together with Plancherel’s theorem to obtain:

c\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|Y_{*}\widetilde{\uppsi}|^{2}+\upzeta(r)(|T\uppsi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\uppsi|^{2})+|\uppsi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+c\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{-2}|\underline{L}\uppsi|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\Omega^{-2}(|K_{+}\uppsi|^{2}+|Y_{*}\widetilde{\uppsi}|^{2})+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\Omega^{-2}(|\uppsi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\uppsi|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+c\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{-2}|L\uppsi|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\Omega^{-2}(|K_{c}\uppsi|^{2}+|Y_{*}\widetilde{\uppsi}|^{2})+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\Omega^{-2}(|\uppsi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\uppsi|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq\overbrace{E\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\chi_{r_{1}}\textnormal{Re}\,(K_{+}\uppsi\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+r\xi G_{\widehat{A}}})\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:I_{1}}+\overbrace{E\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-\chi_{r_{1}})\textnormal{Re}\,(K_{c}\uppsi\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+r\xi G_{\widehat{A}}})\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:I_{2}}\\
+\overbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{3}\}}\Re(2Y_{*}\widetilde{\uppsi}\overline{rF_{\widetilde{A},\xi}+r\xi G_{\widetilde{A}}})\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:I_{3}}-\overbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\Re(2Y_{*}\widetilde{\uppsi}\overline{rF_{\widetilde{A},\xi}+r\xi G_{\widetilde{A}}})\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:I_{4}}\\
-\overbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{1}\}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2\chi_{r_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\underline{L}(e^{iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\uppsi)\cdot e^{-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+rG_{\widehat{A}}})\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:I_{5}}\\
-\overbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{1}\}}\textnormal{Re}\,(2(1-\chi_{r_{1}})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}L(e^{-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\uppsi)\cdot e^{iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+rG_{\widehat{A}}})\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:I_{6}}\\
\ +C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\frac{1}{\eta}(|rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}|^{2}+|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2})+\eta(|\widetilde{\uppsi}|^{2}+|Y_{*}\widetilde{\uppsi}|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\frac{1}{\eta}\rho_{+}\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-2+\delta}\Omega^{2}(|rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}|^{2}+|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2})\\
+\eta(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\Omega^{-2}|Y_{*}\widetilde{\uppsi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau,

(9.2)

where
\eta>0
is a constant that we will take to be suitably small and we used that
r^{-2}\Omega\sim\rho_{+}(\rho_{+}+2\kappa_{+})
near
r=r_{+}
and
r^{-2}\Omega\sim\rho_{c}(\rho_{c}+2\kappa_{c})
near
r=r_{c}
.

Using that
F_{\widehat{A},\xi}
is compactly supported in
\tau
and applying (
5.2
) together with the local-in-time energy estimates from Theorem
2.6
(with
p=0
), we estimate:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}|^{2}d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

We also estimate using Theorem
2.6
:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\rho_{+}\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-2+\delta}\Omega^{2}|rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-1+\delta}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\delta}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\delta}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Furthermore, for suitably small
\eta>0
, we can absorb the
|Y_{*}\widetilde{\uppsi}|^{2}
term on the RHS of (
9.2
) into the LHS.

In the remainder of the proof, we will estimate the integrals
I_{1}
–
I_{6}
.

Estimating
I_{2}
and
I_{1}
:

We will estimate
I_{2}
. The estimates for
I_{1}
proceed entirely analogously, with the roles of
\rho_{+}
and
\rho_{c}
interchanged.

By (
5.2
) and the fact that
\dot{\xi},\ddot{\xi}
are supported in
\{0\leq\tau\leq 1\}
and
(1-\chi_{r_{1}})
vanishes when
r\leq r_{1}
, we can split and estimate:

|I_{2}|\leq C\left|\overbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}2\dot{\xi}(1-\chi_{r_{1}})\textnormal{Re}\,\left(K_{c}(\xi\psi)\cdot\overline{X\psi}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:I_{2,a}}\right|\\
+C\overbrace{\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}|K_{c}(\xi\psi)|\left[|\mathbbm{h}||T\psi|+\left(|\mathbbm{h}|+\left|\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}\right|\right)|\psi|\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{I_{2,b}}\\
+C\overbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\})\setminus\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}|K_{c}(\xi\psi)|\cdot|rG_{\widehat{A}}|\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:I_{2,c}}+\overbrace{\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}(1-\chi_{r_{1}})\textnormal{Re}\,(K_{c}\uppsi\overline{r\xi G_{\widehat{A}}})\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|}^{=:I_{2,d}}.

Notice first that we can estimate
I_{2,d}
by writing
K_{c}\psi=\upzeta K_{c}\psi+(1-\upzeta)K_{c}\psi
and integrating by parts in the
T
-direction to estimate the terms with a factor
1-\upzeta
, using that
\lim_{\tau\to\infty}|\uppsi|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)=0
, by the sufficient integrability assumption. We obtain:
We obtain:

I_{2,d}\leq\mu\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}\xi(1-\upzeta)|\uppsi|^{2}+\xi\upzeta|K_{c}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\mu^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}\xi\upzeta|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}+\xi(1-\upzeta)|TG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Now consider
I_{2,b}
. By Theorem
2.6
with
p=0
, we can estimate:

I_{2,b}\leq\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(|T\psi|^{2}+|\psi|^{2})+r^{-2}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Consider
I_{2,c}
. We can estimate

I_{2,c}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\})\setminus\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}\mu\xi^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})\Omega^{-2}|K_{c}\psi|^{2}+|\dot{\xi}|r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\})\setminus\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}\mu^{-1}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\delta}\rho_{c}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}+|\dot{\xi}|r^{2}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq\eta\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\})\setminus\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}\xi^{2}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\delta}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})\Omega^{-2}|K_{c}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\eta^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{(\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\})\setminus\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-\delta}\rho_{c}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{H}\}}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\rho_{c}d\tau.

The integral of
\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]
can be estimated as above, by applying Theorem
2.6
with
p=0
.

We are left with estimating
I_{2,a}
. We first integrate by parts in the
X
-direction to obtain:

I_{2,a}=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}2\dot{\xi}\chi_{T}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(XK_{c}(\xi\psi)\cdot\overline{\psi}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
-\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}2\dot{\xi}\frac{d\chi_{T}}{dr}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(K_{c}(\xi\psi)\cdot\overline{\psi}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{\mathcal{C}^{+}}2\dot{\xi}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(K_{c}(\xi\psi)\cdot\overline{\psi}\right)\,d\sigma d\tau.

(9.3)

The second term on the RHS of (
9.3
) is supported in
\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}\cap\{0\leq\tau\leq 1
, so its norm can immediately using Theorem
2.6
with
p=0
. The third term on the RHS of (
9.3
) can be estimated by

\int_{\mathcal{C}^{+}}\dot{\xi}(|\psi|^{2}+|K_{c}\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma d\tau,

which can be estimated via Theorem
2.6
with
p=0
after applying a Hardy inequality, making use of the compact support of
\dot{\xi}
.

We estimate the third term on the RHS of (
9.3
) as follows:

\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}2\dot{\xi}\chi_{T}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(XK_{c}(\xi\psi)\cdot\overline{\psi}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|\leq\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}T(\xi^{2})\chi_{T}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(XK_{c}\psi\cdot\overline{\psi}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|\\
+\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}\dot{\xi}^{2}\chi_{T}X(|\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|\\
\leq\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}T(\xi^{2})\chi_{T}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(XK_{c}\psi\cdot\overline{\psi}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|+C\left|\int_{\mathcal{C}^{+}}\dot{\xi}^{2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau\right|\\
+C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau.

The last two terms on the very RHS can be estimated as above. We estimate the first term by applying (
2.22
) to obtain:

XK_{c}\psi=O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{0})X(\Omega^{2}X\psi)+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{0})r^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{0})r^{-2}\psi+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})O_{\infty}(\rho_{c})K_{c}^{2}\psi\\
+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c})X\psi+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{0})rG_{\widehat{A}}.

Using the above identity and integrating by parts to deal with the second-order derivatives, we conclude that:

|I_{2,a}|\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{H}\}}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\rho_{c}d\tau.

Putting the above together, we conclude that:

|I_{2}|\leq\eta\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{1}\}}\xi^{2}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\delta}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})\Omega^{-2}|K_{c}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\eta^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{-\delta}\rho_{c}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\rho_{c}d\tau.

The first term on the RHS can be absorbed into the LHS of (
9.2
) for suitably small
\eta
.

The integral
I_{1}
can be estimated entirely analogously, with
\rho_{+}
taking on the role of
\rho_{c}
.

Estimating
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
:

We will estimate
I_{4}
. The estimate for
I_{3}
will proceed analogously, with the roles of
\rho_{c}
and
\rho_{+}
interchanged. Note first that:

Y_{*}\widetilde{\uppsi}\overline{rF_{\widetilde{A},\xi}+\xi rG_{\widetilde{A}}}=K_{c}\uppsi\cdot\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+\xi rG_{\widehat{A}}}-iq(\rho_{c}-r^{-1}\mathbbm{h})\uppsi\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+\xi rG_{\widehat{A}}}-2L\uppsi\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+\xi rG_{\widehat{A}}}.

The contribution of the first term to
I_{4}
can be estimated in the same way as
I_{2}
. We estimate the terms involving
rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}
by applying Young’s inequality and (
5.2
):

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left((-iq(\rho_{c}-r^{-1}\mathbbm{h})\uppsi-2L\uppsi)\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|\\
\leq C\Bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\dot{\xi}\xi(\Omega^{2}|X\psi|+\rho_{c}|\psi|)\left(\rho_{c}|X\psi|+\rho_{c}^{3}|T\psi|+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})\rho_{c}|\psi|\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\Bigg|\\
\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Furthermore,

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left((-iq(\rho_{c}-r^{-1}\mathbbm{h})\uppsi-2L\uppsi)\overline{r\xi G_{\widehat{A}}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|\\
\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\eta(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\Omega^{-2}((\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})|\uppsi|^{2}+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})^{-1}|L\uppsi|^{2})+C\eta^{-1}\rho_{c}^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p-2}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\eta(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\Omega^{-2}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})|\uppsi|^{2}+\eta\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{-2}|L\uppsi|^{2}+C\eta^{-1}\rho_{c}^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p-2}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

We conclude that

|I_{4}|\leq\eta\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\Omega^{-2}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})|\uppsi|^{2}+\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{-2}|L\uppsi|^{2}\\
+C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr+C\eta^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\rho_{c}^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p-2}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

The first integral on the RHS can be absorbed into the LHS of (
9.2
) for suitably small
\eta
.

The estimates for
|I_{3}|
follow analogously.

Estimating
I_{5}
and
I_{6}
:

Consider
I_{6}
. The estimates for
I_{5}
follow analogously. We write:

(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}L(e^{iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\uppsi)\cdot e^{-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+rG_{\widehat{A}}}=(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}L\uppsi\cdot\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+rG_{\widehat{A}}}\\
+iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\uppsi\cdot\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}+rG_{\widehat{A}}}.

We estimate the terms involving
rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}
by applying Young’s inequality and (
5.2
):

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{1}\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\textnormal{Re}\,\left((iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\uppsi-L\uppsi)\overline{rF_{\widehat{A},\xi}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|\\
\leq C\Bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\dot{\xi}\xi(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(\Omega^{2}|X\psi|+r^{-1}r^{-2}\Omega^{2}|\psi|)\left(\rho_{c}|X\psi|+\rho_{c}^{3}|T\psi|+r^{-2}\Omega^{2}|\psi|\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\Bigg|\\
\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{1}\}}\rho_{c}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Furthermore,

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{1}\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\textnormal{Re}\,\left((-iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\uppsi-L\uppsi)\overline{\xi rG_{\widehat{A}}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|\\
\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\mu(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(\Omega^{-2}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})^{3}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4}|\uppsi|^{2}+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})\Omega^{-2}|L\uppsi|^{2})\\
+C\mu^{-1}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})^{-1}\Omega^{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\mu(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4-p}\Omega^{-2}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})^{3}|\uppsi|^{2}+\mu(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{-2}|L\uppsi|^{2}\\
+C\mu^{-1}\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{2}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Therefore,

|I_{6}|\leq\mu\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4-p}\Omega^{-2}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})^{3}|\uppsi|^{2}+(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{-2}|L\uppsi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p-1}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau+C\mu^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{3}\}}\eta^{-1}\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{2}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

The estimate for
|I_{5}|
proceeds analogously

We conclude that (
9.1
) holds for
\psi_{>L_{0}}
.

The case
\psi_{\leq L_{0}}
proceeds entirely analogously, starting from Proposition
8.2
if we additionally assume that
|q|>q_{0}
, with the key difference being the range of
p
,
\max\{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\epsilon,\epsilon\}<p<\min\{1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},2\}
, and the addition of the following term on the RHS of the final estimate:

C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p-\epsilon}\left(\Omega^{2}|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}+\rho_{+}^{1-\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1-\epsilon}\xi^{2}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\right)\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau.

We estimate via Theorem
2.6
:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p-\epsilon}\Omega^{2}|rF_{\xi,\widetilde{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{E}_{p+\epsilon}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p+\epsilon}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr.\qed

Furthermore, there is no need for
\upzeta
when considering
\psi_{\leq L_{0}}
.

Corollary 9.2
.

Let
\ell_{0},N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\epsilon>0
and
\max\{0,1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell_{0}}\}<p<\min\{2,1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell_{0}}\}+\epsilon
. Then there exist a constant
C=C(\epsilon,p,r_{H},r_{I},\mathbbm{h},N)>0
, such that for
\psi=\psi_{\geq\ell_{0}}
:

\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq N}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{1+k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}\\
+\upzeta(r)(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}+1}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1-2\epsilon},\mathbf{1}_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}\}r^{-2}|r^{3}T^{k}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\\
+\Omega^{2}(1-\upzeta)|T^{k+1}(rG_{\widehat{A}})|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\Bigg]\,d\tau.

(9.4)

If
\psi=\psi_{\leq L_{0}}
, with
L_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then we can omit the factor
\upzeta
in (
9.4
) at the expense of adding
L_{0}
-dependence in the constant
C
.

Proof.

We apply standard elliptic estimates in the region
\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}
to estimates all higher-order derivatives in terms of
T
-derivatives of first-order quantities. Then we use that

[(g_{M,Q,\Lambda}^{-1})^{\mu\nu}(^{\widehat{A}}D)_{\mu}(^{\widehat{A}}D)_{\nu},T]=0
to be able to directly apply (
9.1
) with
\psi
replaced by
T^{k}\psi
.
∎

We can also apply red shift estimates to improve the estimate in Proposition
9.1

at the expense of making the constant in the estimate
\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}
-dependent
.

Corollary 9.3
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}>0
. Then there exists a constant

C_{\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}}=C_{\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}}(\epsilon,p,r_{H},r_{I},\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c},N)>0
, such that:

\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq N}\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[T^{k_{3}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}+1}T^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}+\upzeta(r)(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}+1}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2})+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|XT^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}+|T^{k_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}+|T^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C_{\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}}\left[\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}|rT^{k}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}+\Omega^{2}(1-\upzeta)|rT^{k+1}rG_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau\right].

(9.5)

If
\psi=\psi_{\leq L_{0}}
, with
L_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then we can omit the factor
\upzeta
in (
9.5
) at the expense of adding
L_{0}
-dependence in the constant
C
.

Proof.

The proof of red-shift estimates is standard. We can combine (
9.4
) with energy estimates in the regions
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
and
\{r\geq r_{I}\}
, and consider the vector field multipliers
\Omega^{-2}\underline{L}
and
\Omega^{-2}\underline{L}
, respectively. See
[
DR13
]
[§7] for derivation of red-shift estimates in a general setting. The additional zeroth- and first-order terms appearing in (
2.16
) with
q\neq 0
compared to
q=0
do not cause issues in the argument.
∎

9.2.
Verifying future integrability

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
2.16
) with
A=\widehat{A}
arising from initial data in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)\times C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
, with
G_{\widehat{A}}
compactly supported in spacetime. Assume moreover that
\psi=\psi_{\ell}
with
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Consider the set

\mathcal{A}=\{q\in\mathbb{R}\,|\,\textnormal{$\psi$ is future integrable}\}.

The aim of this section is to show that
\mathcal{A}=\mathbb{R}
if
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
.

Proposition 9.4
.

Let
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
. Then
(-q_{0},q_{0})\subset\mathcal{A}
for suitably small
q_{0}
.

Proof.

We can directly apply
[
Bes20
]
[Theorem 4.2] to conclude future-integrability of fixed-
\ell
solutions to (
2.16
) in the case
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
with
|q|\leq q_{0}
, for
q_{0}
sufficiently small (given initial data in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)\times C_{c}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
).

Alternatively, we can deduce future integrability of fixed-
\ell
solutions for
|q|\leq q_{0}
from integrated energy estimates in the
q=0
case (for fixed
\ell
) and applying the analogue of Corollary
4.2
. The required integrated energy estimates on sub-extremal Reissner–Nordström-de Sitter can be found in
[
Veg24
]
[Proposition 11].
∎

Proposition 9.5
.

Let
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
. Then
\mathcal{A}
is open.

Proof.

We assume that
|q|\geq q_{0}
and consider the operator
L_{q}
defined as follows:

L_{q}f=X(\Omega^{2}Xf)+r^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f-\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]f-2(1-\mathbbm{h})(T+iqr^{-1})Xf\\
-\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T^{2}f+\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)Tf+\left[iqr^{-1}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}+q^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}r^{-2}+iqr^{-2}(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]f.

A solution to (
2.16
) with
A=\widehat{A}
satisfies
L_{q}\psi=rG_{\widehat{A}}
.

Now we consider the operator
L_{q_{\tau}}
where
q_{\tau}=\xi_{\tau}q_{1}+(1-\xi_{\tau})q_{\star}
, with
\xi_{\tau}:[0,\infty)
a smooth cut-off function such that
\xi_{\tau}(\tau^{\prime})=1
for
\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau-\delta_{0}
and
\xi_{\tau}(\tau^{\prime})=0
for
\tau^{\prime}\geq\tau
, with
\delta_{0}>0
.

Then

(L_{q_{\tau}}-L_{q_{\star}})\psi^{\tau}=(q_{\star}-q_{\tau})\left[-2ir^{-1}X\psi^{\tau}+2ir^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T\psi^{\tau}-\left(ir^{-1}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}+(q_{\tau}+q_{0})r^{-2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}+ir^{-2}(1-\mathbbm{h})\right)\psi^{\tau}\right].

Hence,
L_{q_{\star}}\psi^{\tau}=rG_{\widehat{A}}+rG_{q_{\star},q_{\tau}}
, with

rG_{q_{\star},q_{\tau}}:=-(L_{q_{\tau}}-L_{q_{\star}})\psi^{\tau}.

Suppose now that
q_{\star}\in\mathcal{A}
. Since
G_{\widehat{A}}
is sufficiently integrable and
rG_{q_{\star},q_{\tau}}
is compactly supported in time, and hence also sufficiently integrable, we must have that
\psi^{\tau}
is future integrable.

There exists a constant
C>0
, such that
r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{q_{\star},q_{\tau})}|^{2}\leq C(q_{\star}-q_{1})^{2}(|X\psi|^{2}+r^{-4}|T\psi|^{2}+r^{-4}|\psi|^{2})
, so we can apply (
9.5
), using that
\ell
is bounded to omit the factor
\upzeta
, and estimate:

\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq K}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\geq 0}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[T^{k_{3}}\psi^{\tau}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq K+1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Z_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\psi^{\tau}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}\\
+\sum_{k\leq K}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|XT^{k}\psi^{\tau}|^{2}+|T^{k+1}\psi^{\tau}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{3}\psi^{\tau}|^{2}+|T^{k}\psi^{\tau}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}\\
\leq C_{\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\sum_{k\leq K}\mathcal{E}_{2}[T^{k}\psi^{\tau}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}|rT^{k}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}+(q_{\star}-q_{1})^{2}(|XT^{k}\psi^{\tau}|^{2}+|T^{k+1}\psi^{\tau}|^{2}+|T^{k}+\psi^{\tau}|^{2})\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau^{\prime}\Bigg].

For
|q_{\star}-q_{1}|
suitably small depending on
\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}
, we can absorb the terms with a factor
(q_{\star}-q_{1})^{2}
into the left-hand side. Here we use that
\ell
is bounded.

Using that
\psi^{\tau}=\psi
for
\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
and that the constant
C_{\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}}
does not depend on
\tau
, we can take the limit
\tau\to\infty
to conclude that
\psi
is future integrable and hence
q_{1}\in\mathcal{A}
.
∎

Proposition 9.6
.

Let
\kappa_{c},\kappa_{+}>0
. The set
\mathcal{A}
is closed in
\mathbb{R}
and (
9.1
).

Proof.

Consider a sequence
\{q_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\mathcal{A}
such that
q_{n}\to q\in\mathbb{R}
, i.e. solutions
\psi^{q_{n}}
to
L_{q_{n}}\psi^{q_{n}}=rG_{\widehat{A}}
arising from smooth compactly supported initial data are future integrable. We fix the initial data for
\psi^{q_{n}}
to be independent of
n
. We then need to show that the solution
\psi^{q}
to
L_{q}\psi=rG_{\widehat{A}}
with the same initial data as
\psi^{q_{n}}
is future integrable to conclude that
{q}\in\mathcal{A}
and hence, that
\mathcal{A}
is closed.

We consider the differences
\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}}
and
L_{q_{m}}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})=rG_{q_{m},q_{n}}
. We then apply (
9.5
) to estimate:

\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq K}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\geq 0}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[T^{k_{3}}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq K+1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Z_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}\\
+\sum_{k\leq K}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|XT^{k}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})|^{2}+|T^{k+1}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{3}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})|^{2}\\
+|T^{k}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}\\
\leq C_{\kappa_{+},\kappa_{c}}(q_{n}-q_{m})^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}(|XT^{k}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})|^{2}+|T^{k+1}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})|^{2}+|T^{k}(\psi^{q_{n}}-\psi^{q_{m}})|^{2})\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau^{\prime}.

It therefore follows that
\{\psi^{q_{n}}\}
is a Cauchy sequence in the Hilbert space corresponding to the norm on the right-hand side and must therefore converge with limit
\psi^{q}
. We conclude that
q\in\mathcal{A}
.
∎

Corollary 9.7
.

Let
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
. Then
\mathcal{A}=\mathbb{R}
and (
9.1
) and (
9.4
) hold for all solutions to (
2.16
) with
A=\widehat{A}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{A}
is open and closed and contained in
\mathbb{R}
, which is connected, we conclude conclude future integrability for fixed
\ell
, smooth, compactly supported initial data and a smooth and compactly supported (
9.4
). Therefore, (
9.1
) and (
9.4
) hold. By a standard density argument, we can then consider more general initial data and inhomogeneities
G_{\widehat{A}}
for which the norms on the right-hand side of (
9.1
) or (
9.4
) remain finite.
∎

By applying the uniformity in
\kappa_{+}
and
\kappa_{c}
of (
9.4
), together with the
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
result in Corollary
9.7
, we can take
\kappa_{+}\downarrow 0
or
\kappa_{c}\downarrow 0
.

9.3.
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}
-weighted energy estimates in physical space

In this section, we derive additional, physical-space based
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}
-weighted energy estimates in the regions
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
and
\{r\geq r_{I}\}
. The integrated energy estimate in Proposition
9.1
involves
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}
weights on both sides of the equation with an additional
\epsilon
-loss (only for bounded angular frequencies!) when comparing the powers appearing on the LHS.

In this section, we will show that for
|q|<\left(\frac{1}{2}\ell+\frac{1}{4}\right)
or, equivalently,
\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}>\sqrt{3}\left(\ell+\frac{1}{2}\right)
, we can remove this
\epsilon
-loss.

Proposition 9.8
.

Let
\psi
be as in Corollary
9.2
. Assume that
|q|<\left(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\ell_{0}\right)
, or equivalently,
\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell_{0}}>\sqrt{3}\left(\ell_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\right)
. Let
1<p<1+\sqrt{1-\frac{16q^{2}}{(2\ell_{0}+1)^{2}}}
. Then, there exists a constant
C=C(\delta,p,r_{H},r_{I})>0
, such that:

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\sum_{k\leq K}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq K}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}+1}T^{k_{3}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\\
+\upzeta\cdot(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}+1}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}+1}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\Omega^{-2}(\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{H}}|K\psi|^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq r_{I}}|K_{c}\psi|^{2})\\
+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\Omega^{-2}(|\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+|T\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq K}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}\rho_{c},\mathbf{1}_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}\}r^{-2}|r^{3}T^{k}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}+\Omega^{2}(1-\upzeta)|T^{k+1}(rG_{\widehat{A}})|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau.

(9.6)

Proof.

For the sake of convenience, we will assume that
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\equiv 0
in
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
and
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 0
in
\{r\geq r_{I}\}
. The general case follows by applying additionally the applying local energy estimates from Theorem
2.6
. Without of generality, we consider the region
\{r\geq r_{I}\}
. The region
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
can be treated entirely analogously.

By (
2.22
), with
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 0
, we obtain:

rG_{\widehat{A}}=X(\Omega^{2}X\psi)+r^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-2(T+iqr^{-1})X\psi-\left[\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}r^{-1}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}-iqr^{-2}\right]\psi.

Therefore,

-\chi_{R_{1}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left((r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}\overline{X\psi}\cdot rG_{\widehat{A}}\right)=-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}X\left(|\Omega^{2}X\psi|^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\chi_{R_{1}}X\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right)+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
+T\left(\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}\right)+\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\left[\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}r^{-1}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}-iqr^{-2}\right]\psi\overline{X\psi}\right)\\
=-\frac{1}{2}X\left[\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|\Omega^{2}X\psi|^{2}-\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\right]+T\left(\chi_{R_{1}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}\right)+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
+\frac{p}{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\left[\rho_{c}(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}))+\kappa_{c}(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}))\right]\chi_{R_{1}}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}\\
+\frac{1}{2}(2-p)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\left[\rho_{c}(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}))+\kappa_{c}(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}))\right]\chi_{R_{1}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\\
-q(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\chi_{R_{1}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(i\psi\overline{X\psi}\right)+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}(O_{\infty}(\rho_{c})+\kappa_{c}O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{0}))\chi_{R_{1}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\psi\overline{X\psi}\right)\\
+\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\chi_{R_{1}}}{dr}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|\Omega^{2}X\psi|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\chi_{R_{1}}}{dr}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2},

(9.7)

where we applied (
2.2
) and

2\Omega^{2}\left[r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+\frac{2\Lambda}{3}\right]=r^{-2}\Omega^{2}(O_{\infty}(\rho_{c})+\kappa_{c}O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}^{0}))

to arrive at the final equality.

We apply Young’s inequality together with
r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\leq C\rho_{c}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})
to estimate the LHS of (
9.7
) as follows:

2\left|(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}\overline{X\psi}\cdot rG_{\widehat{A}})\right|\leq\eta(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}+C\eta^{-1}\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}.

We now integrate both sides of (
9.7
) in
\{\rho_{c}\leq 2\rho_{c}(R_{1})\}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}
to obtain:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{\rho_{c}\leq 2\rho_{c}(R_{1})\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|X\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{\rho_{c}\leq 2\rho_{c}(R_{1})\}}p(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\left[(1-\eta)(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}))+\kappa_{c}O_{\infty}(\rho_{c})\right]\chi_{R_{1}}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}\\
+(2-p)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\left[\rho_{c}(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}))+\kappa_{c}(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{c}))\right]\chi_{R_{1}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}\cap\{\rho_{c}\leq 2\rho_{c}(R_{1})\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}|X\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{\rho_{c}\leq 2\rho_{c}(R_{1})\}}2(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|q||X\psi||\psi|\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{\rho_{c}\leq 2\rho_{c}(R_{1})\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})|X\psi||\psi|\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{\rho_{c}(R_{1})\leq\rho_{c}\leq 2\rho_{c}(R_{1})\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\eta^{-1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{\rho_{c}\leq 2\rho_{c}(R_{1})\}}\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

(9.8)

We can estimate the RHS of (
9.8
) by applying Corollary
9.2
, which imposes the restriction
p>1
. We will estimate the remaining terms on the RHS of (
9.8
) via a suitably Hardy inequality, which will introduce a restriction on
\beta_{\ell}
.

Without loss of generality, assume that
\psi
is supported on a fixed angular
\ell
-mode. Then we split using Young’s inequality:

2(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|q||X\psi||\psi|\leq\mu p(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{c}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}\\
+\left[\frac{q^{2}}{p\mu\rho_{c}}-(1-\eta)(2-p)r^{2}(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})\ell(\ell+1)\right]r^{-2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\psi|^{2}\\
+(1-\eta)(2-p)\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\psi|^{2}\\
\leq\mu p(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{c}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}\\
+\left[\frac{q^{2}-(1-\eta)p(2-p)\ell(\ell+1)}{p\mu\rho_{c}}\right](r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\psi|^{2}\\
+(1-\eta+O(\kappa_{c}))(2-p)\ell(\ell+1)(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\psi|^{2}.

We have that:

\frac{d}{dr}\left((r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\right)=-\left(1-\frac{p}{2}\right)r^{-2}\frac{d}{d\rho_{c}}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}=-(2-p)r^{-2}(\rho_{c}(1+O(\rho_{c}))\\
+\kappa_{c}(1+O(\rho_{c}))(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\\
=-(2-p)r^{-2}\rho_{c}^{-1}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}-\kappa_{c}(2-p)r^{-2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}+r^{-2}\left[\kappa_{c}O(\rho_{c})+O(\rho_{c}^{2})\right](r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}.

We can therefore estimate:

\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{c}}\rho_{c}^{-1}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}r^{-2}\chi_{R_{1}}|\psi|^{2}\,dr\leq(2-p)^{-1}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{c}}-\frac{d}{dr}\left((r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\right)\chi_{R_{1}}|\psi|^{2}\,dr+\ldots\\
\leq 2(2-p)^{-1}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{c}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\psi||X\psi|\,dr+C\int_{\rho_{c}(R_{1})}^{\rho_{c}(2R_{1})}|\psi|^{2}\,d\rho_{c},

where the terms in
\dots
contain an additional factor of
\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c}
.

Hence,

\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{c}}\rho_{c}^{-1}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}r^{-2}\chi_{R_{1}}|\psi|^{2}\,dr\leq 4(2-p)^{-1}\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{c}}\rho_{c}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}\,dr+C\int_{\rho_{c}(R_{1})}^{\rho_{c}(2R_{1})}|\psi|^{2}\,d\rho_{c}.

We therefore need:

\mu+\mu^{-1}\left[\frac{4q^{2}}{p(2-p)}-\ell(\ell+1)\right]<1

The LHS is minimized for
\mu=\sqrt{\frac{4q^{2}}{p(2-p)}-\ell(\ell+1)}
, in which case we are left with:

\frac{q^{2}}{p(2-p)}-\frac{1}{4}\ell(\ell+1)<1

or equivalently,
p(2-p)>4q^{2}(\ell+\frac{1}{2})^{2}
. Since
p(2-p)\in(0,1)
, this requires
|q|<\frac{1}{2}(\ell+\frac{1}{2})
, which corresponds to:

\beta_{\ell}=\sqrt{4\left(\ell+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}-4q^{2}}>\sqrt{3}\left(\ell+\frac{1}{2}\right).

Correspondingly, the allowed range for
p
is:

1<p<1+\sqrt{1-\frac{16q^{2}}{(2\ell+1)^{2}}}.\qed

9.4.
Proof of Theorem
3.1

Without loss of generality, we consider the electromagnetic gauge
A=\widehat{A}
. Then
D_{X}=X
,
D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
and
D_{T}=T+iqr^{-1}
(when acting on functions). Furthermore,
D_{Y_{*}}\widetilde{\psi}=Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}
. We will first remove the conditions
\kappa_{+}>0
and
\kappa_{c}>0
in Corollary
9.7
by appealing to the uniformity in
\kappa_{+}
and
\kappa_{c}
of the constants
C
in (
9.1
) and (
9.4
). We assume that
\psi=\psi_{\geq\ell}
.

Let
\kappa_{+}\geq 0
and
\kappa_{c}\geq 0
. Let
\{\kappa_{+,n}\}
and
\{\kappa_{c,n}\}
be sequences in
(0,\infty)
such that
\kappa_{+,n}\to 0
and
\kappa_{c,n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

Let
L_{q}
be the operator in the proof of Proposition
9.5
corresponding to the parameters
\kappa_{+}
and
\kappa_{c}
. Denote with
L_{q,n}
the operator corresponding instead to the parameters
\kappa_{+,n}
and
\kappa_{c,n}
.

Let
L_{q}\psi=rG_{\widehat{A}}
. Then
L_{q,n}\psi=rG_{\widehat{A}}+(L_{q,n}-L_{q})\psi
.

By multiplying
\psi
with a smooth cut-off function that is compactly supported in
0\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau+1
and equal to 1 on
0\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
and applying Corollary
9.7
, we obtain:

\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq K}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}+1}T^{k_{3}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+\upzeta(r)(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}+1}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}+1}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2})\\
+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}Y_{*}^{k_{2}}T^{k_{3}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sum_{k\leq K}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\rho_{+}^{2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\Omega^{2}|XT^{k}\psi|^{2}\\
+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}\Omega^{-2}(\mathbf{1}_{r\leq r_{H}}|KT^{k}\psi|^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq r_{I}}|K_{c}T^{k}\psi|^{2})\\
+\rho_{+}^{2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{2\epsilon}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})(\rho_{c}+\kappa_{c})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\Omega^{-2}(|T^{k}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T^{k}\psi|^{2}+|T^{k+1}\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq K}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{0}^{\tau+1}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1-2\epsilon},\mathbf{1}_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}\}r^{-2}|rT^{k}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}+\Omega^{2}(1-\upzeta)|T(rT^{k}G_{\widehat{A}})|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\right]\,d\tau^{\prime}\Bigg]\\
+C\sum_{k\leq K}\int_{0}^{\tau+1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1-2\epsilon},\mathbf{1}_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}\}r^{-2}|T^{k}(L_{q,n}-L_{q})\psi|^{2}\\
+\Omega^{2}(1-\upzeta)|T^{k+1}(L_{q,n}-L_{q})\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\Bigg]d\tau^{\prime}.

(9.9)

We can estimate:

|(L_{q,n}-L_{q})\psi|^{2}\leq(\kappa_{+,n}-\kappa_{+})^{2}(\rho_{+}^{2}|X^{2}\psi|^{2}+|X\psi|^{2}+|\psi|^{2})+(\kappa_{c,n}-\kappa_{c})^{2}(\rho_{c}^{2}|X^{2}\psi|^{2}+|X\psi|^{2}+|\psi|^{2}).

By applying a Grönwall inequality and standard elliptic estimates, there exists a uniform constant
C>0
, such that we can estimate for
1<p<\min\{2,1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}\}+\epsilon
:

\sum_{k\leq K}\int_{0}^{\tau+1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Bigg[\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}\rho_{c}^{1-2\epsilon},\mathbf{1}_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}\}r^{-2}|T^{k}(L_{q,n}-L_{q})\psi|^{2}\\
+\Omega^{2}(1-\upzeta)|T^{k+1}(L_{q,n}-L_{q})\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\Bigg]d\tau^{\prime}\\
\leq C\left[(\kappa_{+,n}-\kappa_{+})^{2}+(\kappa_{c,n}-\kappa_{c})^{2}\right]e^{C\tau}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}\leq N+2}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[X^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr.

For any fixed
\tau
and for initially smooth and compactly supported
\psi
, the right-hand side above is finite and vanishes in the limit
n\to\infty
.

Furthermore, as the constant
C
appearing on the RHS of (
9.9
) does not depend on
n
, we can conclude that (
9.9
) also holds without the third integral on the RHS and we obtain in particular future integrability of
\psi
.

By a standard density argument, we conclude that (
9.1
) and (
9.4
) also hold
without
the assumption of future integrability in the case of more general initial data and inhomogeneities for which the integrals appearing on the right-hand side of (
9.1
) and (
9.4
) are finite.

To include the energy flux term on the right-hand side of (
3.1
), we simply couple (
9.4
) with the physical-space based
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}
-weighted energy estimates of Proposition
9.8
.

Appendix A
A physical-space integrated energy estimate modulo zeroth-order terms

This section provides a purely physical-space-based energy method of deriving integrated energy estimates for (
2.23
)
with spacetime integrals of zeroth-order terms on the right-hand side
and may be read independently of the remainder of the paper. Furthermore, when restricting to sufficiently large angular frequencies
\ell
, the zeroth-order terms on the right-hand side can be removed to obtain a genuine integrated energy estimate.

The motivation for considering a purely-physical space based method is that, when reformulated in terms of
twisted energy currents
, it generalizes to spacetime backgrounds that are close to Reissner–Nordström spacetimes, and hence, it may be applied to derive higher-order energy estimates for quasilinear problems, where simply treating nonlinearities as inhomogeneous terms of a linear problem would result in a loss of derivatives. We refer the reader to the discussion and set-up in
[
DHRT22
,
DHRT24
]
.

In this section, we will assume that
\kappa_{c}=0
for the sake of convenience.
Let
\widetilde{\psi}
be a solution to (
2.23
), i.e.

r\Omega^{2}G_{\widetilde{A}}=Y_{*}^{2}\widetilde{\psi}-T^{2}\widetilde{\psi}+r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}-2iqr^{-1}T\widetilde{\psi}+\left[q^{2}r^{-2}-r^{-1}\Omega^{2}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}\right]\widetilde{\psi}\\
=Y_{*}^{2}\widetilde{\psi}-K_{+}^{2}\widetilde{\psi}+r^{-2}\Omega^{2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}+2iqr^{-1}K_{+}\widetilde{\psi}+\left[q^{2}\rho_{+}^{2}-r^{-1}\Omega^{2}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}\right]\widetilde{\psi}.

(A.1)

Define:

V_{q}(r):=q^{2}r^{-2}-r^{-1}\Omega^{2}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}.

In the proposition below, we obtain an integrated energy estimate up to a spacetime integral of
|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}
, supported away from the photon sphere at
r=r_{\sharp}
.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
r^{-1}\psi
be a solution to (
2.16
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\Lambda=0
. Denote with
r^{-1}\widetilde{\psi}
the associated solution corresponding to
A=\widetilde{A}
, which solves (
A.1
).

Then, for any
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(\mathbbm{h},q,\delta)>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}<\infty
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|T\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|K_{+}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}(r^{-3}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2})+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}r^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-1}|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+(1-\upzeta)|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\upzeta r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+Cq^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(\Omega^{-1}r)^{\delta}r^{-1}\rho_{+}r^{-2}|r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{T\psi}rG_{\widehat{A}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|.

(A.2)

Furthermore, there exists an
L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, so that if we assume in addition that
\psi=\psi_{\geq L}
, then the spacetime integral of
\upzeta r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}
and the
\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}
-integral of
q^{2}r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}
on the right-hand side can be removed.

Proof.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}_{r_{*}}\to\mathbb{R}
and assume that
r_{*}(r_{\sharp})=0
. We consider:

\textnormal{Re}\,\left((f^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}+2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi})r\Omega^{2}\overline{G_{\tilde{A}}}\right).

We can apply Lemma
5.7
with
\kappa_{c}=0
and with
\widetilde{\psi}
taking the role of
u
and
\ell(\ell+1)|u|^{2}
replaced by
|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}
, with the main difference being the appearance of additional total
T
-derivative terms. For this reason, we will focus on the following term:

-\textnormal{Re}\,\left((f^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}+2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi})\overline{(T^{2}\widetilde{\psi}+2iqr^{-1}T\widetilde{\psi}})\right).

Note that

-\textnormal{Re}\,\left(f^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T^{2}\widetilde{\psi}}\right)=-T\left[\textnormal{Re}\,\left(f^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\right)\right]+f^{\prime}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}

and that

-\textnormal{Re}\,\left(2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T^{2}\widetilde{\psi}}\right)=-T\left[\textnormal{Re}\,\left(2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\right)\right]+\textnormal{Re}\,\left(2fY_{*}T\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\right)\\
=T\left[\textnormal{Re}\,\left(2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\right)\right]+Y_{*}\left[f|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\right]-f^{\prime}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}.

Furthermore,

4\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}})=4T\left[\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})\right]-4\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fY_{*}T\widetilde{\psi}\widetilde{\psi})\\
=4T\left[\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})\right]-4Y_{*}\left[\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fT\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})\right]+4(fr^{-1})^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(iqT\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})+4\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fT\widetilde{\psi}Y_{*}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}}).

Note that the left-hand side is equal to minus the term on the very right-hand side, so we obtain:

4\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}})=2T\left[\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})\right]-2Y_{*}\left[\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fT\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})\right]+2(fr^{-1})^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(\widetilde{\psi}\overline{iqT\widetilde{\psi}}).

Hence, we conclude that:

-\textnormal{Re}\,\left((f^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}+2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi})\overline{(T^{2}\widetilde{\psi}+2iqr^{-1}T\widetilde{\psi}})\right)=T\left[\textnormal{Re}\,\left(2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\right)-\textnormal{Re}\,\left(f^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\right)+2\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})\right]\\
+Y_{*}\left[f|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-2\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fT\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})\right]-2fr^{-2}\Omega^{2}\textnormal{Re}\,(\widetilde{\psi}\overline{iqT\widetilde{\psi}}).

We are left with the following identity:

2f^{\prime}|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-f\Omega^{2}\frac{d}{dr}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-fV^{\prime}_{q}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-2fr^{-2}\Omega^{2}\textnormal{Re}\,(\widetilde{\psi}\overline{iqT\widetilde{\psi}})\\
=T\left[\textnormal{Re}\,\left(2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\right)-\textnormal{Re}\,\left(f^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\right)+2\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})\right]+{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
+Y_{*}\left[f|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-2\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fT\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})+f|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-f\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-fV_{q}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+f^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\right]\\
-\textnormal{Re}\,\left((f^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}+2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi})r\Omega^{2}\overline{G_{\tilde{A}}}\right).

Let
f(r_{*})=-1+(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}}
for
r_{*}\leq r_{*}(r_{3})
,
f(r_{*})=1-(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}}
for
r_{*}\geq r_{*}(R_{3})
and
f(r_{*})=\frac{2}{\pi}\arctan(\alpha r_{*})
for
r_{*}(r_{2})\leq r_{*}\leq r_{*}(R_{2})
. Then,

\Omega^{-2}f^{\prime}(r_{*})=\begin{cases}\frac{\delta}{2}r^{-2}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{\frac{\delta}{2}-1}\left[\rho_{+}(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}))+\kappa_{+}(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}))\right]\quad&(r\leq r_{3}),\\
\frac{\delta}{2}r^{-1-\delta}(1+O_{\infty}(r^{-1}))\quad&(r\geq R_{3})\end{cases}

and for
r_{*}(r_{2})\leq r_{*}\leq r_{*}(R_{2})
:

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>\frac{2\alpha}{\pi(1+\alpha^{2}r_{*}^{2})},

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{4\alpha^{3}r_{*}}{\pi(1+\alpha^{2}r_{*}^{2})^{2}},

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime\prime}(r_{*})=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{4\alpha^{3}(1-3\alpha^{2}r_{*}^{2})}{\pi(1+\alpha^{2}r_{*}^{2})^{3}}.

We can choose
f
in
(r_{3},r_{2})\cup(R_{2},R_{3})
such that
f
is a
C^{3}
function,
f^{\prime}>0
globally, and there exists a constant
C>0
such that globally:

\displaystyle|f^{\prime\prime\prime}(r_{*})|\leq

\displaystyle\>C|r_{*}|^{-3}.

See also Proposition
6.3
, where the same
f
appears.

Note that for
|r-r_{\sharp}|\leq 2\eta
:

|\arctan(\alpha r_{*})|\leq\alpha|r_{*}|\leq 2\alpha\Omega^{-2}(r_{\sharp})|r-r_{\sharp}|,

if we take
\eta
suitably small.

We can therefore apply Young’s inequality to estimate for
|r-r_{\sharp}|\leq\eta
:

|2fr^{-2}\Omega^{2}\textnormal{Re}\,(\widetilde{\psi}\overline{iqT\widetilde{\psi}})|\leq\epsilon\arctan^{2}(\alpha r_{*})r^{-2}\Omega^{2}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+\epsilon^{-1}q^{2}\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\\
\leq\epsilon\alpha^{2}r^{-2}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}\Omega^{2}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+Cq^{2}\epsilon^{-1}\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}.

We obtain for
|r_{*}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\alpha
and
r_{*}(r_{2})\leq r_{*}\leq r_{*}(R_{2})
:

-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}|\psi|^{2}\geq\frac{3}{2\pi(\frac{5}{4})^{3}}\alpha^{3}|\psi|^{2}

Taking
\eta>0
suitably small and
\alpha^{3}\gg\epsilon^{-1}
, we therefore obtain for
|r-r_{\sharp}|\leq\eta
:

|2fr^{-2}\Omega^{2}\textnormal{Re}\,(\widetilde{\psi}\overline{iqT\widetilde{\psi}})|\leq\epsilon\alpha^{2}r^{-2}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}\Omega^{2}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-\frac{3}{4\pi(\frac{5}{4})^{3}}\alpha^{3}|\psi|^{2}.

Furthermore, there exists a constant
c>0
, such that:

-f\frac{d}{dr}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\geq cr^{-3}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}.

Hence, taking
\epsilon=\alpha^{-\frac{5}{2}}
, we have that
\alpha^{3}\gg\epsilon^{-1}\gg\alpha^{2}
and we can integrate in
\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}
to obtain:

c\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})r^{-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+(1-\upzeta)|\psi|^{2}+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}r^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-1}|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\upzeta r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}+\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}r^{-2}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+2\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|K_{+}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+2\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
+C\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\sigma+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|(|{Y_{*}}\widetilde{\psi}|+|\widetilde{\psi}|)\,d\sigma dr.

(A.3)

We can add control over
T
-derivatives by introducing a function
y
and considering the vector field multiplier
-2y\overline{Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}}
.
By the computations above involving
T\widetilde{\psi}
together with Lemma
5.7
, we obtain:

y^{\prime}(|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2})-\Omega^{2}\frac{d}{dr}(y\Omega^{2}r^{-2})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-(yV_{0})^{\prime}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-2(fr^{-1})^{\prime}\textnormal{Re}\,(\widetilde{\psi}\overline{iqT\widetilde{\psi}})\\
=T\left[\textnormal{Re}\,\left(2fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\right)+2\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})\right]\\
+Y_{*}\left[f|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-2\textnormal{Re}\,(iqr^{-1}fT\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}})+y|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-\Omega^{2}r^{-2}y|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-V_{0}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\right]-2\textnormal{Re}\,\left(yY_{*}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot r\Omega^{2}\overline{G_{\tilde{A}}}\right).

Now take
y(r)=(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{3}
and combine the above estimate with (
A.3
), with
\alpha
suitably large to obtain:

c\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}((\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})r^{-3}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2})+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}r^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-1}|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+(1-\upzeta)|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\upzeta r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+2\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|K_{+}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+2\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
+C\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{i}}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma drd\sigma+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|(|{Y_{*}}\widetilde{\psi}|+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}r^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\psi}|)\,d\sigma dr.

(A.4)

We are left with estimating the energy fluxes on the RHS.

We evaluate:

-\textnormal{Re}\,\left((\chi_{r_{1}}\overline{K_{+}\widetilde{\psi}}+(1-\chi_{r_{1}})\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}})rG_{\widetilde{A}}\right)

to obtain:

\frac{1}{2}T\left[(1-\chi_{r_{1}})|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+\chi_{r_{1}}|K_{+}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+r^{-2}\Omega^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}-q^{2}((1-\chi_{r_{1}})r^{-2}+\chi_{r_{1}}\rho_{+}^{2})|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+r^{-1}\Omega^{2}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\right]\\
-Y_{*}\left[\chi_{r_{1}}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{K_{+}\widetilde{\psi}}\cdot Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi})\right]-Y_{*}\left[(1-\chi_{r_{1}})\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}}\cdot Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi})\right]\\
=-\textnormal{Re}\,\left((\chi_{r_{1}}\overline{K_{+}\widetilde{\psi}}+(1-\chi_{r_{1}})\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}})rG_{\widetilde{A}}\right)+\frac{d\chi_{r_{1}}}{dr}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{iq\widetilde{\psi}}\cdot Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}).

After integration, we obtain:

\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|T\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|K_{+}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\supp\frac{d\chi_{r_{1}}}{dr}}\epsilon|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+Cq^{2}\epsilon^{-1}|\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+Cq^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left((\chi_{r_{1}}\overline{K_{+}\widetilde{\psi}}+(1-\chi_{r_{1}})\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}})rG_{\widetilde{A}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|.

By combining the above estimate with (
A.4
), we obtain:

\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|T\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|K_{+}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}(r^{-3}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|T\widetilde{\psi}|^{2})+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}r^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-1}|Y_{*}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+(1-\upzeta=|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\upzeta r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+Cq^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}|rG_{\widehat{A}}|(|{Y_{*}}\widetilde{\psi}|+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}r^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\psi}|)\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left((\chi_{r_{1}}\overline{K_{+}\widetilde{\psi}}+(1-\chi_{r_{1}})\overline{T\widetilde{\psi}})rG_{\widetilde{A}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|.

We conclude the proof of (
A.4
) by applying Young’s inequality to estimate
|rG_{\widehat{A}}|(|{Y_{*}}\widetilde{\psi}|+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta}r^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-1}|\widetilde{\psi}|)
. Note that we can absorb the spacetime integral of
\upzeta|\psi|^{2}
into the spacetime integral of
|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}
on the left-hand if we restrict to
\psi=\psi_{\geq L}
with
L
suitably large and we apply a Poincaré inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. Similarly, we can absorb the
q^{2}r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}
flux term into the
r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}
term of
\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi]
when
\psi=\psi_{\geq L}
.
∎

We can further improve the above integrated estimates by coupling them to the
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}
-estimates from Proposition
9.8
modulo zeroth-order terms:

Corollary A.2
.

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
2.22
). Let
0<p<2
and
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
r_{+}<r_{H}<r_{I}<\infty
.

Then there exists a constant
C=C(\mathbbm{h},p,q,r_{H},r_{I})>0
such that for all
0\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}<\infty
:

\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma drd\sigma+\int_{\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|T^{k+1}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau+\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}}|K_{+}T^{k}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma d\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\notin(r_{H},r_{I})\}}\mathcal{E}_{p-1}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\in(r_{H},r_{I})\}}(1-r^{-1}r_{\sharp})^{2}(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}+1}T^{k_{2}}Y_{*}^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}+1}Y_{*}^{k_{3}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2})\\
+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}Y_{*}^{k_{3}+1}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}T^{k_{2}}Y_{*}^{k_{3}}\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{r^{-1}\rho_{+}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\Omega^{-2},r^{-2}\}\upzeta|\psi|^{2}\,dr+Cq^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T^{k}\psi]\,d\sigma drd\sigma+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\rho_{+}r^{-1}(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}r^{-2}|T^{k}(r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\left|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{T^{k+1}\psi}rT^{k}G_{\widehat{A}}\right)\,d\sigma drd\tau\right|.

(A.5)

Furthermore, there exists an
L\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, so that if we assume in addition that
\psi=\psi_{\geq L}
, then the spacetime integral of
\upzeta r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}
and the
\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}
-integral of
q^{2}r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}
on the right-hand side can be removed.

Appendix B
Restricted mode stability for sub-extremal Reissner–Nordström away from extremality

In this section, we establish an additional mode stability result on sub-extremal Reissner–Nordström for a restricted set of frequencies. This result is independent from the rest of the paper and is not involved in the remaining propositions. The aim of this section is to elucidate the extent to which standard methods for proving mode stability apply.

Theorem B.1
.

Let
\kappa_{c}=0
,
\kappa_{+}>0
and
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat,\sim}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat,+}
with
q\omega\notin(0,\frac{q^{2}}{r_{+}+r_{-}})
. Then there exists a constant
K_{\kappa_{+}}=K_{\kappa_{+}}(\kappa_{+},\gamma,L_{0})>0
such that:

|\mathfrak{W}|\gtrsim K_{\kappa_{+}}.

Proof.

It is straightforward to show that the quantity
\widetilde{R}(r)=e^{-\gamma r}(r-r_{+})^{-\xi}(r-r_{-})^{-\eta}R
, with
R=r^{-1}u
, satisfies the equation:

\mathcal{T}_{r}(\widetilde{R})=e^{-\gamma r}(r-r_{+})^{-\xi}(r-r_{-})^{-\eta}rH,

(B.1)

with
\mathcal{T}_{r}
a confluent Heun operator, defined as follows:

\mathcal{T}_{r}:=(r-r_{+})(r-r_{-})\frac{d^{2}}{dr^{2}}+\left[(2\eta+1)(r-r_{+})+(2\xi+1)(r-r_{-})+2\gamma(r-r_{+})(r-r_{-})\right]\frac{d}{dr}\\
+(2\gamma r-L)\mathbf{1},

where

\displaystyle\eta=

\displaystyle\>i\frac{\omega r_{-}^{2}-qr_{-}}{r_{+}-r_{-}},

\displaystyle\xi=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\omega r_{+}^{2}-qr_{+}}{r_{+}-r_{-}},

\displaystyle\gamma=

\displaystyle-i\omega,

\displaystyle L=

\displaystyle\>\ell(\ell+1)-iq.

In Kerr spacetimes with
a^{2}<M^{2}
,
\widetilde{R}=e^{-\gamma r}(r-r_{+})^{-\xi}(r-r_{-})^{-\eta}R
also satisfies (
B.1
), but with
(\eta,\xi,\gamma,L)
replaced by
(\eta_{\rm Kerr},\xi_{\rm Kerr},\gamma_{\rm Kerr},L_{\rm Kerr})
, where

\displaystyle\eta_{\rm Kerr}=

\displaystyle\>i\frac{\omega(r_{-}^{2}+a^{2})-am}{r_{+}-r_{-}},

\displaystyle\xi_{\rm Kerr}=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\omega(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2})-am}{r_{+}-r_{-}},

\displaystyle\gamma_{\rm Kerr}=

\displaystyle-i\omega,

\displaystyle L_{\rm Kerr}=

\displaystyle\>\lambda+a^{2}\omega^{2}-2am\omega,

see
[
TdC20
]
[Eq. (3.22)].

Note that in Ker
r_{\pm}=M\pm\sqrt{M^{2}-a^{2}}
, whereas in Reissner–Nordström
r_{\pm}=M\pm\sqrt{M^{2}-Q^{2}}
. With the translations
a\leftrightarrow Q
and
(\eta_{\rm Kerr},\xi_{\rm Kerr},L_{\rm Kerr})\leftrightarrow(\eta,\xi,L)
, the confluent Heun operators are therefore are the same, so we can directly apply methods from the sub-extremal Kerr setting.

In particular, it follows from
[
TdC20
]
[Proposition 3.8] that

\mathbb{U}(x):=\lim_{y\to 0}(x^{2}+Q^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(x-r_{+})^{\xi+\eta}e^{\gamma x}\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}e^{-2\gamma(r_{+}-r_{-})^{-1}(x+iy-r_{-})(r-r_{-})}(r-r_{-})^{\eta}(r-r_{+})^{\xi}e^{-\gamma r}R(r)\,dr

is well-defined as a limit in
L^{2}_{x}([r_{+},\infty))
and is a smooth solution to the ODE

\frac{d^{2}\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}^{2}}+\mathbb{V}\mathbb{U}=\frac{(x-r_{-})(x-r_{+})}{x^{2}+Q^{2}}\mathbb{H},

with
\frac{dx_{*}}{dx}=\frac{x^{2}+Q^{2}}{(x-r_{-})(x-r_{+})}
,

\mathbb{H}(x)=\lim_{y\to 0}(x^{2}+Q^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(x-r_{+})^{\xi+\eta}e^{\gamma x}\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}e^{-2\gamma(r_{+}-r_{-})^{-1}(x+iy-r_{-})(r-r_{-})}(r-r_{-})^{\eta}(r-r_{+})^{\xi}e^{-\gamma r}(r\Omega^{-2}H)\,dr

and for
M=1
:

\mathbb{V}(x)=\frac{x-r_{-}}{(x^{2}+Q^{2})^{2}}\Big[(r_{+}-r_{-})^{-1}\omega^{2}((r_{+}-r_{-})x+r_{-}^{2}+3Q^{2})(x-r_{-})^{2}-\ell(\ell+1)(x-r_{+})+q^{2}(x-r_{-})\\
-4q\omega(r_{+}-r_{-})^{-1}(x-r_{-})^{2}-(x^{2}+Q^{2})^{-2}(x-r_{+})(2x^{3}+Q^{2}x^{2}-4Q^{2}x+Q^{4})\Big],

which can be derived analogously to the Kerr case in the proof of
[
TdC20
]
[Proposition 3.8]. Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\omega^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(r_{+})=

\displaystyle\>\frac{(r_{+}-r_{-})^{2}}{4r_{+}^{2}}\left((r_{+}+r_{-})\omega-q\right)^{2}.

In comparison, in the Kerr case we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\omega^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(r_{+})=

\displaystyle\>\frac{(r_{+}-r_{-})^{2}}{4r_{+}^{2}}\left(2\omega\right)^{2}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\left[\frac{d\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}}-i\omega\mathbb{U}\right](\infty)=

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle\left[\frac{d\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}}+i\left(\omega-\frac{q}{r_{+}+r_{-}}\right)\frac{r_{+}-r_{-}}{r_{+}}\mathbb{U}\right](-\infty)=

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{U}|^{2}(\infty)=

\displaystyle\>|\Gamma(2\xi+1)|^{2}\left|\frac{r_{+}-r_{-}}{2\omega}\right|^{2}r_{+}^{-2}|u(r_{+})|^{2}.

Defining
\mathbb{J}^{T}[\tilde{u}]:=-\omega\textnormal{Re}\,(i\frac{d\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}}\overline{\mathbb{U}})
, and using that
\mathbb{V}
is real, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx_{*}}\left(\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}]\right)=-\omega\frac{(x-r_{-})(x-r_{+})}{x^{2}+Q^{2}}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{i\mathbb{H}}\mathbb{U}).

By applying the above boundary conditions on
\tilde{u}
, we moreover obtain for
\omega\widetilde{\omega}<0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}\left(\left|\frac{d\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}}\right|^{2}(\infty)+\omega^{2}|\mathbb{U}|^{2}(\infty)\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](-\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}\left(\left|\frac{d\mathbb{U}}{dx_{*}}\right|^{2}(-\infty)+\frac{r_{+}-r_{-}}{r_{+}}\omega\left(\omega-\frac{q}{r_{+}+r_{-}}\right)|\mathbb{U}|^{2}(-\infty)\right),

so, for
\omega\left(\omega-\frac{q}{r_{+}+r_{-}}\right)>0
, we obtain:

|\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](-\infty)|+|\mathbb{J}^{T}[\mathbb{U}](\infty)|\leq\left|\omega\int_{\mathbb{R}_{x_{*}}}\frac{(x-r_{-})(x-r_{+})}{x^{2}+Q^{2}}\textnormal{Re}\,(\overline{i\mathbb{H}}\mathbb{U})\,dx_{*}\right|.

The proof of the Wronskian estimate in the
|a|<M
case in
[
TdC20
]
[Theorem 5.1] then still applies when
\omega\left(\omega-\frac{q}{r_{+}+r_{-}}\right)>0
.

Note that in the sub-extremal Kerr setting, the factor
\omega-\frac{q}{r_{+}+r_{-}}
above is instead simply
\omega
, which ensures mode stability everywhere away from
\omega=0
.
∎

Appendix C
ODE error estimates

In Proposition
C.2
, we will derive general error estimates for solutions to Schrödinger ODEs with perturbed potentials.

The following lemma concerning Volterra integrals forms the main ingredient for these error estimates and is proved in
[
Olv97
]
[Theorem 6.10.2].

Lemma C.1

(
[
Olv97
]
[Theorem 6.10.2])
.

Let
x\in(x_{1},x_{2})
, with
-\infty\leq x_{1}<x_{2}\leq\infty
and consider the integral equation:

\varepsilon(x)=\int_{x_{1}}^{x}K(x,y)\vartheta(y)\left[W(y)+\varepsilon(y)\right]\,dy,

(C.1)

with
K,W
continuous complex-valued functions, such that:

\displaystyle K(x,x)=

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle|K(x,y)|\leq

\displaystyle\>P_{0}(x)Q(y)\quad y\in(x_{1},x),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial K}{\partial x}(x,y)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>P_{1}(x)Q(y)\quad y\in(x_{1},x),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial^{2}K}{\partial x^{2}}(x,y)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>P_{2}(x)Q(y)\quad y\in(x_{1},x)

for some continuous functions
Q
and
P_{\mu}
,
\mu\in\{0,1,2\}
.

Assume moreover that:

\displaystyle\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{2}}|\vartheta(y)|\,dy<\infty,

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x_{2})}Q(y)|W(y)|<\infty,

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x_{2})}P_{0}(y)|Q(y)|<\infty.

Then
\varepsilon
satisfies the following estimates: for all
x\in(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon(x)}{P_{0}(x)}\leq\frac{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}Q(y)|W(y)|}{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}P_{0}(y)Q(y)}\left(e^{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}P_{0}(y)Q(y)\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{2}}|\vartheta(y)|\,dy}-1\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{d\varepsilon}{dx}(x)}{P_{1}(x)}\leq\frac{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}Q(y)|W(y)|}{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}P_{0}(y)Q(y)}\left(e^{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}P_{0}(y)Q(y)\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{2}}|\vartheta(y)|\,dy}-1\right).

Proposition C.2
.

Consider the following differential equation in the interval
(x_{1},x_{2})
with
-\infty\leq x_{1}<x_{2}\leq\infty
:

U^{\prime\prime}(x)=\left[\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)+\vartheta(x)\right]U(x),

(C.2)

with
\mathcal{V}_{0}
and
\vartheta
smooth functions. Assume that there exist solutions
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
to (
C.2
) without the
\vartheta
term, such that their Wronskian
\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})
is non-vanishing.

Let
f:(x_{1},x_{2})\to(0,\infty)
and assume moreover that there exists a constant
C>0
and functions

P_{0},P_{1},Q:(x_{1},x_{2})\to[0,\infty)
such that:

\displaystyle|f(x)W_{1}(x)f(y)W_{2}(y)|+|f(y)W_{1}(y)f(x)W_{2}(x)|\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{0}(x)Q(y)\quad y\in(x_{1},x_{2}),

\displaystyle\left|(fW_{1})^{\prime}(x)(fW_{2})(y)\right|+\left|(fW_{1})(y)(fW_{2})^{\prime}(x)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{1}(x)Q(y)\quad y\in(x_{1},x_{2}),

\displaystyle\left|f^{\prime}(x)W_{1}^{\prime}(x)f(y)W_{2}(y)\right|+\left|f(y)W_{1}(y)f^{\prime}(x)W_{2}^{\prime}(x)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{2}(x)Q(y)\quad y\in(x_{1},x_{2}),

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in(x_{1},x_{2})}(|f(x)W_{1}|(x)+|f(x)W_{2}|(x))Q(x)<

\displaystyle\>\infty,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in(x_{1},x_{2})}P_{0}(x)Q(x)<

\displaystyle\>\infty,

\displaystyle\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{2}}\frac{|\vartheta|(y)}{f^{2}(y)}\,dy<

\displaystyle\>\infty.

Then, there exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
such that

fU=C_{1}(fW_{1}+f\varepsilon_{1})+C_{2}(fW_{2}+f\varepsilon_{2}),

where
\varepsilon_{i}
,
i=1,2
, satisfy for all
x\in(x_{1},x_{2})
: there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|f(x)\varepsilon_{i}\right|(x)\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{0}(x)\frac{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}|fW_{i}|(y)Q(y)}{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}P_{0}(y)Q(y)}\left(e^{\frac{C}{|\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})|}\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}P_{0}(y)Q(y)\int_{x_{1}}^{x}\frac{|\vartheta(y)|}{f^{2}(y)}\,dy}-1\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\frac{d(f\varepsilon_{i})}{dx}\right|(x)}{P_{1}(x)}\leq

\displaystyle\>C\frac{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}|fW_{i}|(y)Q(y)}{\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}P_{0}(y)Q(y)}\left(e^{\frac{C}{|\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})|}\sup_{y\in(x_{1},x)}P_{0}(y)Q(y)\int_{x_{1}}^{x}\frac{|\vartheta(y)|}{f^{2}(y)}\,dy}-1\right).

The above estimates hold also with the interval
(x_{1},x)
in the suprema and the integrals replaced with
(x,x_{2})
.

Proof.

We write
U(x)=C_{1}(W_{1}(x)+\varepsilon_{1}(x))+C_{2}(W_{2}(x)+\varepsilon_{2}(x))
, with
C_{\pm}\in\mathbb{C}
. Then
U
is a solution to (
C.2
) if:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{V}_{0}\varepsilon_{1}=

\displaystyle\>\vartheta(\varepsilon_{1}+W_{1}),

(C.3)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{2}^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{V}_{0}\varepsilon_{2}=

\displaystyle\>\vartheta(\varepsilon_{2}+W_{2}).

(C.4)

Consider the following Wronskian:
\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})=W_{1}W_{2}^{\prime}-W_{1}^{\prime}W_{2}
, which is moreover constant in
x
. Then, by Green’s formula (variation of parameters), (
C.3
) and (
C.4
) are satisfied and
\varepsilon_{i}(x_{1})=0
and:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}(x)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})}\int_{x_{1}}^{x}(W_{2}(x)W_{1}(y)-W_{1}(x)W_{2}(y))\vartheta(y)(\varepsilon_{1}(y)+W_{1}(y))\,dy,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{2}(x)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})}\int_{x_{1}}^{x}(W_{1}(x)W_{2}(y)-W_{2}(x)W_{1}(y))\vartheta(y)(\varepsilon_{2}(y)+W_{2}(y))\,dy.

We can insert the function
f
in the above integral equalities in the following way:

\displaystyle f(x)\varepsilon_{1}(x)=

\displaystyle\>\int_{x_{1}}^{x}K(x,y)\frac{\vartheta(y)}{f^{2}(y)}(f(y)\varepsilon_{1}(y)+f(y)W_{1}(y))\,dy,

\displaystyle f(x)\varepsilon_{2}(x)=

\displaystyle\>\int_{x_{1}}^{x}(-K(x,y)))\frac{\vartheta(y)}{f^{2}(y)}(f(y)\varepsilon_{2}(y)+f(y)W_{2}(y))\,dy,

with
K(x,y)=\frac{1}{\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})}[f(x)W_{2}(x)f(y)W_{1}(y)-f(x)W_{1}(x)f(y)W_{2}(y)]
.

Note that
K(x,x)=0
. By the assumptions on
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
, we can estimate

\displaystyle|\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})||K(x,y)|\leq

\displaystyle\>|f(x)W_{1}(x)f(y)W_{2}(y)|+|f(y)W_{1}(y)f(x)W_{2}(x)|\leq CP_{0}(x)Q(y)\quad y\in(x_{1},x),

\displaystyle|\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})|\left|\frac{\partial K}{\partial x}(x,y)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>\left|(fW_{1})^{\prime}(x)f(y)W_{2}(y)\right|+\left|f(y)W_{1}(y)(fW_{2})^{\prime}(x)\right|\leq CP_{1}(x)Q(y)\quad y\in(x_{1},x)

Furthermore,

|\mathcal{W}(W_{1},W_{2})|\left|\frac{\partial^{2}K}{\partial x^{2}}(x,y)\right|\leq|\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)|(|f(x)W_{1}(x)f(y)W_{2}(y)|+|f(y)W_{1}(y)f(x)W_{2}(x)|)\\
+\left|(f^{\prime}(x)W_{1}^{\prime}(x)f(y)W_{2}(y)\right|+\left|f(y)W_{1}(y)f^{\prime}(x)W_{2}^{\prime}(x)\right|\\
\leq(|\mathcal{V}_{0}(x)|P_{0}(x)+P_{2}(x))Q(y)\quad y\in(x_{1},x).

We can now apply Lemma
C.1
to conclude the estimates in the proposition. One may repeat the above argument with the interval
(x_{1},x)
replaced by
(x,x_{2})
and with
\varepsilon_{i}(x_{2})=0
.
∎

Lemma C.3
.

Consider the Whittaker equation

\frac{d^{2}U}{d\xi^{2}}=\left[-\frac{1}{4}-\sigma\xi^{-1}+\left(\mu^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\xi^{-2}\right]U,

(C.5)

with either
\xi\in(0,\infty)
or
\xi\in(-\infty,0)
and
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mu\in[0,\infty)\times i(0,\infty)
.

Then there exist solutions
W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)
and
M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)
to (
C.5
) (Whittaker functions), with principal branches corresponding to the range
\arg z\in(-\pi,\pi]
that satisfy the following properties:

\displaystyle W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)=

\displaystyle\>e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\xi)\frac{\pi}{2}\sigma}e^{-i\frac{\xi}{2}}|\xi|^{-i\sigma}(1+O(|\xi|^{-1}))\quad|\xi|>1,

(C.6)

\displaystyle M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu-i\sigma)}e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\xi)\pi(\sigma-i\mu-\frac{i}{2})}W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)+\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma)}e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\xi)\pi\sigma}W_{i\sigma,\mu}(-i\xi),

(C.7)

\displaystyle M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)=

\displaystyle\>e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\xi)i\frac{\pi}{2}(\frac{1}{2}+\mu)}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}(1+O(|\xi|^{-1}))\quad|\xi|\leq 1,

(C.8)

\displaystyle W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma)}M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)+\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma)}M_{-i\sigma,-\mu}(i\xi)\quad(2\mu\notin\mathbb{N}_{0}),

(C.9)

\displaystyle W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma)}M_{-i\sigma,-\mu}(i\xi)(1+O(|\xi|^{-1}))\quad|\xi|\leq 1,\quad(\textnormal{Re}\,2\mu>1),

(C.10)

\displaystyle W_{-i\sigma,\frac{1}{2}}(i\xi)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{\Gamma(1+i\sigma)}M_{\sigma,-\frac{1}{2}}(i\xi)(1+\log|\xi|O(|\xi|^{-1}))\quad|\xi|\leq 1,

(C.11)

\displaystyle W_{-i\sigma,0}(i\xi)=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{1}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}M_{-i\sigma,0}(i\xi)\left(\log\xi+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma)}+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\log|\xi|O(|\xi|)\right)\quad|\xi|\leq 1,

(C.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}

\displaystyle\left(W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi),M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)\right)=i\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma)}.

(C.13)

with
\Gamma
the Gamma function and
\gamma_{\rm Euler}
the Euler–Mascheroni constant. Here the constants in the Big-O notation can depend on
\sigma
and
\mu
.

Proof.

We apply standard properties of Whittaker functions, see for example
[
NIS
]
[§13.14] with the constant
\kappa
replaced by
-i\sigma
.
∎

Corollary C.4
.

Let
U
be a solution to

\frac{d^{2}U}{d\xi^{2}}=\left[-\frac{1}{4}-\sigma\xi^{-1}+\left(\mu^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\xi^{-2}+\vartheta(\xi)\right]U,

(C.14)

with either
\xi\in(0,\infty)
or
\xi\in(-\infty,0)
and
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mu\in[0,\infty)\cup i(0,\infty)
. Let
0\leq\xi_{0}\leq\infty
. Assume that:

\int_{|\xi|}^{\xi_{0}}(\eta^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d|\eta|<\infty.

Let

\displaystyle P_{0}(\xi):=

\displaystyle\>(\xi^{-2}+1)^{\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}.

Then there exist complex constants
B_{1},B_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
and a constant
C>0
that depends on
\sigma
and
\mu
, such that we can write:

U(\xi)=B_{1}(W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)+\varepsilon_{1}(\xi))+B_{2}(M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)+\varepsilon_{2}(\xi)),

with
\varepsilon_{i}(\xi)
, with
i\in\{1,2\}
, satisfying the following bounds:

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{i}\right|(\xi)\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\xi^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{4}}(1+\delta_{\mu 0}\log(2+\xi^{-2}))P_{0}(\xi)\left(e^{C\int^{\xi_{0}}_{|\xi|}(1+\delta_{\mu 0}\log^{2}(2+\eta^{-2}))(\eta^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d|\eta|}-1\right),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d\varepsilon_{i}}{d\xi}\right|(\xi)\leq

\displaystyle\>C(\xi^{-2}+1)^{\frac{1}{4}}(1+\delta_{\mu 0}\log(2+\xi^{-2}))P_{0}(\xi)\left(e^{C\int^{\xi_{0}}_{|\xi|}(1+\delta_{\mu 0}\log^{2}(2+\eta^{-2}))(\eta^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d|\eta|}-1\right).

Proof.

Let
W_{1}(\xi):=W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)
and
W_{2}(\xi):=M_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)
. Let
f(\xi):=(\xi^{-2}+1)^{\frac{1}{4}}
if
\mu\neq 0
and
f(\xi):=(\xi^{-2}+1)^{\frac{1}{4}}(\log(2+\xi^{-2}))^{-1}
if
\mu=0
.

We define
P_{i}
, with
i\in\{0,1\}
, and
Q
as follows:

\displaystyle Q(\xi):=

\displaystyle\>(\xi^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}\quad(\mu\neq 0)

\displaystyle Q(\xi):=

\displaystyle\>(\xi^{-2}+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}(\log(2+\xi^{-2}))^{-1}\quad(\mu=0),

\displaystyle P_{0}(\xi):=

\displaystyle\>(\xi^{-2}+1)^{\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\mu},

\displaystyle P_{1}(\xi):=

\displaystyle\>(\xi^{-2}+1)^{\frac{1}{2}}P_{0}(\xi).

It follows from the above asymptotic properties of
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
from Lemma
C.3
that:

\displaystyle|(fW_{1})(\xi)(fW_{2})(\eta)|+|(fW_{1})(\eta)(fW_{2})(\xi)|\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{0}(\xi)Q(\eta)\quad|\eta|\in(|\xi|,\infty),

\displaystyle\left|(fW_{1})^{\prime}(\xi)(fW_{2})(\eta)\right|+\left|(fW_{1})(\eta)(fW_{2})^{\prime}(\xi)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{1}(\xi)Q(\eta)\quad|\eta|\in(|\xi|,\infty),

\displaystyle\left|(f^{\prime}W_{1})(\xi)(f^{\prime}W_{2})(\eta)\right|+\left|(fW_{1})(\eta)(f^{\prime}W_{2})(\xi)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{1}(\xi)Q(\eta)\quad|\eta|\in(|\xi|,\infty),
