\displaystyle\sup_{|\xi|\in(|\xi_{0}|,\infty)}[|fW_{1}|(\xi)+|fW_{2}|(\xi)]Q(\xi)<

\displaystyle\>\infty\quad\textnormal{for any $\xi_{0}>0$},

\displaystyle\sup_{|\xi|\in(|\xi_{0}|,\infty)}P_{0}(\xi)Q(\xi)<

\displaystyle\>\infty\quad\textnormal{for any $\xi_{0}>0$},

Hence, the conditions in Proposition
C.2
with
x=|\xi|
and
x_{1}=\infty
are satisfied.

Note moreover that there exists a constant
C>0
, such that:

\displaystyle\sup_{|\eta|\in(|\xi|,\infty)}P_{0}(\eta)Q(\eta)=

\displaystyle\>\begin{cases}1\quad&\mu\neq 0,\\
(\log(2+\xi^{-2}))^{-1}&\mu=0,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\sup_{|\eta|\in(|\xi|,\infty)}|fW_{2}(\xi)|Q(\eta)\leq

\displaystyle\>C,

\displaystyle\sup_{|\eta|\in(|\xi|,\infty)}|fW_{1}(\xi)|Q(\eta)\leq

\displaystyle\>C

We can therefore apply Proposition
C.2
to conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma C.5
.

The Whittaker functions
W_{\mp i\sigma,\mu}(\pm i\xi)
have no zeroes for
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

Let
W(\xi)=W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(i\xi)
. Then, using that
W
solve a Schrödinger equation with a real potential, we obtain:

\frac{d}{d\xi}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(iW\frac{d\overline{W}}{d\xi}\right)=\textnormal{Re}\,\left(iW\overline{W}^{\prime\prime}\right)+\textnormal{Re}\,\left(i\left|\frac{d\overline{W}}{d\xi}\right|^{2}\right)=0

Hence, we have that for all
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
:

\textnormal{Re}\,\left(iW\frac{d\overline{W}}{d\xi}\right)(\xi_{1})=\textnormal{Re}\,\left(iW\frac{d\overline{W}}{d\xi}\right)(\xi_{2})

In particular,

\lim_{\xi\to\infty}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(iW_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-i\xi)\frac{d\overline{W}_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-i\xi)}{d\xi}\right)\\
=\lim_{\xi\to\infty}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(ie^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\xi)\frac{\pi}{2}\sigma}e^{-i\frac{\xi}{2}}|\xi|^{-i\sigma}(1+O(|\xi|^{-1}))\cdot\frac{i}{2}e^{-i\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\xi)\frac{\pi}{2}\overline{\sigma}}e^{i\frac{\xi}{2}}|\xi|^{i\overline{\sigma}}(1+O(|\xi|^{-1}))\right)=-\frac{1}{2}e^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(\xi)\pi\textnormal{Re}\,\sigma}.

Hence,
W_{-i\sigma,\mu}(-i\xi)\neq 0
for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. The same argument also applies to
W_{+i\sigma,\mu}(-i\xi)
.
∎

Lemma C.6
.

Consider the hypergeometric equation

\zeta(1-\zeta)\frac{d^{2}F}{d\zeta^{2}}+(c-(a+b+1)\zeta)\frac{dF}{d\zeta}-abF=0,

(C.15)

with
a,b,c\in\mathbb{C}
and
\zeta\in(0,1)
.

Define and assume that there exist constants
\gamma,\mu_{0}>0
, such that:

\displaystyle\mu:=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}(c-1)\in[0,\mu_{0})\cup i(0,\mu_{0}),

\displaystyle-iw:=

\displaystyle\>a+b-c\in i(-\gamma,\gamma),

\displaystyle v:=

\displaystyle\>ab-\frac{1}{2}c(1+a+b-c),

\displaystyle i\sigma_{a}:=

\displaystyle\>a-\frac{c}{2}\in i\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle i\sigma_{b}:=

\displaystyle\>b-\frac{c}{2}\in i\mathbb{R}.

We can express:

\displaystyle\sigma_{a}=

\displaystyle-\frac{w}{2}\pm\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-\mu^{2}+v+\frac{1}{4}w^{2}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{b}=

\displaystyle-\frac{w}{2}\mp\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-\mu^{2}+v+\frac{1}{4}w^{2}}.

Let
\tilde{s}:=\frac{\zeta}{1-\zeta}
and define
U(\tilde{s})=\zeta^{\frac{c}{2}}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{\frac{1}{2}(a+b-c-1)}F(\zeta(s))
.
Then
U
satisfies the equation

\frac{d^{2}U}{d\tilde{s}^{2}}=(1+\tilde{s})^{-2}\left[-\frac{1}{4}(1+w^{2})+v\tilde{s}^{-1}+\left(\mu^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\tilde{s}^{-2}\right]U.

(C.16)

Furthermore, the following functions are solutions to (
C.16
):

\displaystyle G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}):=

\displaystyle\>\zeta^{\frac{c}{2}}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{\frac{1}{2}(a+b-c-1)}{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta(\tilde{s})),

(C.17)

\displaystyle F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}):=

\displaystyle\>\zeta^{\frac{c}{2}}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{\frac{1}{2}(a+b-c-1)}{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b+1-c;1-\zeta(\tilde{s})).

(C.18)

where
{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b+1-c;1-\zeta)
and
{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta)
are Gauss hypergeometric functions, which solve (
C.15
) and satisfy:

\displaystyle{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b+1-c;1-\zeta)=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(a+k)\Gamma(b+k)\Gamma(a+b+1-c+k)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)\Gamma(a+b+1-c)}(1-\zeta)^{k}=1+O_{\infty}(1-\zeta),

(C.19)

\displaystyle{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta)=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(a+k)\Gamma(b+k)\Gamma(c+k)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)\Gamma(c)}\zeta^{k}=1+O_{\infty}(\zeta).

(C.20)

There exists a constant
C=C(\mu_{0},\gamma)>0
, such that the following Wronskian bounds hold:

\left|\mathcal{W}(F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu},G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu})\right|\geq C_{\gamma,\mu_{0}}^{-1}

and we can write:

\displaystyle F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))=

\displaystyle\>\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\Bigg[\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b})}G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))

(C.21)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}G_{\underline{\sigma},-\mu}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))\Bigg]\quad(2\mu\notin\mathbb{N}_{0}),

\displaystyle F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))=

\displaystyle\>\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\Bigg[-\frac{(-1)^{-2\mu}}{(2\mu)!}\log\zeta

(C.22)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}G_{\underline{\sigma},-\mu}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))(1+O_{\infty}(\zeta))\Bigg]\quad(2\mu\in\mathbb{N}_{1}),

\displaystyle F_{\underline{\sigma},0}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+i\sigma_{b})}G_{0,\sigma}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))

(C.23)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\Bigg[\log\zeta+\left(2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a)}{\Gamma(a)}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(b)}{\Gamma(b)}\right)+\log\zeta O_{\infty}(\zeta)\Bigg],

\displaystyle G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))=

\displaystyle\>\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)\Gamma(iw)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu-i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu-i\sigma_{b})}F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))

(C.24)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)\Gamma(-iw)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}F_{-\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))\quad(w\neq 0),

\displaystyle G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}\zeta^{\mu+\frac{1}{2}}(1-\zeta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}

(C.25)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\left(\log(1-\zeta)+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a)}{\Gamma(a)}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(b)}{\Gamma(b)}+O_{\infty}((1-\zeta))\right)\quad(w=0).

Proof.

We will derive the equivalence of (
C.15
) and (
C.16
) by starting with (
C.16
) and expressing
a,b,c
in terms of
\mu,w,v
.

We can express:
\tilde{s}=\frac{\zeta}{1-\zeta}
and
\frac{d}{d\tilde{s}}=(1+\tilde{s})^{-2}\frac{d}{d\zeta}=(1-\zeta)^{2}\frac{d}{d\zeta}
. We therefore obtain:

(1+\tilde{s})^{2}\frac{d^{2}U}{d\tilde{s}^{2}}=\frac{d}{d\zeta}\left((1-\zeta)^{2}\frac{dU}{d\zeta}\right)=(1-\zeta)^{2}\frac{d^{2}U}{d\zeta^{2}}-2(1-\zeta)\frac{dU}{d\zeta}.

Therefore, (
C.16
) is equivalent to:

0=\zeta(1-\zeta)\frac{d^{2}U}{d\zeta^{2}}-2\zeta\frac{dU}{d\zeta}+\left[\frac{1}{4}(1+w^{2})\frac{\zeta}{1-\zeta}-v-\left(\mu^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\left(\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta}\right)\right]U.

(C.26)

Let
-iw=a+b-c
. Then
U(\tilde{s})=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{\frac{1}{2}(-1-iw)}F(\zeta(\tilde{s}))
. Then we can write in terms of
F(\zeta)
:

\frac{dU}{d\zeta}=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}(1-\zeta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{iw}{2}}\frac{dF}{d\zeta}+\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu\right)\zeta^{-1}+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{iw}{2}\right)(1-\zeta)^{-1}\right]\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu}(1-\zeta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{iw}{2}}F

so

\zeta^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu}(1-\zeta)^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{iw}{2}}\frac{d^{2}U}{d\zeta^{2}}=\frac{d^{2}F}{d\zeta^{2}}+\left[\left(1+2\mu\right)\zeta^{-1}+\left(1+iw\right)(1-\zeta)^{-1}\right]\frac{dF}{d\zeta}\\
+\left[\left(\mu^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\zeta^{-2}+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu\right)\left(1+iw\right)\zeta^{-1}(1-\zeta)^{-1}+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{iw}{2}\right)\left(\frac{3}{2}+\frac{iw}{2}\right)(1-\zeta)^{-2}\right]F.

Therefore, (
C.26
) is equivalent to:

0=\zeta(1-\zeta)\frac{d^{2}F}{d\zeta^{2}}+\left[\left(1+2\mu\right)(1-\zeta)+\left(-1+iw\right)\zeta\right]\frac{dF}{d\zeta}-2\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu\right)+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{iw}{2}\right)\zeta(1-\zeta)^{-1}\right]F\\
+\left[\left(\mu^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\zeta^{-1}(1-\zeta)+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu\right)\left(1+iw\right)+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{iw}{2}\right)\left(\frac{3}{2}+\frac{iw}{2}\right)\zeta(1-\zeta)^{-1}\right]F\\
+\left[\frac{1}{4}(1+w^{2})\zeta(1-\zeta)^{-1}-v-\left(\mu^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\left(\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta}\right)\right]F\\
=\zeta(1-\zeta)\frac{d^{2}F}{d\zeta^{2}}+\left[-\left(2+2\mu-iw\right)\zeta+\left(1+2\mu\right)\right]\frac{dF}{d\zeta}-\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu\right)\left(1-iw\right)+v\right]F.

To obtain equivalence of (
C.16
) and (
C.16
), we must have that

\displaystyle c=

\displaystyle\>1+2\mu,

\displaystyle a+b+1=

\displaystyle\>2+2\mu-iw=c+1-iw,

\displaystyle ab=

\displaystyle\>\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu\right)(1-iw)+v.

Then
we also obtain

v=ab-\frac{c}{2}(a+b+1-c).

Write

\displaystyle a=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}+\mu-\frac{iw}{2}+i\lambda,

\displaystyle b=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}+\mu-\frac{iw}{2}-i\lambda.

Then we also obtain:

ab=\frac{1}{4}\left(1+2\mu-iw\right)^{2}+\lambda^{2}=\mu^{2}-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{4}\left(iw\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{1}{2}+\mu\right)\left(1-iw\right)+\lambda^{2},

from which it follows that

\lambda^{2}=\frac{1}{4}-\mu^{2}+v+\frac{1}{4}w^{2}

and
\lambda=\pm\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-\mu^{2}+v+\frac{1}{4}w^{2}}
.

If we write
a=\frac{c}{2}+i\sigma_{a}
and
b=\frac{c}{2}+i\sigma_{b}
, then

\displaystyle i\sigma_{a}=

\displaystyle-\frac{iw}{2}\pm i\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-\mu^{2}+v+\frac{1}{4}w^{2}},

\displaystyle i\sigma_{b}=

\displaystyle-\frac{iw}{2}\mp i\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-\mu^{2}+v+\frac{1}{4}w^{2}}.

Let
2\mu\notin\mathbb{N}_{0}
, which is equivalent to
c\notin\mathbb{N}_{1}
. Then:

\mathcal{W}(F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu},G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu})=\zeta^{c}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{a+b-c-1}\frac{d\zeta}{d\tilde{s}}\mathcal{W}\left({}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b+1-c;1-\zeta),{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta)\right)\\
=\zeta^{c}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{a+b-c+1}\mathcal{W}\left({}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b+1-c;1-\zeta),{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta)\right).

Furthermore, by
[
NIS
]
[§15.10(ii)] we obtain

\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b+1-c;1-\zeta)=\frac{\Gamma(1-c)\Gamma(a+b-c+1)}{\Gamma(a-c+1)\Gamma(b-c+1)}\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta)\\
+\frac{\Gamma(c-1)\Gamma(a+b-c+1)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}\zeta^{1-c}\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}(a-c+1,b-c+1,2-c;\zeta)

(C.27)

and by
[
NIS
]
[§15.10(i)]:

\mathcal{W}\left({}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c,\zeta),\zeta^{1-c}\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}(a-c+1,b-c+1,2-c;\zeta)\right)=(1-c)\zeta^{-c}(1-\zeta)^{c-a-b-1}.

Combining the above identities, we obtain:

\mathcal{W}(F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu},G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu})=(c-1)\frac{\Gamma(c-1)\Gamma(a+b-c+1)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}=\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)\Gamma(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b}))}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}.

Gamma functions have no zeroes and the product
\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})
has no poles. We conclude that there exists a uniform constant
C_{\gamma}>0
, such that

\left|\mathcal{W}(F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu},G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu})\right|\geq C_{\gamma,\mu_{0}}^{-1}.

Note that
G_{\underline{\sigma},-\mu}(\tilde{s})=\zeta^{1-\frac{c}{2}}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{\frac{1}{2}(a+b-c-1)}{}_{2}F_{1}(a-c+1,b-c+1,2-c;\zeta(s))
, so (
C.27
) implies that for
2\mu\notin\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s})=\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\left[\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b})}G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s})+\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}G_{\underline{\sigma},-\mu}(\tilde{s})\right].

(C.28)

Now let
2\mu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, or equivalently,
c\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
. Then we apply
[
NIS
]
[§15.8(ii)] to expand:

{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b+1-c;1-\zeta)=(1-\delta_{\mu 0})\zeta^{-2\mu}\frac{\Gamma(a+b+1-c)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}((2\mu-1)!+O_{\infty}(\zeta))\\
-(-1)^{2\mu}\frac{\Gamma(a+b+1-c)}{(2\mu)!\Gamma(a-2\mu)\Gamma(b-2\mu)}\left(\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(2\mu+1)}{\Gamma(2\mu+1)}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a)}{\Gamma(a)}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(b)}{\Gamma(b)}\right)\\
-(-1)^{2\mu}\frac{\Gamma(a+b+1-c)}{\Gamma(a-c+1)\Gamma(b-c+1)}\log\zeta\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(a+k)\Gamma(b+k)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)k!(2\mu+k)!}\zeta^{k}\\
=-\frac{(-1)^{-2\mu}}{(2\mu)!}\log\zeta F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta)+(1-\delta_{\mu 0})\zeta^{-2\mu}\frac{\Gamma(c-1)\Gamma(a+b-c+1)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}(1+O_{\infty}(\zeta))\\
-(-1)^{2\mu}\frac{\Gamma(a+b+1-c)}{(2\mu)!\Gamma(a-2\mu)\Gamma(b-2\mu)}\left(\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(2\mu+1)}{\Gamma(2\mu+1)}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a)}{\Gamma(a)}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(b)}{\Gamma(b)}\right)\\
=\begin{cases}-\frac{(-1)^{-2\mu}\Gamma(a+b)}{(2\mu)!\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}\log\zeta F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta)+\zeta^{-2\mu}\frac{\Gamma(c-1)\Gamma(a+b-c+1)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}(1+O_{\infty}(\zeta))\quad(2\mu\in\mathbb{N}_{1}),\\
-\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}\log\zeta F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta)-\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}\left(2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a)}{\Gamma(a)}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(b)}{\Gamma(b)}\right)+O_{\infty}(\zeta)\quad(2\mu=0).\end{cases}

We conclude that for
2\mu\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
:

F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s})=\Gamma\left(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b})\right)\Bigg[-\frac{(-1)^{-2\mu}\Gamma(a+b)}{(2\mu)!\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}\log\zeta(\tilde{s})\\
+\frac{\Gamma(-2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\mu+i\sigma_{b})}(G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s})+\log\zeta(\tilde{s})O_{\infty}(\zeta(\tilde{s}))\\
+\frac{\Gamma(2\mu)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}G_{\underline{\sigma},-\mu}(\tilde{s})\Bigg].

We conclude that, as in the
2\mu\notin\mathbb{N}_{0}
case:

\mathcal{W}(F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu},G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu})=\frac{\Gamma(1+2\mu)\Gamma(1+i(\sigma_{a}+\sigma_{b}))}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{a})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\mu+i\sigma_{b})}.

For
2\mu=0
, we obtain:

F_{\underline{\sigma},0}(\tilde{s})=-G_{\underline{\sigma},0}(\tilde{s})\left[\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}\log\zeta+\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}\left(2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a)}{\Gamma(a)}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(b)}{\Gamma(b)}\right)+O_{\infty}(\zeta)\right].

In this case, we therefore have that:

\frac{d\zeta}{d\tilde{s}}(\zeta)\mathcal{W}(F_{\underline{\sigma},0}(\tilde{s}(\zeta)),G_{\underline{\sigma},0}(\tilde{s}(\zeta))=\left[(1-\zeta)^{2}{F}_{\underline{\sigma},0}\frac{dG_{\underline{\sigma},0}}{d\zeta}-(1-\zeta)^{2}\frac{d{F}_{\underline{\sigma},0}}{d\zeta}G_{\underline{\sigma},0}\right]|_{\zeta=0}\\
=\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}(1-\zeta)^{2}(\log\zeta G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu})G^{\prime}_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}-\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}(1-\zeta)^{2}(\log\zeta G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu})^{\prime}G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}+(1-\zeta)^{2}O_{\infty}(\zeta)\Bigg|_{\zeta=0}\\
=-\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}\lim_{\zeta\downarrow 0}\zeta^{-1}(1-\zeta)^{2}G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}^{2}(\zeta)=-\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}.

Hence,
\mathcal{W}(F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu},G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu})=-\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}
.

By
[
NIS
]
[§15.10(ii)], we have that for
c\neq a+b
:

\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c;\zeta)=\frac{\Gamma(c)\Gamma(c-a-b)}{\Gamma(c-a)\Gamma(c-b)}\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b+1-c;1-\zeta)\\
+\frac{\Gamma(c)\Gamma(a+b-c)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}(1-\zeta)^{c-a-b}\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}(c-a,c-b,c-a-b+1;1-\zeta).

(C.29)

We can view
(a,b,c)
as functions of
(\mu,\underline{\sigma})
. Under the transformation
(\mu,\underline{\sigma})\mapsto(\mu,-\underline{\sigma})
, we have that
(a,b,c)\mapsto(c-a,c-b,c)
, so we can express:

\displaystyle F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}):=

\displaystyle\>\zeta^{\frac{c}{2}}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{\frac{1}{2}(a+b-c-1)}{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b+1-c;1-\zeta(\tilde{s})),

\displaystyle F_{-\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s}):=

\displaystyle\>\zeta^{\frac{c}{2}}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{-\frac{1}{2}(a+b-c+1)}{}_{2}F_{1}(c-a,c-b,c-a-b+1;1-\zeta(\tilde{s})).

We conclude that (
C.24
) holds.

By
[
NIS
]
[§15.8(ii)], we moreover have that in the case
c=a+b
, or
w=0
:

\mbox{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,a+b;\zeta)=-\frac{\Gamma(a+b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}\left(\log(1-\zeta)+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a)}{\Gamma(a)}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(b)}{\Gamma(b)}+O_{\infty}((1-\zeta))\right),

from which (
C.25
) follows.
∎

Proposition C.7
.

Assume that

\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d\eta<\infty

and let
U
be a solution to:

\frac{d^{2}U}{d\tilde{s}^{2}}=(1+\tilde{s})^{-2}\left[-\frac{1}{4}(1+w^{2})+v\tilde{s}^{-1}+\left(\mu^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\right)\tilde{s}^{-2}\right]U+\vartheta U.

(C.30)

Let
\zeta(\tilde{s})=\frac{\tilde{s}}{1+\tilde{s}}
and
f(\tilde{s}):=(1+|\log(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{-1}|)\zeta^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{\frac{1}{2}}=\tilde{s}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+\log(\tilde{s}+1))
. Then there exists constants
A_{1},A_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
and a positive constant
C>0
, such that we can write:

U=A_{1}(F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}+\varepsilon_{1})+A_{2}(G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}+\varepsilon_{2}),

with

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{1}\right|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C|F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}|(\tilde{s})(1+\log(\tilde{s}+1))\left(e^{C\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d\eta}-1\right)\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{1}\right|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C|F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}|(\tilde{s})(1+\log(\tilde{s}+1))\left(e^{C\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d\eta}-1\right)\quad(\mu=0),

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{2}\right|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C|F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}|(\tilde{s})(1+\log(\tilde{s}+1))\left(e^{C\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d\eta}-1\right)\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{2}\right|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\frac{|F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}|(\tilde{s})(1+\log(\tilde{s}+1))}{\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))}\left(e^{C\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d\eta}-1\right)\quad(\mu=0),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d(f\varepsilon_{1})}{d\tilde{s}}\right|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\tilde{s}^{-1}f(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))|F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}|(\tilde{s})\left(e^{C\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d\eta}-1\right)\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d(f\varepsilon_{1})}{d\tilde{s}}\right|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\tilde{s}^{-1}f(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))|F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}|(\tilde{\tilde{s}})\left(e^{C\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d\eta}-1\right)\quad(\mu=0),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d(f\varepsilon_{2})}{d\tilde{s}}\right|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\tilde{s}^{-1}f(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))|F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}|(\tilde{s})\left(e^{C\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d\eta}-1\right)\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d(f\varepsilon_{2})}{d\tilde{s}}\right|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\tilde{s}^{-1}f(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))\frac{|F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}|(\tilde{s})}{\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))}\left(e^{C\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\int_{\tilde{s}}^{\infty}\eta\log^{2}(2+\eta)|\vartheta(\eta)|\,d\eta}-1\right)\quad(\mu=0).

Proof.

We need to check that the assumptions in Proposition
C.2
are satisfied. Let
U_{1}(\tilde{s}):=F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s})
and
U_{2}(\tilde{s}):=F_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}(\tilde{s})
.

By definition of hypergeometric functions, we have that for any triple of complex constants
(\tilde{a},\tilde{b},\tilde{c})
,
{}_{2}F_{1}(\tilde{a},\tilde{b},\tilde{c};\zeta)=1+O_{\infty}(\zeta)
.

Hence,

\displaystyle|U_{1}|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+O_{1}(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))),

\displaystyle|U_{2}|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+|\log(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))|((1+O_{1}(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))),

\displaystyle|U_{1}|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}-{\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}}(\tilde{s})(1+O_{1}(\zeta(\tilde{s}))+O_{1}(\zeta^{2{\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}}(\tilde{s})))\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle|U_{1}|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tilde{s})\log\zeta(\tilde{s})(1+O_{1}(\zeta(\tilde{s})))\quad(\mu=0),

\displaystyle|U_{2}|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}+{\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}}(\tilde{s})(1+O_{1}(\zeta(\tilde{s}))).

We define the functions
P_{i},Q:\mathbb{R}\to(0,\infty)
, with
i\in\{0,1,2\}
, as follows:

\displaystyle Q(\tilde{s}):=

\displaystyle\>\zeta(\tilde{s})^{\frac{1}{2}{\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}},

\displaystyle P_{0}(\tilde{s}):=

\displaystyle\>\zeta(\tilde{s})^{-\frac{1}{2}{\textnormal{Re}\,\mu}}\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle P_{0}(\tilde{s}):=

\displaystyle\>\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\quad(\mu=0),

\displaystyle P_{1}(\tilde{s}):=

\displaystyle\>\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s})\frac{d\zeta}{d\tilde{s}}(\tilde{s})P_{0}(\tilde{s})=2\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{2}P_{0}(\tilde{s})=\tilde{s}^{-1}(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))P_{0}(\tilde{s}),

\displaystyle P_{2}(\tilde{s}):=

\displaystyle\>\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{-1}\frac{d\zeta}{d\tilde{s}}(\tilde{s})P_{0}(\tilde{s})=\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s})(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))P_{0}(\tilde{s})=\tilde{s}^{-1}P_{0}(\tilde{s}).

Then

\displaystyle|(fU_{1})(s)(fU_{2})(\eta)|+|(fU_{1})(\eta)(fU_{2})(s)|\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{0}(s)Q(\eta)\quad\eta\in(s,\infty),

\displaystyle\left|(fU_{1})^{\prime}(s)(fU_{2})(\eta)\right|+\left|(fU_{1})(\eta)(fU_{2})^{\prime}(s)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{1}(s)Q(\eta)\quad\eta\in(s,\infty),

\displaystyle\left|(f^{\prime}U_{1})(s)(f^{\prime}U_{2})(\eta)\right|+\left|(fU_{1})(\eta)(f^{\prime}U_{2})(s)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>CP_{2}(s)Q(\eta)\quad\eta\in(s,\infty),

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(\eta_{0},\infty)}[|fU_{1}|(\eta)+|fU_{2}|(\eta)]Q(\eta)<

\displaystyle\>\infty\quad\textnormal{for any $\eta_{0}>0$},

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(\tilde{s},\infty)}P_{0}(\eta)Q(\eta)<

\displaystyle\>\infty\quad\textnormal{for any $\tilde{s}>0$},

Note moreover that there exists a constant
C>0
, such that:

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(\tilde{s},\infty)}P_{0}(\eta)Q(\eta)=

\displaystyle\>1\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(\tilde{s},\infty)}P_{0}(\eta)Q(\eta)=

\displaystyle\>\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\quad(\mu=0),

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(\tilde{s},\infty)}|fU_{2}(\eta)|Q(\eta)\leq

\displaystyle\>C,

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(\tilde{s},\infty)}|fU_{1}(\eta)|Q(\eta)\leq

\displaystyle\>C\quad(\mu\neq 0),

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(\tilde{s},\infty)}|fU_{1}(\eta)|Q(\eta)\leq

\displaystyle\>C\log(1+\zeta^{-1}(\tilde{s}))\quad(\mu=0).

We can now apply Proposition
C.2
to conclude the estimates for
\varepsilon_{i}
.
∎

Corollary C.8
.

Let
\delta>0
and
0\leq\tilde{s}\leq e^{\delta|w|^{-1}}
. Then there exists a uniform constant
C>0
(independent of
w
), such that:

\displaystyle|G_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))\leq C\delta|w|^{-1}(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\left|\frac{dG_{\underline{\sigma},\mu}}{d\tilde{s}}\right|(\tilde{s})\leq

\displaystyle\>C\tilde{s}^{-1}(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))\leq C\delta\tilde{s}^{-1}|w|^{-1}(1-\zeta(\tilde{s}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We apply Proposition
C.7
with
w=0
to (
C.16
) by taking
\vartheta(\tilde{s})=-\frac{w^{2}}{4}(1+\tilde{s})^{-2}
.

∎

Lemma C.9
.

Let
c>0
and consider
f_{c}:\mathbb{R}\to(0,\infty)
, with
f_{c}(x):=\frac{\cosh(x+c)}{\cosh(x-c)}=\frac{\cosh(-x-c)}{\cosh(c-x)}
. Then

\displaystyle f_{c}^{\prime}>

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle f_{c}(0)=

\displaystyle\>1,

\displaystyle\lim_{x\to\infty}f_{c}(x)=

\displaystyle\>e^{2c},

\displaystyle\lim_{x\to-\infty}f_{c}(x)=

\displaystyle\>e^{-2c}.

Proof.

The limiting properties of
f_{c}
follow immediately. To conclude that
f_{c}^{\prime}>0
, we consider
\log f_{c}(x)
and compute:

(\log f_{c})^{\prime}(x)=(\log(\cosh((\cdot)+a))-\log(\cosh((\cdot)-a))^{\prime}(x)=\tanh(x+a)-\tanh(x-a).

We conclude that the right-hand side above is strictly positive by using that
(\tanh)^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\cosh^{2}}>0
.
∎
